  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC30` gives one of two projects a record that throws in parsing,
    then one that throws in drawing, and checks the other project's box is unchanged each time.
- AC31 [trace: V1 → AC31] — When a record line or field doesn't have the shape J9 or J10 gives it,
  then the page names it in that project's `notes` (file, line and field) and skips it, and uses the
  rest of the record.
  - **Examples:** `stage = "three"`, `card = "6"`, a dispatch-log line whose `time` isn't a string,
    an `outcomes.jsonl` line whose `session` is a number.
  - A skipped field shows as "not recorded", never as a wrong value or as zero.
  - A skipped field never un-flags anything: a `waiting-owner` status whose `card` is skipped is
    still flagged through its card file (J2), or through Terms' safety net.
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC31` covers each J9 field type with a wrong-typed value.
- AC32 [trace: V1 → AC32] — When the function answers 403 for a project (the Access session is
  gone), then the screen's state line says "Signed out — reload to sign in", even if building any
  box or the rest of the screen throws an error. The signed-out line is set before and apart from
  the boxes, and a render-error message never replaces it.
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC32` makes a box throw while signed out and checks the line.
- AC33 [trace: V4 → AC33] — When `ACCESS_TEAM_DOMAIN` is entered with capital letters or
  surrounding spaces, then the function trims and lowercases it before comparing the JWT's `iss`
  with `https://<team domain>` (J8). A valid owner session is accepted, and a token another Access
  team issued is still refused (AC13).
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC33` sets `ACCESS_TEAM_DOMAIN` to a mixed-case value with a
    trailing space.
- AC34 [trace: V1 → AC34] — When a project's reads fail with reason `page` several times in a row
  and the error changes, then its box shows the latest error's words. "Since HH:MM" keeps the time
  of the first failure in that run. The same holds for any reason whose words carry detail.
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC34` raises two different page errors in two polls.
- AC46 [trace: V1 → AC46] — When the Orchestrator's hold card `queue/ci-hold-<sha12>.md` is on a v3
  project's default branch and still open (J2), then within 5 minutes the desk flags it in that
  project's box, like any waiting card, and no longer shows it as "can't parse" activity.
  - **What the desk shows:** its title, "the default branch's governance run is not green", its
    `tip`, `state` and `held for` values from the card's third line, and its "What to do" section
    rendered from its markdown, with a link to the repository's Actions page for governance.
  - **No answer link:** the Orchestrator reads no answer from it, so AC16's link is not offered.
    The desk says "No answer needed: fix or re-run the governance run; this clears once it passes."
  - **When it stops being flagged:** within 5 minutes of the latest governance run on the
    default-branch head concluding `success` (J1's 3a and 3b), or of a newer hold card replacing
    it, or of its file being deleted.
  - It counts in V5's and AC20's figures as nothing: it has no `card` log entry and no answer. It is
    not listed in "Time asked of you".
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC46` uses a hold-card fixture in the real form (J2) and covers:
    flagged while governance is `failed` or `running`; cleared when it passes; only the newest of
    two hold cards flagged; never offered an answer link.
- AC47 [trace: V1 → AC47] — When an Orchestrator merge card (`queue/<item>-merge-conflict.md` or
  `queue/<item>-merge-by-hand.md`) is on a v3 project's default branch and still open (J2), then
  within 5 minutes the desk flags it in that project's box, like any waiting card, and no longer
  shows it as "can't parse" activity.
  - **What the desk shows:** the item, the step ("merge conflict" or "merge by hand"), the item
    tip, and the card's "The decision", "Why" and "What to do" sections rendered from its markdown,
    with a link to the item branch `item/<item>` on GitHub.
  - **No answer link:** the Orchestrator reads no answer from it, so AC16's link is not offered.
    The desk says "No answer needed: merge item/<item> by hand, or change the item and let the
    merge gate try again."
  - **When it stops being flagged** (J2): within 5 minutes of the item's tip being merged into the
    default branch (by the gate, recorded in `status/merges.jsonl`, or by hand), of the item being
    `closed`, or of the card file being deleted. A new card for a newer tip replaces the old one.
  - It counts in V5's and AC20's figures as nothing, as for hold cards.
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC47` uses merge-card fixtures in the real form (J2) for both
    steps and covers: flagged while open; cleared by a `merges.jsonl` line for its tip; cleared by
    a compare answer of `ahead` or `identical` (a by-hand merge); cleared when the item is
    `closed`; still flagged when the compare answer is `diverged` or the compare read fails; never
    offered an answer link.

### MD — Design: research, design lab, sandbox, then the chosen front end on the real back end
*Added by the owner's ruling of 2026-10-07 (decisions/questions/P-001-design-step.md), after the
owner found M1 "very drab... almost exclusively just text". Its criteria come from the owner's
eight answers (decisions/questions/P-001-design-discovery.md). They state outcomes and process,
not visual answers: those come from research Q-010 and the design lab. MD starts once M2 is accepted, because M2 brings the data a richer page shows
(answering, time asked, usage and context).*

**Inputs, read before any mockup:**
- research Q-010 (intake/requests/Q-010.toml): practical design for a phone-first status dashboard;
- research Q-011 (intake/requests/Q-011.toml): ways to send the owner a phone notification while
  the desk is closed;
- the discovery map (decisions/questions/P-001-design-discovery-notes.md): its questions A1–J3, the
  gaps it found, and its eleven design traps;
- the owner's eight answers (decisions/questions/P-001-design-discovery.md).

**What the desk must achieve** (each from an owner answer, quoted)
- AC35 [trace: V1 → AC35] — When the owner opens the desk on the phone, then the first screen is a
  calm overview grouped by project, one box each, from which a glance of a few seconds tells the owner
  which projects need them and where to tap for more.
  - Answers 3, 4 and 7: "I want to glance for a few seconds, but pick up enough information from
    that glance to know where to press into more details"; "bucketed by project"; "a clean, modern
    look, that facilitates a drill down workflow".
  - Waits stay grouped under their project, never pooled into one combined list.
  - Everything AC1–AC7 promise still holds on that screen: what is flagged, can't-read, "Checking…"
    and "Quiet".
  - **AC8 under MD:** each project's state (its flagged count, "Can't read", or quiet) and AC8's
    box order stay on the first screen. The rest of AC8's box contents (headline, sprint or open
    items, CI) may move one tap down (AC36). The design lab decides which, and the sandbox pull
    request says so (AC43).
  - **Changed by AC54 (trial-view notes 1, 2026-10-14):** the first screen's boxes sit in three
    collapsible groups, in this order: Active; Done; Closed or not applicable. AC8's box order
    (flagged, then can't-read, then default branch failing, then the rest by name) holds within
    each group. A box in a closed group is one tap down, but its group's header still says when
    something in it waits on the owner or can't be read, so the glance still tells the owner which
    projects need them.
  - Judged by the glance test (AC44, EXP-005).
- AC36 [trace: V4 → AC36] — When the owner taps a project's box, then the dashboard-level detail is
  there, one tap down: its pipeline or reduced view (AC9, AC10), its waits (AC11, AC12), CI, time
  asked (AC20), agents and usage (AC22, AC23). None of that detail is on the first screen beyond
  what AC35 needs.
  - Answer 7: "I want to naturally live at that car dashboard level - but not get hit in the face
    with it right off the bat."
  - Every view M1 and M2 built stays reachable; nothing is dropped by the redesign.
  - **Changed by AC55 (trial-view note 7, 2026-10-15):** inside the sandbox this detail is reached
    through the views AC55 rebuilds in the new design, never through a link to the accepted M2
    view. Without `?sandbox` the accepted views are unchanged.
  - **Changed by AC68 (trial-view notes 2, note 31, 2026-10-16):** inside the sandbox, a v3
    project's Pipeline view folds into the Timeline (its items as sprints, AC60), the Queue (its
    waits, AC11 and AC12) and the Team screen (who works on what, AC67), as the owner chose ("The
    old Pipeline page under "More detail" folds into the Timeline, since they now show the same
    thing."). "Every view M1 and M2 built stays reachable" holds for the sandbox through those
    screens; any fact of AC9's that none of them shows is listed in the sandbox pull request.
- AC37 [trace: V4 → AC37] — When the owner looks at a project, then it has a "where it's heading"
  line: the current phase, the upcoming work, and what that unlocks, built only from the project's
  records.
  - Answer 2, the owner's example: "Design phase right now - we need to establish KPIs, create
    visualizations, and trial their update cadence - once this is done you'll be able to view
    progress across the project".
  - **Never guessed** (AC9's rule): each part quotes or plainly restates a record, and names it. A
    part no record gives shows "not recorded"; the desk never writes a forecast of its own.
  - Which records feed each part (for example a v3 item's stage and next role, J9; a v2.5 ledger
    entry's title and `licenses_next:`, J9; a spec's milestone list) is settled in the design lab
    against real records, and written down in the sandbox pull request (AC43). If a part needs a
    record no project keeps today, that is a model change for the owner to rule on, not desk work.
  - **Changed by rulings 3 and 4 (2026-10-14, now model v3.3.0, L-0008):** for a v3 project the
    "current phase" is the phase run AC50 marks as now, and "the upcoming work" is that run's
    milestones with no work filed yet, by their plain-English names (docs/PROJECT.md section 3).
    From 2026-10-17 a milestone marked reached (J11) is never upcoming work (AC50).
    A v2.5 project keeps J9's ledger sources.
  - **Changed by AC54 (trial-view notes 1, 2026-10-14):** on Home this line is no longer printed
    on the card. It opens, word for word, in a bubble from a small control on the card. It stays
    on the Status screen, under its title (AC48).
- AC38 [trace: V1 → AC38] — When the owner sees what waits on them, then each decision kind (Terms)
  is shown differently, and an irreversible owner question, or one whose file names a default
  (AC12's label), is told apart from a
  routine pull request at a glance. None is ranked or styled as more or less urgent than another.
  - Answer 6: "They need to be viewed differently, not more or less urgent."
  - The difference is never colour alone (the standards lookup in Q-010).
  - AC8's box order is unchanged: kinds don't reorder boxes.
  - **Changed by ruling 6 (2026-10-14):** inside a project the waits are grouped by the kind of
    action they need (AC52), and the groups are told apart by their titles and edges, not colour
    alone. Order within a group follows the time each item was raised, never urgency, so this
    criterion's "none is ranked" still holds.
- AC39 [trace: V4 → AC39] — When the owner opens a project's activity view, then it shows which
  agents are working now, on which item, why (the stage and route that dispatched them), and what
  changed (their latest outcomes and records), from the dispatch log and status files (J9, J10).
  - Answer 8: "I want to know all the agents that are working, what they're working on, why
    they're working on it, what has changed".
  - A v2.5 project shows its reduced view's facts, and "not recorded in this repository's records
    (operating model v2.5.1)" for agents, as AC10 does.
  - **Extended by ruling 1 (2026-10-14):** this is the Team screen, with who has the ball and each
    member's page (AC49). The Agents view (AC22, AC23) stays reachable from it (AC36).
- AC40 [trace: V1 → AC40] — When the owner opens the desk after time away, then items, events and
  agent results that arrived since the owner last looked are marked "new since you last looked", on the
  overview and inside each project.
  - **"Last looked"** is the last time the desk had been visible on this device for at least 10
    seconds in a row (an open window, Terms, of 10 s or more), taken at the end of that window. A
    shorter flash, such as switching through tabs, doesn't count. 10 s is my choice (C), a
    constant to tune in the lab. It is kept on the device only, in local storage under its own key
    `desk-seen-v1`, wrapped as J5's storage is.
  - Something is new when its time (a card's raised time, a log entry's `time`, a PR's
    `created_at`) is later than "last looked". Marks clear when the current window reaches 10 s and
    ends.
  - **Known gap:** a draft PR marked ready after the last look keeps its older `created_at`, so it
    is flagged but not marked new: J2's list doesn't give the ready time (C). With no
    record (first visit, private window, cleared storage), nothing is marked new, and the desk says
    "new-since markers off on this device". Nothing is stored on Cloudflare.
  - The marker never moves a box or reorders a list under the owner's thumb (discovery map E5 and
    trap 7).
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC40` drives a fake clock and visibility changes: a window of
    5 s doesn't move "last looked", one of 10 s does, items before and after it are marked
    correctly, and failing storage shows "new-since markers off on this device".
- AC41 [trace: V1 → AC41] — When a value is unknown, stale or estimated (Terms), then it is never
  shown as a real value or as zero, in a number, a chart, a gauge or a summary line.
  - Unknown shows its words ("not recorded", "not available", "not given").
  - Stale data visibly degrades, and each project shows when it was last read.
  - An estimate is labelled as one.
  - No progress bar or track implies a percentage done (discovery map I3 and trap 4).

**Hard constraints** (the sandbox build and the final front end both meet them)
- AC42 [trace: V4 → AC42] — When the Reviewer checks MD's pull requests, then the design:
  - **uses no packages:** charts, icons and layout are hand-built CSS and SVG in `src/public/`,
    under the Toolchain's "no runtime dependencies" rule (discovery map H1);
  - **keeps the security headers:** `src/public/_headers` and the page's Content-Security-Policy
    are unchanged or stricter. So no web font, script or image from another origin (H2);
  - **stays within AC14's request budget:** the sandbox and the new views reuse the page's one poll
    scheduler and its data, and add no function call. AC14's test still passes. *Clarified
    2026-10-14:* J11's reads add no new kind of call, but they do add blobs to J6's existing blob
    calls, so a cold load can make about one more `/api/blobs` call. AC14's arithmetic (A3)
    counts it. *Clarified 2026-10-16:* J12 adds one request form to GitHub (a fixed GraphQL query)
    but no function call: it travels in the blob call, and A3 counts it;
  - **works at 360 px:** nothing scrolls sideways on a phone screen;
  - **survives the extremes of discovery map H5:** 5 connected projects, 12 waits, long titles, a
    360 px screen. Fixtures for each are in `tests/desk/fixtures/`, and a test marked
    `ac-test: S-001/AC42` renders each without error or overflow;
  - **respects reduced motion**, and never uses colour alone to carry meaning (Q-010's standards
    lookup).

**How MD runs**
- AC43 [trace: V4 → AC43] — When MD runs, then it goes through two stages, in order, and no
  self-design.
  - **A, the design lab** (run by the Chief of Staff; outside the Builder's surface; no pull
    request, no deploy).
    - **Where:** the Chief of Staff publishes mockups as Claude artifacts, private to the owner's
      claude.ai account by default (C). Only the owner sees them, and anyone the owner chooses to share one
      with. Their cost is nil on the owner's existing plan (E).
    - **What they hold:** a snapshot of real records from the connected projects, including text
      from the private Personal-Org-Operating-Model repository (titles, ledger lines, question
      text). The snapshot holds no token or secret. A mockup is never shared beyond the owner
      without their say.
    - **Buildable only:** each mockup keeps to AC42's constraints (no packages, the security
      headers, the request budget, 360 px, H5's extremes), so whatever the owner picks can be built.
      The Chief of Staff checks each mockup against AC42 before the owner picks.
    - The owner looks on the phone and asks for changes in plain language, screenshots or images.
      The Chief of Staff changes the mockup and republishes.
    - Each mockup cites the Q-010 findings it rests on, and shows the H5 extremes as well as today's
      data (discovery map trap 9, "designing on ideal data").
    - **The lab ends** when the owner picks a direction: one or at most three candidates to build.
      The Chief of Staff records that pick as a ruling in `decisions/questions/`, with links to the
      chosen mockups.
  - **B, the sandbox view** (the Builder, through the normal route: Reviewer PASS, owner merge,
    owner-approved preview deploy).
    - The desk at `?sandbox` renders the chosen candidates against the desk's live data on the
      preview.
    - Every sandbox screen carries a plain marker, "Sandbox: candidate design", so it can't be
      mistaken for the real view (discovery map H4). Without `?sandbox`, the desk is exactly the
      accepted M2 view.
    - The sandbox answers cards and questions through the same J3 links as the real view, and
      nothing else.
    - The pull request names, for each AC35–AC41 criterion, where the candidate meets it, and AC37's
      record sources. **From 2026-10-14** it does the same for AC48–AC56, and lists every place a
      screen shows "not recorded" with the model change that would fill it. **From 2026-10-16**
      it does the same for AC57–AC72 and J12.
  - **No self-design** (P-001-design-step.md, question 4). The desk can't edit its own layout,
    write code or deploy. It holds no write credential, as before.
- AC44 [trace: V4 → AC44] — When MD is to be accepted, then, in order:
  1. the owner has picked a direction in the lab (AC43);
  2. the Reviewer has passed the sandbox build, and it is deployed to the preview;
  3. **the owner has picked one candidate** on the preview's sandbox, against live data. The Chief
     of Staff records that pick as a ruling in `decisions/questions/` (CS10), naming the candidate.
     With only one candidate built, the owner confirms it or sends it back to the lab;
  4. **the chosen front end is merged with the real back end:** in a pull request the Reviewer
     passes, the candidate step 3 recorded becomes the desk's default view, the sandbox and the
     unchosen candidates are removed, and the owner approves its preview deploy. The owner then
     sees exactly how the desk will run at launch;
  5. the owner has run the 5-second glance test on the preview, and it passed
     (experiments/EXP-005-md-glance-test.md);
  6. CS9's gate check;
  7. the owner accepts MD.

  M3's new views (AC24, AC25) are then built in the chosen design.
- AC45 [trace: V1 → AC45] — When MD is accepted, then the channel for phone notifications while the
  desk is closed has been decided from Q-011's result, and recorded as an owner ruling.
  - Answer 1: "Phone notifications". Polling only while the desk is open can't reach the owner
    when it is closed (discovery map gap 1).
  - **The baseline that already exists** (not desk work): Service-Desk's Orchestrator opens one
    issue labelled `needs-you`, titled "Needs you: <item> <gate>-<n>", for each waiting v3 card,
    assigns it to the owner, and closes it once the card no longer waits (model S-017 AC3;
    governance/checks/orchestrator_git.py:3057–3060 and :3317; orchestrator.yml:474–480). The
    GitHub mobile app notifies the owner of the assignment (C; Q-011 answers it). Real cases: issues #33 ("Needs you:
    P-001 failure-5") and #34 ("Needs you: P-001 stop-6"), both assigned to `shumpmaster`.
  - **Its gaps:** it covers only cards in Service-Desk. These reach the owner only when they open
    the desk:
    - owner questions (`questions/<name>.md`), in either project;
    - pull requests waiting for the owner's review or merge;
    - anything in a v2.5 project (Personal-Org-Operating-Model), which has no Orchestrator.
  - **Only extra channels need an exception or a model change.** For example:
    - a Web Push sender for the desk would need a server-side sender running while the page is
      closed (a Cloudflare Cron Trigger or a GitHub Actions workflow) and somewhere to keep the
      push subscription (for example Cloudflare KV). Kept on Cloudflare, that would be an
      exception to the desk's design: it keeps no server-side state ("Nothing of this is stored on
      Cloudflare", AC25) and holds no write credential (mechanism). *Ruled since (below,
      2026-10-08):* the sender is the Orchestrator's GitHub run and the subscription a GitHub
      secret, with no database and no new Cloudflare service, so the desk keeps no server-side
      state; the Reviewer's security review still applies to it in M3 (plan review round 3, N3);
    - wider issue alerts (for questions, pull requests, or v2.5 projects) would be a change to the
      Orchestrator, or to Personal-Org-Operating-Model's model, not to the desk.

    Either needs the owner's ruling.
  - **Ruled, 2026-10-08** (decisions/questions/P-001-notifications.md, line 1; draft 4 of the
    trial-view notes 2 amendment puts it back, since no criterion carried it): "phone
    notifications are sent as Web Push to the desk installed on the owner's iPhone Home Screen, by
    the Orchestrator's GitHub run when it raises a card; the subscription is kept as a GitHub
    secret the owner sets; no database and no new Cloudflare service; built in M3". The owner's
    answers: "iPhone", and "Desk push via GitHub (Recommended)". So the channel is decided, and
    this criterion's first sentence is met by that record: MD needs nothing more for it.
  - **Brought forward and specified, 2026-10-18** ("Notifications the moment anything waits on
    you"): the owner asked for it "as an immediate priority" and widened it to "whenever anything
    at all is waiting on me". It is now the first piece of work, ahead of M3A and of MD3's
    remaining work, built in the desk as accepted, and specified in AC73 to AC78, J16 to J21,
    Workflows 3 and 4 and EXP-006 ("N — Notifications", below; draft 4). The bullet below is kept as it was
    written; where it differs, AC73 to AC77 govern.
  - **Built in M3, not specified yet.** The ruling's follow-ups list what M3's criteria must
    carry: the manifest and service worker (or Declarative Web Push on iOS 18.4 and later), the
    permission button, the send from the Orchestrator's card step (a model change to the
    Orchestrator's workflow, raised there), the VAPID keys and the subscription held as secrets
    the owner sets by hand, and an experiment registration for how Cloudflare Access treats the
    service worker, manifest and click-through (Q-011's Q-f). None of that is written in this
    spec yet: the Definer adds it to M3 (its criteria, a join sheet and the experiment's
    registration) in a revision before M3 starts, and nothing of it is built before then (the
    pointer in M3, below).

**The screens, as the design lab has settled them** (added 2026-10-14)
*From the owner's rulings in the lab (decisions/questions/P-001-design-lab-1.md, 2026-10-09), the
phases and milestones of model v3.3.0 (L-0008; docs/PROJECT.md section 3), the owner's comments
on the screens of 10 to 13 Oct (docs/design/references/comments-2026-10.md, quoted below exactly as
written, typos included), and the saved screens (docs/design/README.md and docs/design/screens/).
They say what the sandbox's candidate shows and which records feed it. They don't record the
owner's pick: the lab ends only when the owner picks (AC43). Where a saved screen and this spec
differ, this spec governs and the Builder raises the difference.*

**Where the saved screens are not to be copied** (each breaks a rule below):
- Home's phase checklist draws a bar that fills by the share of phases finished
  (01-home.html:63 and :231). AC50 shows the count in words and no bar (AC41). The owner agreed
  (design-lab question 4, decisions/questions/P-001-design-lab-open.md: "Agree").
- The Team screen draws a "just finished" member's light partly filled (03-team.html:116), which
  reads as a gauge. AC49's light is either lit or not, never partly filled.
- The Timeline's "I merged it" and the Queue's "I committed it" buttons tick a step on a tap. In
  the desk only a record ticks a step (AC51, AC52).

The rules for all six:
- AC42's hard constraints hold for every view below, and each has H5's extremes among AC42's
  fixtures.
- AC41 holds everywhere: an unknown value shows its words, or "–" where a tile has room for no
  more, with the reason one tap away. The owner's words: "no made up numbers or scores - the
  visual was just appealing"; "Absolutely no fake numbers."
- Every screen reads only the records J9, J10 and J11 name, and, from 2026-10-16, the open pull
  requests' bodies in J1's open list and the pull requests J12 reads (AC60, AC61; plan review
  round 1, N2). A part that needs a record no project
  keeps shows "not recorded" and is listed as a model change ("What this spec does not cover").
- On screens the word is "Done", never "Graduated" (AC50).

- AC48 [trace: V1 → AC48] — When the owner opens the sandbox, then it shows the lab's screens one
  full view at a time, and moves between them as the lab settled:
  - **Home** (screen 1, the Universe screen): one card per connected project. Each card holds,
    top to bottom:
    - a quiet line icon ("Less colorful icons here please. I like having the icons but i dont want
      them to pop and feel as cartoonish.", 13 Oct): thin grey lines on a plain tile;
    - the project's name;
    - where it is, in plain words (AC37's line). *Changed by AC54 (2026-10-14):* in its place the
      card shows the project's brief summary, and AC37's line opens in a bubble from a small
      control on the card; the line stays on the Status screen. Home's cards sit in a two-column
      grid on the phone, in AC54's three groups;
    - the phase checklist (AC50). *Changed by AC58 (trial-view notes 2, 2026-10-16):* in its
      place, the compact branch drawing, read from the bottom up;
    - who has the ball (AC49);
    - one pill for that project's queue (AC52).
  - **The pill:**
    - when something waits on the owner, it gives the count and the kind of action in words ("1 to
      merge", "1 decision");
    - "All clear" only when nothing waits **and** the project was read successfully within the
      last 3 minutes (Quiet's rule, per project);
    - "Can't read" with AC5's words when it can't be read, and "Checking…" before its first read
      (AC1–AC7 still hold);
    - tapping it opens that project's queue. Nothing from one project appears on another
      project's card (ruling 6: "I want each of my projects and everything within them retained
      within their respective tile on that front page").
  - **Tapping a card** opens that project's Project screen.
  - **Home's top line:** how many things wait on the owner in all (a count, never a combined list,
    AC35) and when the desk last read. AC21's V5 line stays on Home.
  - **No "New project" card** ("Get rid of the new project function here since it doesnt
    apply.", 13 Oct).
  - **No bottom band on Home.** *Changed 2026-10-16 by the owner's ruling decisions/questions/P-001-design-lab-open.md,
    answer 1:* "I see no need for it if it’s not doing anything right now." The lab's "Tell your
    Chief of Staff…" bar is not built, nor the "Deferred: waiting on your ruling" words it carried.
    The bar was all the band held, so the band goes too; that is my reading (C). (Its look,
    neutral grey with a hairline above it and not coral, came from the owner's comment pinned to
    it, "Great page - only thing is change this color down here.", 11 Oct; the project screens'
    bottom bar keeps that look, below.)
  - **Inside a project** there are four screens: **Status** (the Project screen, screen 2),
    **Timeline** (screen 4, AC51), **Team** (screen 3, AC49) and **Queue** (screen 6, AC52), with
    the response sheet (screen 7) opening over the queue.
    - Every project screen has the same bottom bar, in that order: Status · Timeline · Team ·
      Queue, neutral grey with a hairline above it. The lab's "+" in the bar's middle is not
      built.
    - Every project screen has a way back to Home.
    - The Status screen's line under its title says what is happening in one sentence (AC37).
      Under it, two buttons open the queue and the team, so neither is more than one tap away.
  - **The shared look** (docs/design/README.md, "The shared look"): one palette, and each colour
    has one meaning on every screen:
    - coral: the owner;
    - blue: an agent;
    - green and red: pass and fail;
    - purple: a change to merge;
    - amber: a blocker;
    - gold: phase progress.

    Milestones are neutral. Colour is never the only carrier of meaning (AC42): a word, an icon or
    a shape goes with it. Codes such as `Q-012` and `PR #61` come after the plain words, in small
    monospace.
    - **Extended by trial-view notes 2, notes 13 and 14 (2026-10-16):** two families join the
      palette, each used only where these criteria name it, and always with its words:
      - **one colour per phase** (Discovery, Design, Build, Launch; Done takes the neutral idle
        grey): on AC57's phase box stripes and AC59's bands, line and chips, and nowhere else;
      - **one colour per Timeline bullet** (Technical slate, Context dusty rose, Why muted violet):
        on AC60's bullet labels and left edges, and nowhere else.

      **Their values, re-picked so that no colour on the desk has two meanings** (the owner's
      answer of 2026-10-16, "Pick distinct shades (Recommended)"; the mockup's own phase and bullet
      colours sat on the agent blue, the merge purple, the blocker amber and the owner's coral, and
      are not used). Light theme, then dark:
      - Discovery, teal: `#0f766e`, `#65bdb3`;
      - Design, magenta: `#9d2f7e`, `#f07cca`;
      - Build, olive: `#5c6a10`, `#a4b562`;
      - Launch, clay: `#7a5a48`, `#c6a28f`;
      - Technical, slate: the existing muted grey, `#4b525b`, `#a3a9b2` (`--muted`);
      - Context, dusty rose: `#ad606a`, `#bc7e8d`;
      - Why, muted violet: `#756592`, `#c7adcb`.

      The owner approved the phase colours with the swatch page
      (docs/design/mockups/colour-swatches.html, "Yes on the swatches") and then Context's and
      Why's re-picked values with the audit-additions mockup
      (docs/design/mockups/audit-additions.html, "Approve as drawn (Recommended)"), after plan
      review round 2 (N1) found the first Context and Why too near the agent blue and Design's
      magenta; both answers are in the batch file's last sections, 2026-10-16. Each always shows
      with its name in words (AC42). Milestones stay neutral: a milestone's mark is a plain neutral
      circle with an icon (AC59), never the phase's colour or one of its own.
    - **One older value changes:** the dark theme's gold, `--gold`, moves from `#d4ae4f` to
      `#c9b04f`, so that it stands apart from the dark theme's blocker amber, `#e3a33b`; the light
      theme's gold is unchanged (the owner's answer, "Yes, fix it too (Recommended)", same record).
      Every place gold is used keeps its meaning (phase progress, the now marks, next to reach).
    - **Coral only for what waits on the owner** (note 32, AC69): coral, `--you`, marks only what
      the owner does; a gap the agents fill is slate (AC69).
    - **Every colour that carries a meaning is distinct** (plan review round 2, N1): the CIEDE2000
      colour difference (sRGB, D65) between any two of them is at least **10**, in the light theme
      and in the dark theme alike. The colours are the shared look's seven (coral, blue, green,
      red, purple, amber, gold, from `sandbox.css`'s `--you`, `--agent`, `--pass`, `--fail`,
      `--merge`, `--block` and `--gold`), the four phase colours, Context's and Why's, and, from
      draft 5, slate (`--muted`), which now carries a meaning too: Technical's bullet, and a gap
      the agents fill (AC69; plan review round 3, N5). That is 14 in each theme, 91 pairs. With the
      values above every pair passes; the closest are coral and red in the light theme (about
      11.4) and amber and the new gold in the dark theme (about 11.0); slate's closest are purple
      in the light theme (about 17.6) and Why's muted violet in the dark theme (about 16.1),
      measured on 2026-10-16 with the formula the test uses. A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC48`
      reads the 14 tokens from `sandbox.css` for both themes, computes every pair's difference
      with the formula written in the test (no package), and fails any pair under 10. From draft 6 it also checks AC57's run 3 and run 4 shades, in each theme, against the 14 and
      against each other (build review round 1, N4).
  - Every view M1 and M2 built stays reachable (AC36). The sandbox pull request says where each
    one now lives.
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC48` renders each screen from fixtures. It follows each card,
    pill and bar tap to the screen it names. It checks that no item of one project appears on
    another project's card, queue or team, and that "All clear" is not shown for a project whose
    last successful read is older than 3 minutes. From 2026-10-16 it also checks that Home shows
    no "Tell your Chief of Staff…" bar, no "Deferred: waiting on your ruling" words, and nothing
    fixed to the bottom of Home's screen, with no sheet or bubble open. The AC51 + AC48 stacking test
    (tests/desk/ac51-timeline-line.test.mjs) then checks the project screens' bottom bar
    (`.sb-dock`) only, with no `.sb-band`.

- AC49 [trace: V4 → AC49] — When the owner opens a project's Team screen, then it shows who has the
  ball and every member of the team, lit where one is working or has just finished, and a tap on a
  member shows the work they did; each value comes from a record the desk reads (J9, J10, J11).
  - **Changed by AC67 (trial-view notes 2, note 30, 2026-10-16):** each member's light is replaced
    by the Timeline's circle icons, and its glow goes with it; each card also names the last sprint
    the member worked on and the milestone it feeds. Where this criterion and AC67 differ on the
    Team screen's cards, AC67 governs.
  - Ruling 1, the owner's words: "In my head the agent dashboard is just a panel that lights up
    where an agent is working something, or recently has finished something and it becomes a way
    to quickly click into an agent and see the work they've performed."
  - **Across projects:** ruling 1's "one panel across projects" is met on Home, where each card's
    who-has-the-ball line lights while an agent works in that project. The panel itself sits inside
    each project, as the lab agreed on 13 Oct (screen 3), so it crosses no boundary (ruling 6).
  - **Who has the ball** (on each Home card and at the top of the Team screen) names each of these
    that holds, in this order (the order is the Definer's choice, C):
    1. **waiting on the owner:** the project's queue holds an item (AC52): "Waiting on you", with
       the oldest item's wait so far (AC20's rule) or "wait not recorded";
    2. **an agent working:** a status file in state `dispatched` (J9): "<role> working on <item>";
    3. **the Chief of Staff working** (from 2026-10-17, model S-033; J13): a piece of work of one
       of its sessions is under way (J13's rule, with its 8-hour limit): "Chief of Staff working".
       What it is doing is on its tile and its Working now sheet, not in this line, which stays
       one short line on the Home card (C, reading ruling 1 of
       decisions/questions/P-001-design-lab-1.md, that the panel "also answers "who has the
       ball"", ruling 5, that the Chief of Staff's sessions "appear on the agent panel", and
       design-lab answer 2, "Yes", decisions/questions/P-001-design-lab-open.md; the line names
       every case that holds, so its place in the order hides none);
    4. **checks running:** a workflow run in J1's lists for the head with `status` `queued` or
       `in_progress`: "Checks running";
    5. **queued for the next Orchestrator run:** a status file in state `ready` or `returned` (J9):
       "Next Orchestrator run", with when it is due by the Orchestrator's schedule (J11), or "due
       time not available";
    6. none of these: "Nothing under way".

    No score, rating or figure is shown beyond these recorded times. A v2.5 project shows "not
    recorded in this repository's records (operating model v2.5.1)", as AC10 does. *Until
    2026-10-17* the Chief of Staff's own work was never shown here, since its sessions weren't
    recorded (ruling 5, a model change); from then case 3 reads its session lines (model S-033,
    L-0016).
  - **The owner's tile, at the top, full width:** the owner's status and the next lines in this
    project's queue, the first three in AC52's order (3 is my choice, C, to tune in the lab).
    - The owner's words: "Lets get rid of that usage section and instead bring the "You" tile up and
      give it slightly more real estate to show the same data - but represent next upcoming items
      that will need me. So visualize a few lines in the queue." (13 Oct)
    - No usage card sits on the Team screen. Usage stays behind each session's drill-down (AC23).
  - **One tile per member:**
    - who: the owner, the Chief of Staff, the Orchestrator, and each role in the roster block of
      the project's `governance/SURFACES.md` (J11). The roster's `chief-of-staff` row is that same
      member, so it gets one tile, not two;
    - each tile shows its status word, its last piece of work and a light that is lit, and glows,
      only while it works. A finished member's light is never partly filled. The status words
      are:
      - "Working": a status file in state `dispatched` with that role;
      - "Just finished": the role's latest outcome entry (J9) is under 30 minutes old (30 is my
        choice, C, to tune in the lab);
      - "Waiting on you": the owner's own tile, when the queue holds an item;
      - "Idle" otherwise, with the time and words of its last recorded work, or "not recorded".
    - **The Chief of Staff's tile** (from 2026-10-17, J13; until then it read "Its sessions
      aren't recorded", ruling 5) takes its status word from its session lines on the default
      branch, every session file of the project together:
      - "Working": a piece of work is under way (J13). With two or more under way at once (two
        sessions), the tile names the one started last, and its log shows each;
      - "Just finished": none is under way and the latest end line's `time` is under 30 minutes
        old (AC49's 30 minutes);
      - "Idle" otherwise, with its last recorded work: the latest piece's `doing`, as written,
        and its end line's time, or "ended, time not recorded" (J13);
      - "No session recorded yet" while the tree holds no session file for the project, with its
        last work "not recorded". It never reads "Its sessions aren't recorded" again.
    - **A helper on its own tile** (from 2026-10-17, C, reading ruling 5, that the subagents the
      Chief of Staff starts "are logged like pipeline sessions and appear on the agent panel",
      decisions/questions/P-001-design-lab-1.md:20, and ruling 1's words above, that the panel
      is "a way to quickly click into an agent and see the work they've performed", :10): a
      helper line (J13) whose `role` is a
      roster id other than `chief-of-staff` (for example `definer`) is recorded work of that
      member. It gives that member's tile "Just finished" while its `ended` is under 30 minutes
      old, and its last recorded work when it is the member's latest by time (against the
      member's latest outcome entry, J9): "a helper session for the Chief of Staff", with its
      `ended` time. It never makes a member "Working": the line is written after the helper
      ends. A `role` the roster does not hold shows only on the Chief of Staff's page.
    - A role whose work ran outside the pipeline (P-001's Builder and Reviewer since the re-scope)
      shows only what the records the desk reads hold, and otherwise "not recorded in what the desk
      reads".
  - **A member's page** has three parts:
    1. **Status**, as on the tile.
    2. **Responsible for:** three to five groups, each a kind of record that member's work leaves
       (for the owner: decisions, rulings, verdicts, merges). Each group shows a count of records
       and the latest one; a tap lists them all, newest first. The groups for each role are fixed
       and written down in the sandbox pull request. A count is of records only, never an estimate.
       A group no record the desk reads can fill shows "not recorded". Today that includes the
       owner's merges and verdicts: J1 reads only open pull requests, `status/merges.jsonl` holds
       the Orchestrator's item merges, not the owner's, and verdict files land on pull-request
       branches. From 2026-10-17 the Chief of Staff's groups include "Pieces of work" and
       "Helpers", counted from its session lines (J13); the sandbox pull request lists them with
       the other fixed groups.
    3. **Running log**, drawn as a branch timeline:
       - newest at the top ("Can we have the branch chart build up from the bottom so the most
         recent events are at the top?", 13 Oct);
       - the main line is the project. Each piece of work (an item) branches off in its own lane
         and joins back when it is filed: its line in `status/merges.jsonl`, or its outcome's
         `record`;
       - lanes of the same kind take different shades of their colour ("the branches should be a
         different color or shade. Right now i cant easily tell what is and isnt the same
         responsibility.", 13 Oct);
       - while the member is working, its current task is ringed and marked "now". Above it,
         "Up next" lists only the next steps the records name: J9's "next" words for that item
         ("can we show upcoming tasks in some pleasing way? and emphasize the current task when
         the agent is working to indicate this is the stage in progress.", 13 Oct).
       - **The Chief of Staff's log** (from 2026-10-17, J13; until then "Its sessions aren't
         recorded, so there is no running log."): each piece of work is a lane. It leaves the
         main line at its start line's row and joins back at its end line's row; one under way
         ends in the dashed stub with its latest row ringed "now", and one that ended unrecorded
         (J13) ends in the dashed stub with "ended, time not recorded" on its last row. Its
         rows, each at its line's time:
         - the start line: "Started: <doing>", the `doing` as written;
         - each helper line, at its `ended` (C): "<role> helper · <started> to <ended> ·
           <tokens> tokens · <tool_uses> tool uses · <duration_ms as AC23 rounds it>", each
           figure "not recorded" when `null` (AC23's exception for session lines), times as J9
           gives times;
         - the end line: "Handed back · <its usage's duration_ms, as AC23 rounds it>", and its
           other usage figures one tap away on the row's summary sheet, each `null` one "not
           recorded".

         It shows no "Up next": no record names the Chief of Staff's next step. A member whose
         helper lines J13 gives (above) shows each as a row of its own log too, at its `ended`,
         on the main line, with the same words.
       - **Drawn correctly on every member's page** (trial-view note 11, 2026-10-15: "The running
         log is messed up on every pop up"; at 11:30 its lanes started and ended at angles, with
         dots off the line, docs/design/references/trial-view/2026-10-14-team-log-1130.png). It is
         drawn as the agreed screen 3 draws it (docs/design/screens/03-team.html, its running-log
         drawing):
         - one drawing behind all the log's rows, never one piece per row, so every line is
           continuous from row to row;
         - the main line and each lane are straight vertical lines, each at one fixed position
           across, from the lane's first row to its last;
         - a lane leaves the main line with one curve at its first (oldest) row, and joins back
           with one curve at the row where its work is filed. There are no other slanted pieces.
           A lane whose work is not filed yet ends in a short dashed stub; a lane already open at
           the oldest row shown runs in from the edge, straight;
         - lanes open at the same time sit at different positions;
         - each row's dot sits on its own lane's line (the main line for a row with no lane),
           centred on the row.
       - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC49` also renders the log of every member in the roster
         fixture, the Chief of Staff's included, and reads the drawing's shapes: every straight
         piece is vertical; each lane has one fork (none for a lane already open at the oldest
         row shown, which runs in straight from the edge) and at most one join, at the rows its records
         give; each dot's centre is on its lane's line and at its row's centre (within 1 px); and
         two lanes open at once never share a position.
  - **Behind every item and log row**, a summary sheet. Its headings (What happened, Why, For you)
    each sit on their own line, with the text full width below ("i am okay with the Section title
    and the update just one line below it so we can maximize the horizontal space here.", 13 Oct).
    - What happened: J9's headline words for the entry, and a quote from the record with a link to
      it (AC51's link rule).
    - Why: the stage and route that dispatched the work (AC39).
    - For you: the queue item it raised, or "Nothing for you".
    - This is the standard summary. A written plain-English summary needs a model change; until
      then none is shown, and the desk writes no prose of its own beyond these fields.
    - **For a session line's row** (from 2026-10-17, J13): What happened is the row's words and
      the line quoted as written, linked to its file by AC51's link rule; Why and For you read
      "not recorded in the session line" (C): the line names neither what woke the session nor a
      queue item.
  - **The Working now sheet** opens from a working member's "now" row ("yes mock it up - that is
    exactly what i want to see", 13 Oct). It reads in a couple of seconds ("in my head this page -
    specific to the working steps - reads in like 2 seconds. Doing then almost like the KPIS of that
    working step. "Started @, time elapsed, last check in, update, next check in... " That sort of
    thing", 13 Oct). It holds one line, four tiles and three short lines. Each value comes from a
    record, or shows "–" with the reason:
    - **Doing:** "<role> on <item>, <stage name>" (the status file and its dispatch, J9).
    - **Started:** the status file's `dispatched_at`.
    - **Time so far:** now less Started, labelled "counted from the start", as AC22's elapsed time.
    - **Last check-in:** the `time` of the latest dispatch-log entry naming the item (J9's "as of").
    - **Next check-in:** the Orchestrator's next run by its schedule (J11), or "–" with "schedule
      not readable".
    - **Update:** the latest dispatch-log entry for the item, in J9's headline words.
    - **Next:** "the Orchestrator routes its return" while it runs. This is a fixed statement of
      what the model does next (J9's "returned" rule), not a record, and the sheet labels it so.
    - **Cost:** "–" with "from the usage record when it ends" while it runs; after it ends, AC23's
      estimate.
    - **For the Chief of Staff** (from 2026-10-17, J13; until then Started, Time so far and Cost
      showed "–" with "not recorded: its sessions aren't logged yet", ruling 5), from the piece
      under way:
      - **Doing:** "Chief of Staff: <doing>", the start line's `doing` as written;
      - **Started:** the start line's `time`;
      - **Time so far:** now less Started, labelled "counted from the start";
      - **Last check-in:** the latest `time` or helper `ended` among the session's lines;
      - **Next check-in:** "–" with "the Chief of Staff runs on no schedule";
      - **Update:** the latest helper line, in the log row's words, or "–" with "no helper
        recorded yet";
      - **Next:** "hands the work back to you", labelled as a fixed statement of the model's rule
        (S-033 AC6: an end line when it hands the work back), not a record;
      - **Cost:** while it runs, "–" with "not recorded: the Chief of Staff can't read its own
        cost"; after it ends, its end line's `cost_usd_estimate` as AC23 shows a cost ("estimate
        $x.xx") when it is not `null`, and otherwise, as it always is today, "not recorded: the
        Chief of Staff can't read its own cost" (S-033, Known limits; AC23's exception for
        session lines; J13's figures).
    - It shows no live narration. The desk reads only what has landed in GitHub (the answer to
      the owner's 13 Oct question on agent feedback).
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC49` uses fixtures for each who-has-the-ball case (an open
    card, a `dispatched` status, a running workflow, a `ready` status with a readable and an
    unreadable schedule, and none). It checks each case's words, the Chief of Staff's "not
    recorded", every Working now value against its record, and "–" wherever a record is missing.
    From 2026-10-17 it uses J13's fixtures (session files `v3_checks sessions` passes) and
    checks: "Chief of Staff working" in who has the ball, in its place in the order; the Chief of
    Staff's tile "Working", "Just finished" at 29 and not at 31 minutes, "Idle" with its last
    work, "ended, time not recorded" for a piece followed by a second start and for one past 8
    hours (at 7 h 59 min still "Working"), and "No session recorded yet" with no file; its log's
    lanes, rows and words, each `null` figure "not recorded" and never "not available"; every
    Chief of Staff Working now value against its line; a `definer` helper line giving the
    Definer's tile "Just finished" and its last work, never "Working"; a helper whose
    `role` the roster lacks shown on the Chief of Staff's page only; an end line with no open
    piece (one before any start, one after an end) giving its own "Handed back (no recorded
    start)" row and "Just finished" at 29 minutes; an end line whose `usage` has a figure of the
    wrong kind still closing its piece, that figure alone "not recorded"; and the Working now
    sheet's Cost "not recorded" while running and, for a made-up end line with a cost, "estimate
    $x.xx" after it ends.

- AC50 [trace: V4 → AC50] — When the owner looks at a project's phases, on its Home card or its
  Status screen, then they are the model's phases (Terms) and each project's own milestones, shown
  only as the records give them (rulings 3 and 4, now model v3.3.0).
  - The owner's words: "Like discovery and design are phases. That is standard and set in stone
    ... the milestones don't need to be identical for every project like phases should be.
    Milestones should be established project by project, but I want them to be lead with plain
    English."
  - **Four phases, then Done.** The desk shows Discovery, Design, Build and Launch, in that
    order, then Done. Operate and Review belong to the product after the Launch gate and are not
    shown. The owner asked "I see 4 phases here, i thought we had 6?" and then said "No thats okay
    i wasnt thinking about it." (13 Oct).
  - **"Done", never "Graduated",** on every screen ("I really dont like the "Graduated". Done is
    fine", 13 Oct). A finished phase reads "Finished", so "Done" only ever means the whole project.
    The model's own word in its records stays "Graduated"; the desk only translates it.
    - A project is Done when the model's timeline shows its Graduated mark, or its graduation
      ruling record (`decisions/questions/<item>-graduation.md`, decisions/README.md) is on the
      default branch.
  - **Phase runs and milestone states come from the model's timeline** (J11), never from the
    desk's own reckoning. The one exception is which run is "now": the model's timeline marks no
    run as now, so the desk applies the rule below.
    - The **now** run is the earliest run, in the timeline's order, holding a project milestone
      that is not dropped and has no work filed. If every one has work filed, it is the run holding
      the latest recorded step. If neither exists, the phase shows "not recorded". This rule is
      mine (C), not the model's, and the owner agreed (design-lab question 3,
      decisions/questions/P-001-design-lab-open.md: "Agree").
      - *From 2026-10-17 (J11's reached part):* a milestone marked reached counts here as one
        with work filed, whether or not its line lists sprints, so a reached milestone never
        makes a run "now" and is never the next milestone to reach (AC57's rule 2) (C: the owner
        accepted it, so nothing is left to file for it; reading design-lab answer 3, "Agree", to
        the now-run rule above, and answer 5, "Yes", to recording reached,
        decisions/questions/P-001-design-lab-open.md, with trial-view notes 2, question 3, "Next
        to reach, everywhere (Recommended)", decisions/questions/P-001-trial-view-notes-2.md:145:
        a milestone already accepted cannot be the next to reach).
    - Each phase's state word is the state of its latest run up to the now run:
      - "Finished" for a run before the now run;
      - "now" for the now run, with its iteration when above 1 ("Design i2");
      - "Not started" when no run of that phase comes before or at the now run.
    - So for Service-Desk at 3117150 the desk shows Discovery Finished, Design now ("Design i2"),
      Build Finished, Launch Not started, and not Done.
    - **Changed by trial-view notes 2, note 1 (2026-10-16):** "the app shows us back in a design
      iteration but also implies build is done? Chronologically that can’t be true". Each phase's
      state word is now the state of its **latest run in the model's timeline**, not its latest
      run up to the now run. The words are taken in this order, the first that fits giving the
      word (plan review round 2, N5):
      1. "Now", for the phase holding the now run, whatever later runs it has, followed by
         " · run <k>" when the project has more than one run;
      2. "Finished" when its latest run comes before the now run;
      3. "Run <k> still to come" when its latest run comes after the now run and an earlier run of
         it came before (C, the mockup's words);
      4. "Not started" when no run of it comes at or before the now run.

      <k> is the run of AC57's Terms (the column) that holds the run the word is about: the now
      run in 1, the phase's latest run in 3. It is never the phase's iteration: in a project whose
      third run goes back to Build, Launch i2 in that run reads "Run 3 still to come" (plan review
      round 1, N4). Words 2 and 4 take no run.

      So for Service-Desk at 3117150 the desk shows Discovery Finished, Design Now · run 2, Build
      Run 2 still to come, Launch Not started, and not Done; Build's first run reads "finished,
      then gone back over" inside its box (AC57). This replaces the line above. The now run's rule
      is unchanged.
  - **Home's phase checklist** (*changed by AC58, 2026-10-16:* drawn as AC58's compact branch
    drawing, read from the bottom up; the count in words and "Phases not tracked" below still
    hold, the count taking the state words above, so Service-Desk reads "1 of 4 phases
    finished"): the four phases in order, then Done. (Its ticks for finished phases are
    gone: AC58's marks replace them, plan review round 1, N4.) The now phase has a gold edge and the word "now";
    a project that is Done gets a "DONE" stamp.
    - The count is in words, "<n> of 4 phases finished". No bar or track fills by fraction
      (AC41): phases differ in size.
    - A project with no phase records (a v2.5 project, or no timeline) shows "Phases not tracked"
      with empty rows, never a guess.
  - **The Status screen** (*changed by AC57, 2026-10-16:* where this bullet and the next differ
    from AC57, AC57 governs; the order now reads from the bottom up, note 15) lists the phases
    strictly in order ("order the phases sequentially rather
    than propping the current phase up to the top to show its in progress. Just find a way to
    emphasize it inline.", 13 Oct).
    - The now phase is marked where it sits: a gold left bar, a gold hairline border and a small
      "NOW" tag.
    - Every row has its state word and a status circle (a filled tick, a gold ring or an empty
      ring), so the state never depends on colour alone.
    - Its info screen lists the six phases as a numbered list ("Just put the phases in a numbered
      list on the info screen here", 10 Oct).
  - **Tapping a phase** opens its milestones below it, each mark in the phase icon's column and its
    text in line with the phase name ("shift the bullets over to the left more. Maybe inline with
    the icons.", 11 Oct). The now phase opens by default. Below the phase come:
    - first, the phase's standard milestones: a count by state, then the list on a tap. Each one
      shows the model's own state word for it: reached, not reached, not recorded, carried over or
      not needed;
    - then the project's own milestones, from docs/PROJECT.md section 3. Each shows its plain-English
      name, its "done when", and its sprints, each linked (AC51's link rule). Its state is "work
      filed" when a sprint is listed or tied to it, "no work filed yet" when none is, or "dropped".
      *Until 2026-10-17* it never read "reached", since no record said so (a model change). From
      then (model S-033 AC7, L-0016; J11) a line that carries ` · reached: <yyyy-mm-dd>` reads
      "reached" with its date, and only such a line: the state is "reached <date>" in place of
      "work filed" or "no work filed yet", its sprints still listed. A line both reached and
      dropped reads "dropped", with J11's note. The date is the line's calendar date as written,
      shown as the desk shows dates ("7 Oct", with the year when it is not this year), never
      moved by a time zone (C);
    - going deeper, into a milestone's steps, is the Timeline (AC51).
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC50` uses Service-Desk's records at a fixed commit (J11's
    fixture). It checks the four phases and their words above, the count in words, that no element
    is sized by a fraction of phases, that no screen text reads "Graduated", each standard
    milestone's state against the fixture's expected lines, and the now run against the fixture's
    expected now run (Design i2 at 3117150). From 2026-10-16 it checks the state words under note
    1's rule above (Build "Run 2 still to come", not "Finished") on the Status screen. Home's
    count and row words are AC65's, which its test checks (no "of 4 phases finished"; plan review
    round 3, N1). From 2026-10-17 it also uses J11's reached fixture (NOT a real record: J11's
    docs/PROJECT.md with M1's line ending ` · reached: 2026-10-07` and MD2's, which lists no
    sprints there, ending ` · reached: 2026-10-16`, J11) and checks, with the test's clock in
    2026, M1's "reached 7 Oct" with its sprints, MD2's "reached 16 Oct", that MD2 neither makes
    its run "now" nor is next to reach (MD3 is), and,
    with J11's fixture as it is, that no milestone reads "reached".

- AC51 [trace: V4 → AC51] — When the owner opens a project's Timeline, then it shows that project's
  one line of work, in order and without a time scale: work, reviews, failures, re-issues and
  blockers, rolling up to milestones and then to phases (ruling 2), every step linked to its
  record.
  - **Superseded on the Timeline screen by AC59 and AC60 (trial-view notes 2, 2026-10-16).** The
    owner's notes 7, 8, 18, 21 and 24 rebuild the Timeline as one continuous feed: the project's
    breakdown into phases, milestones and sprints, read from the bottom up, with what is planned
    above Now. Where this criterion, its changes of 2026-10-15 included, and AC59 or AC60 differ,
    AC59 and AC60 govern. In particular:
    - **"Newest at the top" goes.** Batch 1's note 12 ordered one run's steps newest first. Now the
      whole project reads from the bottom up, from where it started to the finish line (note 15),
      and the top of the feed is what we're delivering, with the planned milestones under it down
      to Now (note 18). So the top is no longer the newest thing done;
    - **one run at a time, the phase strip and the run switch go.** Every run shows, each as its
      own band, in the order it happened (notes 7 and 13);
    - **"Bold headings, no colour" is replaced for phases** (the owner's answer to batch 1's
      plan-review question, decisions/questions/P-001-trial-view-notes-1.md, last paragraph).
      Batch 2's note 13 gives each phase its own colour, on its band and its stretch of the line.
      Milestones themselves stay neutral: a plain neutral circle with a status icon (note 23),
      never a colour of their own (AC48);
    - **steps leave the Timeline.** The levels are project, phases, milestones and sprints; each
      sprint links to its tasks, which are not shown on the Timeline (notes 21 and 24: "I dont
      need to see your specific action plans for delivering each milestone, but i expect to be
      able to access them"). Reviews and checks show as each sprint's marks (AC60); a phase run
      that went back shows as its own band, "gone back over" (AC59);
    - **what stays:** no clock times and no durations (ruling 2), though dates appear, each from a
      record (AC59, AC60); nothing ticks on a tap; the link rule below; and AC22's own timeline in
      the Agents view, with its time axis.

    The tests marked `ac-test: S-001/AC51` that check what goes are replaced by AC59's and AC60's;
    those that check the link rule stay.
  - The owner's words: "the idea is just in general one timeline that shows what has been done, in
    what order, with reviews, blockers, re issues all progressing toward milestones - ultimately
    rolling up to the operating model phases." There is no timeline across projects.
  - **The phase strip** at the top: the four phases, then Done.
    - The run on screen is marked, and its phase names its iterations ("i1 i2").
    - Under each phase, one dot per milestone of its latest run. A dot is filled for reached or
      carried over, ringed for under way (work filed, or not reached in the now run), and empty
      otherwise. "Not recorded" and "not needed" get a hatched mark of their own (AC41). Tapping
      a dot gives the milestone's own state word.
    - Under the strip, a one-line key: "Milestones · reached · under way · not started" (the
      owner's choice: "The latter please", 13 Oct). It adds "not recorded" only when a hatched mark
      shows.
    - Done stays dashed until the Launch gate is passed (AC50).
  - **Runs:** each is labelled "<Phase> i<n>". Where a phase ran more than once, a switch moves
    between its runs.
    - Each run opens with one plain sentence, bad news first. It states only counts and outcomes
      its own steps hold (for example how many checks failed before one passed).
  - **Milestones in a run:** first the standard milestones, in a box that starts closed, showing its
    heading and its count by state; tapping the heading opens the list ("Can we make the milestones
    collapsable?", 13 Oct). Then the project's own milestones, each heading its part of the line.
  - **Steps:**
    - **What they are:** each dispatch, each outcome (passed or failed, by its verdict), each card
      raised, each decision of the owner's, each anomaly and retry (J9, J10), each merge
      (`status/merges.jsonl`), and each sprint's pull request (docs/PROJECT.md section 3). A step's
      colour says who acted (AC48's palette).
    - **Order:** newest at the top, oldest at the bottom, with a milestone still under way first
      ("can you orient the timeline from the bottom to top ( bottom of the scroll is the oldest
      items top is the newest.", 13 Oct).
    - **No time scale:** no clock times and no durations, only order (ruling 2). A marker shows
      where the day changes.
    - **Within a run:** a retry, or a piece of research sent round again, is a note on the line
      ("Second try", "Round 2 · after your re-specify"), not a new iteration.
    - **Stalls:** a stall note shows only where a session ran past its limit (J9) or a card waited.
      It names what waited and gives no length: "no durations" wins here (ruling 2). The lab's
      "Nothing moved for 2 h 32 min" is not copied; the length stays in the Agents view (AC22).
  - **Every step is linked to its record** ("can you link wherever these
    documents/recordings/actions/PRs/Merges/Actions etc  are recorded in the repo?", 13 Oct).
    - A step's "From the record" opens its file on the default branch
      (`https://github.com/<repo>/blob/<defaultBranch>/<path>`, a path the tree holds, J1) or its
      pull request (`https://github.com/<repo>/pull/<n>`).
    - **The record that holds each kind of step**, linked only when the tree holds the path:
      - an outcome: its `record` (J9);
      - a dispatch: its session's outcome `record` once the session has ended, and until then the
        item's `status/<item>.toml`;
      - a card raised: `queue/<item>-<gate>-<card>.md`;
      - an owner's decision on a card: `decisions/<item>/<gate>-<card>.md`;
      - an owner question's ruling: `decisions/questions/<name>.md`;
      - an anomaly or a retry: its month's `dispatch-log/<YYYY-MM>.jsonl`;
      - a merge: its `merge` commit from `status/merges.jsonl`
        (`https://github.com/<repo>/commit/<sha>`);
      - a sprint: its pull request, or its item's outcome `record`.
    - So a step whose check report was never kept as a file names the record that holds the
      result. The lab's 13 Oct note says four steps and lists five such records:
      decisions/P-001/dor-fail-2.md, decisions/P-001/dor-4.md, queue/P-001-stop-6.md,
      decisions/Q-010/stop-1.md and decisions/Q-012/stop-1.md.
    - Otherwise it says "no file recorded". No link is guessed.
  - **Lighter** ("Can you lighten the timeline up - as far as colors and thin out the line weight
    and just generally tigthen this timeline visual up a little bit.", 13 Oct): tinted icon tiles
    with a thin outline, a 1 px line, outline ticks, lighter titles.
  - **A milestone's detail opens in place** (the lab folded screen 5, the breakdown, into this
    one). It holds:
    - its steps so far and how they went;
    - the pieces of work under it;
    - who did which step, or "not recorded" where a record doesn't name who.

    All of it is counted from the steps on the line.
  - **The owner's answer shows in place:** once the owner answers from the queue, the step that
    waited ticks off only when AC52's tracker has its record. A tap in the desk alone ticks
    nothing.
  - AC22's per-item timeline in the Agents view keeps its time axis. This timeline has none.
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC51` uses J11's fixture. It checks the runs, their labels, the
    standard milestones' states and the sprints against the fixture's expected lines, and the
    newest-first order. It checks that every link is either a path in the fixture's tree or a pull
    request, and that a step with no file shows "no file recorded".
  - **Changed by trial-view notes 12 and 13 (2026-10-15).** The owner's words: "The timeline
    doesn’t look like what we discussed originally, and additionally it’s a little difficult to
    exactly grasp the timeline flow and critical elements"; "the milestones in the timeline are so
    bland you would honestly miss them, nor does the grouping function work here for the actual
    tkmeljne". The Timeline is rebuilt to the agreed screen 4
    (docs/design/screens/04-timeline.html; the screenshots at 11:31 and 11:33 in
    docs/design/references/trial-view/). Where this block and the bullets above differ, this
    block governs.
    - **It opens on AC50's now run** (plan review round 1, N6): Design i2 in J11's fixture. When
      no run is now (AC50's "not recorded"), it opens on the newest run (C).
    - **One line per phase run.** One run is on screen at a time: the phase strip picks the
      phase, and the switch picks its run ("Design i1", "Design i2"). The run's line reads newest
      at the top.
    - **The line:** a thin vertical line (1 px, as the agreed screen's final pass draws it)
      through the icon column. Each step is a tinted icon tile centred on that line, with its
      bold title over a small line, and a state circle at the right: ticked when done, failed,
      ringed when waiting on the owner, dashed while under way, plain when still to come. The
      tile's tint and icon say who acted (AC48's palette), with a word or shape too (AC42).
    - **Milestones stand out as headings on the line.** Each milestone is a heading row (a
      heading to assistive technology too): a taller capsule tile, a larger title, and above it
      a small upper-case label with the milestone's code ("MD2") and its state word (AC50). It
      stands out by its shape, size and weight; its colour stays neutral (AC48: "Milestones are
      neutral"; the owner's answer, "Bold headings, no colour (Recommended)",
      decisions/questions/P-001-trial-view-notes-1.md, last paragraph).
    - **Each run's steps are grouped under the milestone they belong to**, as the model's
      timeline places them (J11): a project milestone, then each of its sprints, then that
      sprint's steps. Steps the model's timeline ties to no milestone (its `untagged` lines) sit
      together under a heading "Not tied to a milestone" (C), at the bottom of the run.
      Milestones are ordered as the bullets above say: a milestone still under way first, then
      newest first. Tapping a milestone heading opens its detail in place, as above.
    - **The standard milestones box** sits at the top of the run and starts folded: its heading
      and its count by state. A tap opens the list and a second tap folds it away. Its state is
      kept through a refresh (AC56).
    - **In place on the line:** a marker where the day changes; iteration notes where a run
      began after a gate sent the work back; retry notes ("Second try", "Round 2 · after your
      re-specify"); stall notes (what waited, no length); and confirmations: the step that waited
      on the owner ticks off where it sits once its record arrives (AC52's tracker).
    - **What the agreed screen has that is not carried** (each breaks a rule of this spec):
      - the clock time beside each step: the line shows order and day markers only (ruling 2);
      - stall lengths such as "Nothing moved for 2 h 32 min" (ruling 2; the length stays in the
        Agents view, AC22);
      - the "I merged it" button that ticks a step on a tap: only a record ticks a step;
      - a colour for each milestone carried down the line (AC48: milestones are neutral; the
        owner's answer, "Bold headings, no colour (Recommended)",
        decisions/questions/P-001-trial-view-notes-1.md, last paragraph);
      - the written plain-English text in each step's pop-up: the standard summary only (AC49);
      - the coral "+" in the bottom bar (AC48), and the Inter web font (AC42; the phone's own
        font, decisions/questions/P-001-design-pick.md);
      - the lab's history written by hand: every step comes from the records (J9, J10, J11).

      Every link still follows the link rule above: a path the tree holds or a pull request, and
      otherwise "no file recorded". No link is guessed.
  - The test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC51` also checks, with J11's fixture:
    - that the Timeline opens on the fixture's expected now run (Design i2), and on the newest
      run in a fixture with no now run;
    - that one run is on screen, and the switch moves between a phase's runs;
    - that every step sits under the milestone and sprint the fixture's `NOTE:` lines place it
      under, and every `untagged` step under "Not tied to a milestone";
    - that each milestone row is a heading with its code and state word, and that milestone rows
      use no colour of their own;
    - that each step's tile is centred on the line (within 1 px), and the line is unbroken from
      the run's first row to its last;
    - that the standard milestones box starts folded, opens and folds on a tap;
    - that no clock time or duration appears on the line.

- AC52 [trace: V5 → AC52] — When the owner opens a project's Queue, then everything that needs the
  owner in that project, and nothing from any other, is there, grouped by the kind of action it
  needs; a tap opens its response sheet; the answer goes to GitHub in two taps; and a tracker
  shows the answer recorded, picked up and handed on, each from its record (ruling 6).
  - The owner's words: "I actually think the groups map to the type of action needed. Keep in mind
    I don't like to "cross boundaries" when I work. I want each of my projects and everything
    within them retained within their respective tile on that front page. ... And agreed on the 2
    taps." There is no queue across projects (for now).
  - **The groups.** Every item that needs the owner (Terms) sits in exactly one group:
    - **Decisions:** open cards other than gate cards and merge cards (hold cards, AC46, go here:
      my choice, C), open owner questions, and a `waiting-owner` status whose card file is missing
      (Terms);
    - **Gate rulings:** open cards whose gate is `dor`, `criteria` or `launch` (model S-024);
    - **Verdicts to write:** the verdict files a pull request needs from the owner. No record the
      desk reads names them today, so this group's one line reads "not recorded" (a model change).
      Until then the owner's own verdict box opens from each pull request in Merges (below);
    - **Merges:** pull requests that need the owner (Terms), and merge cards (AC47);
    - **Deploys:** a workflow run in J1's lists whose `status` is `waiting` (waiting for an
      environment approval, C), linked to its run. Its approver shows "not recorded" (a model
      change). Terms' four things don't include it, so it is shown but not counted in what needs
      the owner, and it never stops "Quiet".
      - *The owner's answer, 2026-10-16:* waiting deploys should count as needing the owner
        (design-lab question 6, "Yes", decisions/questions/P-001-design-lab-open.md), once the
        operating model records who approves each one ("After the model change (Recommended)",
        decisions/questions/P-001-design-lab-answers-followup.md, answer 1). Until the model
        change "deploy approvals tied to the owner" lands ("What this spec does not cover"), this
        line stays as built.
      - **Changed 2026-10-18** (decisions/questions/P-001-notify-deploys.md, Terms): in a project whose J7
        entry has `"deploysNeedOwner": true` (Service-Desk only), a waiting deploy counts as
        needing the owner: in the group's count, the pill, Home's top line and Quiet. In any other
        project the line stays as built, shown and not counted. Its approver still shows "not
        recorded".
      - **Known gap:** J1 lists only the runs on the current head. A deploy still waiting on an
        earlier head drops out of the group once the head moves, and the desk can't show it.
  - **Each group** has a title and a count. Its rows have a coloured edge and a word or shape, never
    colour alone. A group with nothing in it shrinks to one line.
    - **Changed by AC66 (trial-view notes 2, note 29, 2026-10-16):** the coloured edges and icons
      go neutral; only the coral diamond, "needs you", keeps its colour. Each row also names the
      milestone it unblocks and that milestone's why, with its Context one tap away, and each
      "Done today" row links to its sprint on the Timeline. Where this criterion and AC66 differ,
      AC66 governs.
  - **Each row** says:
    - what it is, in plain words, with its code after;
    - how long it has waited: from its raised time (AC20's rules), or "wait not recorded".

    A row never says whether it can be acted on yet: no record the desk reads says so (see
    Dependencies below).
  - **Order within a group:** the earliest raised first. An item with no raised time goes last.
    Nothing reorders under the owner's thumb (AC40).
  - **Dependencies** ("after #54") are named only where a record the desk reads states one. None
    does today (a model change), so none is named, and no row is marked "do first".
  - **When nothing waits** and the project was read successfully within 3 minutes: "Nothing waits
    on you in <project>".
  - **"Done today":** a fold at the bottom holds the items answered today (in `ownerTimeZone`), by
    the time each record gives:
    - a card: the `time` of its `decision` entry in the dispatch log (J2), which is when the
      Orchestrator read the answer;
    - an owner question: the oldest commit of its answer file (J2's history read, as AC20);
    - a pull request, hold card or merge card: no record the desk reads gives the time it was
      closed, merged or cleared. One that left the queue while the page was open shows in the fold
      as "no longer open, time not recorded"; one that left while it was closed doesn't show.
    - **Each row says what was ruled, in words, with its code after** (trial-view note 10,
      2026-10-15: "Should done today really be 2 p-001 records?"; two separate rulings made that
      day, so two rows is right, but each row showed only its file name):
      - an owner's ruling: its record's first line, wrapping, without the leading
        `Ruling:`, clipped to three lines when longer as "Your last action" is (AC55, from
        2026-10-16) (for example "the five agreed screens (Home, Project, Team, Timeline, and Queue
        with its response card) are the desk's one design direction, …", from
        `decisions/questions/P-001-design-pick.md`), then the file's name as its code;
      - a card answered: J9's decision words ("P-001: you answered dor-4 with build", with
