        " (proxy)" when the log entry says so), then the card's code;
      - a pull request: its title, then `PR #<n>`.

      **Only the owner's rulings count** (plan review round 1, B1): a file in
      `decisions/questions/` counts only when its line 1 starts `Ruling:`, or `Decision:` for a
      target-user profile approval (`<item>-user-profile-<n>.md`, decisions/README.md), whose row
      shows the words after `Decision:` ("approve"), then its code. Every other file there, such
      as a `-notes.md` file or a Critic's verdict (`P-001-milestones-adoption-critic-1.md`, line 1
      `Verdict: FAIL`), is not the owner's and is ignored: never shown, never counted. No words
      are added.
  - **The response sheet** slides up over the queue when the owner taps an item. It holds:
    - **what it is, why it's here and what was checked** (CI, AC29), each from its record;
    - **for a gate ruling:** the case's plain-English opening paragraph, quoted, then links to the
      case (`docs/gates/<item>/<phase>-gate-<n>-case.md`) and the Critic's view
      (`reviews/<item>/_<phase>-gate-<n>-critic.md`), each only when the tree holds it.
      `<phase>` comes from the card's gate: `dor` → `design`, `criteria` → `build`, `launch` →
      `launch` (docs/gates/_GATE.md:4); `<n>` is the card's number. Then the
      user representative's line: "not needed" when docs/PROJECT.md section 9 holds "The owner is
      the user.", otherwise its record;
    - **the options**, one per chip, each spelled out:
      - a card's option words, exactly as the card lists them (J2);
      - a question's lettered options;
      - a gate card's proceed, hold, recycle and kill, and any older words the card lists;
    - **the recommendation**, labelled and never preselected: a question's `RECOMMENDATION:` line,
      or the recommendation a gate card quotes. A verdict has none: that word is the owner's alone;
    - **what happens next** with the chip the owner picks, in the card's own words or
      decisions/README.md's;
    - **everything else prefilled** (AC53).
  - **A pull request in Merges** has no option chips. Its sheet holds two things:
    - **"Open the pull request in GitHub"**, where the owner merges;
    - **the owner's own verdict box** (AC53), for `reviews/<PR>/owner.md` at the pull request's head
      branch (`head.ref` in J1's open-PR list). Whether that file already exists on that branch
      shows "not recorded", because the desk reads only the default branch's tree.
  - **Hold cards and merge cards** have no answer file (AC46, AC47) and no pull request to open,
    so they have no chips, no text box and no two-tap route. Their sheet says what the owner does,
    in AC46's or AC47's words, and links the card's file and, for a hold card, the governance run
    that is not green.
  - **Two taps:** for a card or a question, the owner picks an option, then taps to open GitHub
    with the answer written (AC53), and commits there. For the owner's verdict, the box is
    prefilled, so the one tap opens GitHub. The desk holds no write credential.
  - **A short confirmation** follows: it says what happens next and offers "See it on the timeline".
    The row stays in its group, marked "opened in GitHub", until the record arrives.
  - **The tracker**, three steps. Each is ticked only from its record, and shows "not recorded"
    where no record of that kind exists:
    1. **answer recorded:** the answer file is on the default branch (J2; AC3, AC7). For a pull
       request: it is no longer in the open list ("no longer open"; the desk can't tell merged
       from closed). For the owner's verdict file on a pull request's branch: "not recorded",
       since the desk reads only the default branch;
    2. **picked up:** the dispatch log's `decision` entry for that card (J2). For an owner question:
       its question file has gone (AC19);
    3. **handed to the next step:** the item's next `dispatch` or `done` entry after that decision
       (J9).
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC52` uses fixtures holding one item of each group in each of two
    projects. It checks:
    - each item's group, and that neither project's queue holds the other's;
    - the order within a group, and that no row claims whether it can be acted on;
    - that the recommendation is never preselected, and that a verdict has none;
    - that each pull request in Merges offers the owner's verdict box at its head branch, with
      "not recorded" for whether the file exists;
    - that hold and merge cards offer no chips and no link to commit;
    - each tracker step and each "Done today" time against its record;
    - from 2026-10-18: a waiting deploy in a `deploysNeedOwner` project counted in its group, the
      pill and Home's top line, and stopping Quiet; one in a project without it shown and not
      counted;
    - from 2026-10-15 (note 10): each "Done today" row's words against its record's first line or
      J9's decision words, with the code after, for two rulings made on the same day, and that a
      file whose line 1 starts neither `Ruling:` nor, for a profile approval, `Decision:` (such as
      a Critic's verdict) is not shown;
    - from 2026-10-16 (the follow-up ruling, answer 2): with the owner's real record
      decisions/questions/P-001-design-lab-open.md as a fixture, at 360×740 and 414×896, that its
      "Done today" row shows the first three lines of its words, faded at the cut, with its code
      after and outside the control; that its control opens a bubble whose text equals those
      words exactly, from "11 Ruling: 1 I see no need…" to the end of line 1; that the bubble,
      where it is taller than the viewport, is held within it less a margin, clear of the bottom
      bar, scrolls up and down and never sideways, with "Close" in view; and that a ruling of
      three lines or fewer shows whole, with no fade and no control. AC56's test checks its close
      and refresh rules.

- AC53 [trace: V5 → AC53] — When an item in the queue is answered by a file the owner commits in
  GitHub, then its response sheet holds that file's text, prefilled in a text box the owner can
  edit, with a line that applies the repository's rule for it, and one button that opens GitHub's
  new-file page at the right branch and path with the text in it, or copies the text when the link
  would pass the cap.
  - The owner's words (13 Oct): "For anything that is in my queue that i submit or push in Github -
    can you put the prefilled message and a text box here. If i am merging a reviewer statement fully
    prefilled. If it is my own statement,  It will be very beneficial to have the text box/prefilled
    content store the structure and minimum requirement that is expected in the repo. For example
    the textbox would be "Verdict: PASS
    Any suggestions to commit along witht the verdict"

    This way i can read the summary, write up my message and jump straight to github to merge or
    commit"
  - **What the box holds:**
    - **a card:** `Decision: <word>`, then an empty line 2 (no `Proxy:` line: the owner commits it,
      J3). For a gate card, `Into: ` follows with recycle, and `Reason: ` with hold and recycle
      (decisions/README.md);
    - **an owner question:** J3's question content (`Ruling: <letter>`, then
      `Question: questions/<name>.md`), then, from 2026-10-17, on the next line the question's
      `Milestone:` line when it has one (J2), copied as the question has it, trailing spaces
      dropped, as decisions/README.md:43 asks of an answer to a filed question (model S-033).
      The line is the answer box's only: the accepted views' answer link (AC19, J3) stays as
      accepted (AC43), a known limit J2 names;
    - **the owner's own verdict** on a pull request: `Verdict: PASS`, an empty line, then
      `Any suggestions to commit along with the verdict:` and an empty line. Its path is
      `reviews/<PR>/owner.md`. It opens from every open pull request in Merges (AC52);
    - **a Reviewer's statement:** the Reviewer's words, exactly as written, labelled "Commit it
      unchanged". Its path is `reviews/<PR>/reviewer.md`. No record the desk reads holds the
      statement before it is committed (a model change). Until one does, the box shows "not
      recorded" and offers no link. The desk never writes the statement itself.
  - **The rule line** updates as the owner types. It says what is wrong, and leaves the button
    available (C):
    - for a verdict: the first line starting `Verdict:` at column 0 decides; `Verdict:` may only
      start a line; and it must read exactly `Verdict: PASS` or `Verdict: FAIL` (reviews/README.md);
    - for a card: line 1 is `Decision: <word>`, one of the card's words; hold and recycle need a
      `Reason:` line with text, and recycle an `Into:` line (decisions/README.md);
    - for a question: line 1 is `Ruling: <letter>`. From 2026-10-17, when the question file has a
      `Milestone:` line (J2), the answer's first `Milestone:` line in column 0 after line 1 holds
      the same value, as decisions/README.md asks of an answer to a filed question (model
      S-033); when it is missing or differs, the line says so, for example "The answer's
      Milestone line should read `Milestone: M2`, as the question's does".

    It covers these forms only. It doesn't check rules this project doesn't use, such as the
    `User-Override:` line or the phases each gate allows for `Into:` (decisions/README.md). The
    Orchestrator still applies those.
  - **The button** opens J3's URL with the box's text as the content:
    - for cards and questions, at the default branch (J3, unchanged);
    - for a verdict file, at the pull request's head branch (its `head.ref` in J1's open-PR list);
    - the box shows the branch and path it will write.
  - **When it would not fit:** when the link would pass `linkCap` (6,000 characters, J7), or the
    repository's `contentPrefill` is `false`, the button copies the text and opens the new-file page
    with only the file name, and says so (AC17).
    - AC18's message applies to cards and questions only: a pull request's branch is not the
      default branch.
    - A v2.5 project keeps AC19's copy route.
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC53`, and J3's join test, check:
    - the URL for a card, a gate card with recycle, a question, and the owner's verdict at a pull
      request's head branch;
    - the copy route for a text over 6,000 characters;
    - the rule line's words for a right verdict, a verdict with `Verdict:` mid-line, a hold with no
      reason, and a recycle with no `Into:`;
    - "not recorded" and no link for a Reviewer's statement;
    - from 2026-10-17: a question in S-033's form gives a box and a URL with its `Milestone:`
      line after the `Question:` line, value for value (a project milestone, two ids, a standard
      id, and `none (<reason>)`), for a lettered, a numbered and an unsplittable question, with
      the round trip of a `Milestone: none (<reason>)` value, brackets and spaces, through the
      URL; a question in the old form gives none; the rule line names a missing or changed
      `Milestone:` line and leaves the button available; and the accepted view's answer link for
      the same question is unchanged, with no `Milestone:` line (AC19's test,
      tests/desk/ac16-19-answering.test.mjs:89-94, stands as it is).
  - **Extended by trial-view note 15 (2026-10-15):** "whereever a ruling is requested can you
    format it in a question, then answer box that would then prefill my rulings?"
    - **Where it applies:** every ruling asked of the owner: an owner question
      (`questions/<name>.md`), a decision card and a gate card. Not a hold card or a merge card
      (they have no answer file, AC52), and not the owner's verdict on a pull request (its box is
      above).
    - **The sheet shows each question on its own, followed by its own answer box.** The box holds:
      - the question's options as choices, when the question lists them;
      - the recommendation, labelled "Recommended" and never picked (AC52), or "no recommendation
        given";
      - a text box for the owner's own words.
    - **Where the questions come from:**
      - **A card:** one question, the text under its `## 1. The decision` heading (the card form,
        for example queue/P-001-stop-6.md lines 6 to 7). Its choices are the card's option words
        (J2), and its recommendation as AC52 says.
      - **An owner question:** its `OPTIONS:` block, the lines after `OPTIONS:` that start with
        two spaces, up to the first line that doesn't (questions/_TEMPLATE.md). A line indented
        further continues the line above it (C).
        - **Lettered:** every line in the block starts `  <capital letter>. `. It is one question,
          the words after `# Ruling needed: `, with the letters as its choices. Its
          recommendation is the letter that starts the `RECOMMENDATION:` line.
        - **Numbered:** every line starts `  <number>. `, the numbers running 1, 2, 3 and so on.
          It is one question per number, its text the rest of its line. The file lists no options
          for a number, so its box has no choices: the recommendation and the text box. The
          number's recommendation is its part of the `RECOMMENDATION:` line: the words
          `RECOMMENDATION: ` are removed first, then the rest is split at `; `, and the part that
          starts with that number and a space is that number's ("1 " never matches "10 " or
          "11 "), with a final full stop dropped (for example
          questions/P-001-design-lab-open.md: "1 leave deferred; 2 yes; …; 11 yes, Active.").
      - **Can't be split:** no `OPTIONS:` line, an empty block, lettered and numbered lines
        mixed, or numbers repeated, missing or out of order. Then the sheet falls back to one
        box, as above: the file's text shown whole, its link, and one text box, with the line
        "This question file couldn't be split into questions". Nothing is guessed.
    - **What the answers prefill**, in the box above, in the form decisions/README.md requires
      (the ruling line, then the question and its options as they were put, never a
      recommendation):
      - **a card:** as above: `Decision: <the word picked>` and an empty line 2; for a gate card
        the owner's words go in `Reason: ` with hold and recycle, and otherwise, on any card,
        after a blank line as J3's note. On a phase-gate card each line of that note is indented
        by two spaces, so that no note line can be read as an `Into:`, `Reason:`, `Nothing
        needed:` or `User-Override:` field (plan review round 2, N5). **A card's answer carries only that card form**
        (decisions/README.md's first section, plan review round 1, N3), not the question and its
        options: they are already committed in the card file itself
        (`queue/<item>-<gate>-<card>.md`), which the answer's path names by gate and number, and
        the Orchestrator reads only lines 1 and 2 of a card's answer, plus a gate card's
        `Into:`, `Reason:`, `Nothing needed:` and `User-Override:` lines; any other later line is
        not read (decisions/README.md; J2's "only line 1 is read"). The question-and-options
        line of the rule is met by the card file (C);
      - **a lettered question:** `Ruling: <letter>`, an empty line, `Question:
        questions/<name>.md` (J3), from 2026-10-17 the question's `Milestone:` line on the next
        line when it has one (as above), an empty line, then `Question, as put: <the title>` and
        `Options, as put:` with the block's lines exactly as in the file. The owner's words
        follow after a blank line;
      - **a numbered question:** line 1 is `Ruling: ` then each answered number and its answer,
        in number order, joined by `; ` ("Ruling: 1 leave deferred; 3 agree"), the form of
        decisions/questions/P-001-s001-owner-questions.md line 1. Each answer is the owner's
        words for that number, made into one line, in this order: first each line break, and
        each run of spaces around it, becomes one space; then each `; ` inside an answer becomes
        `, `, so that `; ` only ever separates numbers (C). So "a;", a line break and "b" give
        "a, b" (plan review round 2, N1). Then an empty line, `Question: questions/<name>.md`
        (from 2026-10-17 followed on the next line by the question's `Milestone:` line when it
        has one, as above), an empty line, and `Questions, as put:` with the block's lines exactly as
        in the file. A number
        left blank is left out of line 1. No recommendation is copied in;
      - **a file that couldn't be split:** J3's question form, from 2026-10-17 with the
        `Milestone:` line as above, and the owner's words after it.

      The path stays `decisions/questions/<name>.md` for a question and the card's answer path
      for a card (J3). A longer text that passes `linkCap` takes AC17's copy route, as above.
    - **The rule line** also checks a numbered ruling: line 1 starts `Ruling: `, and it names any
      number with no answer yet ("No answer yet for 3 and 7"). When an answer held a line break
      or `; `, it says so and how it was joined ("Answer 4 joined into one line"). The button
      stays available (C).
    - Each answer box keeps what the owner typed or picked through a refresh (AC56).
  - The test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC53` also checks, from 2026-10-15:
    - a card's question from its `## 1. The decision` section, with its option words as choices;
    - a lettered question file, and questions/P-001-design-lab-open.md as a numbered one (eleven
      pairs, each with its own recommendation, none picked);
    - the prefilled text for each, character for character, with a blank number left out of
      line 1, an answer with a line break and one with `; ` each joined into one line, an answer
      of "a;", a line break and "b" giving "a, b" (line breaks joined first), and the rule line's
      words for them;
    - on a phase-gate card, that each line of the owner's note is indented by two spaces, so that
      a note line such as `Into: build` or `Reason: none` is never read as a field;
    - the one-box fallback for a file with no `OPTIONS:` line, one with mixed lines and one with
      a number missing.

**Home, after the owner's trial-view notes** (added 2026-10-14)
*From the owner's notes of 14 Oct on the sandbox's Home screen and two answers, recorded verbatim
at decisions/questions/P-001-trial-view-notes-1.md, read as its "How it is read" section reads
them, and, for (a)'s summary, the owner's later ruling at
decisions/questions/P-001-tile-summary-clip.md, read as its own "How it is read" section reads it;
where the two differ on the summary, the later ruling governs. The owner's words are quoted
exactly as written, typos included. Where AC48 and this criterion differ on Home, this criterion
governs.*

- AC54 [trace: V1 → AC54] — When the owner opens the sandbox's Home screen, then each card shows
  the first three lines of the project's own brief summary, with the whole summary one tap away;
  the "latest" line opens from the card in a bubble; the
  cards sit in a two-column grid on the phone; and the projects sit in three collapsible groups
  whose headers never hide what waits on the owner.
  - **(a) The brief summary**, in the place AC37's line had on the card (AC48). Note 1: "Can we
    introduce a brief project summary  that doesn’t get clipped off at the top of the card on the
    home page that takes the place of the “latest” text." Answer 1: "README's first paragraph
    (Recommended)". Changed on 2026-10-15 by the owner's ruling
    decisions/questions/P-001-tile-summary-clip.md: "I don’t like how the text forces the tile to
    run on so long vertically"; answer: "Clip to 3 lines (Recommended)" ("Show the summary's
    first three lines with a fade, and the whole text one tap away. Keeps the two-column grid you
    asked for and every tile the same height.").
    - **Where it comes from:** the first paragraph after the title in the project's `README.md`
      on its default branch: the file of that name at the root of the tree J1 already reads,
      fetched as J11 says. Every connected project gets one, a v2.5 project too.
    - **How the paragraph is found** (the parsing rules are mine, C; the record names only "the
      first paragraph after the title"):
      - the title is the file's first line starting `# `;
      - a block is a run of non-blank lines. After the title, the summary is the first block that
        is a plain paragraph: one whose first line does not start with `#`, `-`, `*`, `+`, `>`,
        `|`, `<`, a code fence or a number followed by `.`. Blocks of those other kinds are
        skipped. A heading after the title ends the search;
      - its lines are joined with single spaces. Inline Markdown is shown as plain text: emphasis
        marks and backticks are dropped and a link shows only its text. Nothing else is changed,
        added or shortened.
      - Example: Service-Desk's README at 4433c4f gives "The owner's phone-first desk over every
        project the agents run: one card per project showing what waits on the owner, what the
        team is doing and where the work is heading, with each answer opened in GitHub ready to
        commit. It reads the projects' records and holds no write access."
    - **Three lines on the card, faded at the cut:** the summary wraps, and the card shows its
      first three lines. When the whole summary takes more than three lines at the card's
      width, the third line fades out at its end into the card's background, so the owner can
      see it goes on. This holds at every width down to 360 px, in both the light and the dark
      theme, the fade matching each theme's card background.
      - **The cut never changes the words, only how many show:** the card holds the whole
        summary, word for word, and only hides the lines after the third. Nothing is shortened,
        reworded or given an ellipsis, and nothing scrolls inside the card.
      - **Three lines or fewer:** the summary shows whole, with no fade and no control.
      - **Every tile the same height** (the answer's words): the summary area always takes
        exactly three lines' height, whatever it holds, so a shorter summary, "no summary in this
        project's README" or "summary not available" leaves the same space. Cards in the same
        grid row share one height.
      - Whether the summary runs past three lines is judged at the card's width as it is drawn,
        and again when that width changes (a turned phone, a resized window).
    - **The whole summary, one tap away:** when the summary runs past three lines, the three
      lines on the card are a control of their own, a real button: in the tab order, opened by a
      tap, a click, or Enter or Space, with the accessible name "Show the whole summary of <project
      name>" (C), not the summary's own words. Like the "Latest" control (b), it is a separate tap
      target: a tap on it never opens the Project screen, and a tap elsewhere on the card still
      does.
      - It opens a bubble holding the whole summary, word for word and never cut: its text
        equals the summary exactly, wraps, sits wholly inside the viewport at every width down
        to 360 px, placed above or below the summary to fit; nothing in it is cut short, given
        an ellipsis or scrolled sideways. It respects reduced motion (AC42). From 2026-10-16, a
        bubble taller than the viewport follows AC55's rule for one (C): held to the viewport's
        height less a margin, scrolling up and down inside itself, its "Close" in view.
      - It closes as AC56 requires of every sheet and bubble: its visible "Close" control, a tap
        outside it, or Escape, with focus back on the summary control. A second tap on the
        summary also closes it, as with "Latest" (C).
      - Only one bubble is open at a time: opening the summary bubble closes the "Latest"
        bubble, and opening the "Latest" bubble, by tap or by hover, closes the summary bubble.
    - **With no `README.md`, no title, or no plain paragraph before the next heading or the end
      of the file:** the card says "no summary in this project's README". The desk writes no
      summary of its own (AC41).
    - **Before the read arrives, or when it fails:** "summary not available", with the reason one
      tap away (AC41; J11's optional reads). The project is never "Can't read" for that reason.
  - **(b) The "latest" line, in a bubble.** Note 2: "Move the latest text the be a popup bubble
    that appears on hover (and the mobile version of hover) also not clipping it."
    - **Which line:** for a v3 project, AC37's where-it's-heading line; for a v2.5 project, its
      latest ledger entry's title (AC10's headline, J9). The bubble's text is that line exactly,
      character for character, with any "not recorded" part as AC37 shows it. It is no longer
      printed on the card. It stays on the Status screen (AC48).
    - **The control:** a small control on the card, with the accessible name "Latest" (the
      owner's word; C) and an icon in Home's quiet line style (AC48). It is a separate tap target:
      a tap on it never opens the Project screen, and a tap elsewhere on the card still does.
    - **Opening:**
      - with a pointer, on hover over the control, and it closes when the pointer leaves the
        control and the bubble;
      - on a touch screen, on a tap on the control; a second tap, a tap outside the bubble, or
        Escape closes it;
      - from the keyboard: the control is in the tab order, opens on Enter or Space and on focus,
        and Escape closes it, returning focus to the control. A bubble closed with Escape stays
        closed while focus stays on its control; it opens again only on Enter or Space, or when
        focus leaves and comes back (plan review round 1, N1; the usual tooltip pattern).

      One bubble is open at a time.
    - **Never clipped:** the bubble's text wraps; the bubble sits wholly inside the viewport at
      every width down to 360 px, placed above or below the control to fit; nothing in it is cut
      short, given an ellipsis or scrolled sideways. It respects reduced motion (AC42). From
      2026-10-16, a bubble taller than the viewport follows AC55's rule for one (C).
  - **(c) A two-column grid on the phone.** Note 3: "Currently on my phone the cards are just
    single rows, I liked the summary view being a grid of sorts so I could look across multiple
    projects - can we condense the current structure down to accommodate that?"
    - At every width up to 600 px (C), 360 px included, each group's cards sit in two columns,
      as in the lab's Home screen (docs/design/screens/01-home.html:40). Wider screens may show
      more.
    - The card is condensed to fit, never clipped: long names wrap (H5's long titles), the
      summary holds to its three lines (a), and nothing scrolls sideways (AC42).
    - H5's extremes still pass AC42's test, with one more fixture: a README paragraph of 600
      characters (C).
  - **(d) Three collapsible groups.** Note 4: "Can we introduce essentially a collapsible function
    for projects that aren’t active or, in this case not covered by the service desk?" Answer 2:
    "Active, Done, and closed or NA so 3 collapsible groupings".
    - **The groups, in this order:** Active; Done; Closed or not applicable. AC8's box order holds
      within each group (AC35). A group with no project still shows its header, with "0 projects"
      (C), so the screen's shape does not change as projects move.
    - **Which group a project is in**, from records the desk already reads, checked in this
      order:
      1. **Closed or not applicable, not run through its phases:** a v2.5 project (`"model":
         "v2.5"` in J7). The desk does not take it through its phases (AC50 shows "Phases not
         tracked").
      2. **Done:** a v3 project that is Done by AC50's rule: the model's timeline shows its
         Graduated mark, or its graduation ruling record (`decisions/questions/<item>-graduation.md`)
         is on the default branch (J11).
      3. **Closed or not applicable, closed:** a v3 project whose every project item (a status
         file with `kind = "project"`, J9) is in state `closed`, with an outcome other than
         `re-scope`: for example `drop`, or `kill` at a gate ("the project closes").
      4. **Active:** every other project. That includes a project whose group no record decides:
         one with no project item's status file, one whose records have not been read yet or
         can't be read, and one with any project item not `closed`.
    - **A project item closed with `re-scope`** goes in Active, until the owner rules otherwise.
      This reading is mine (C): the record names "one that was closed", and the model records
      `re-scope` as "closed (re-scope)" (orchestrator.py:857), but re-scope is the stop rule's
      option to scope the work again, not to end it, and the owner's note 4 counts the project
      the desk does cover as active. Service-Desk's own item, P-001, is closed with `re-scope`
      (decisions/P-001/stop-6.md) and its work has gone on since on the owner's lighter path; read
      literally, both connected projects would sit in collapsed groups and Active would be empty.
      The owner was asked to confirm (Open questions, trial-view notes 1; filed as question 11 of
      questions/P-001-design-lab-open.md, 2026-10-15), and answered "Yes" (decisions/questions/P-001-design-lab-open.md).
    - **How each group starts:** Active open; Done and Closed or not applicable closed.
    - **Each group's header:**
      - the group's name and its project count ("1 project", "2 projects");
      - when anything in it waits on the owner, that count in words ("2 waiting on you"), on the
        header whether the group is open or closed, so nothing that needs the owner is hidden. It
        is the same count the cards' pills give (AC48), added up over the group;
      - when a project in it can't be read, that count in words ("1 can't be read"), so AC35's
        promise that can't-read shows on the first screen still holds (C);
      - its open or closed state, shown by a mark and by the header's accessible state, never by
        colour alone (AC42).

      The header is a button: a tap, or Enter or Space from the keyboard, opens or closes the
      group. Opening or closing a group never reorders the cards in any group (AC40).
    - **Home's top line** still counts everything that waits on the owner across all groups
      (AC48).
    - **Kept on the device only:** each group's open or closed state is kept in local storage
      under its own key, `desk-home-groups-v1`, wrapped as J5's storage is. When storage fails
      (a private window, cleared storage), the groups start as above on each load and nothing
      else breaks. Nothing is stored on Cloudflare.
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC54` renders Home from fixtures and checks:
    - **(a)** the summary for Service-Desk's README at 4433c4f, exactly as quoted above; a
      paragraph over several lines, joined; inline Markdown shown as plain text; a README whose
      title is followed only by a list and a heading (no paragraph) and one with no `README.md`,
      each giving "no summary in this project's README"; a failed read giving "summary not
      available"; and, at 360 px in both the light and the dark theme with the 600-character
      fixture, that the card shows exactly three lines of the summary with the fade at the
      cut, that the card's text is the summary's full text word for word with no ellipsis, no
      sideways overflow and nothing scrolling inside the card, that the summary control is
      present, in the tab order and named "Show the whole summary of <project name>", that a tap
      on it does not open the Project screen, that a tap, Enter and Space each open the bubble
      whose text equals the summary exactly and which lies wholly inside the viewport, and that
      its "Close" control, a tap outside, Escape and a second tap on the summary each close it
      with focus back on the summary control; for a one-line summary, that it shows whole with
      no fade and no control; with two cards in one grid row, one holding the 600-character
      fixture and one a one-line summary, at 360 px, that the two cards have equal heights and
      both summary areas take three lines' height; at 360 px and then 414 px, with a summary
      that runs past three lines at 360 px and fits in three at 414 px, that the fade and the
      control show at 360 px and are gone at 414 px; and that opening the summary bubble closes
      an open "Latest" bubble, and opening "Latest" by tap or by hover closes an open summary
      bubble;
    - **(b)** that the bubble's text equals AC37's line (v3) and AC10's headline (v2.5) exactly;
      that the line is not printed on the card; that the bubble opens on hover, on a tap and on
      keyboard focus, and closes on Escape, a second tap and a tap outside; that a tap on the
      control does not open the Project screen; that a bubble closed with Escape stays closed
      while focus stays on its control; and that at 360 px the bubble lies wholly inside the
      viewport, unclipped;
    - **(c)** two columns at 360 px and at 414 px, and H5's extremes without error or overflow
      (AC42's test);
    - **(d)** one fixture for each of the group rules above, the closed `re-scope` case among
      them; the groups' order; AC8's order within a group; the starting states; a closed group
      holding a waiting item, whose header says "1 waiting on you"; a closed group holding a
      project that can't be read; that opening and closing survive a reload through
      `desk-home-groups-v1`; and that failing storage gives the starting states with no error.

**The project screens, after the owner's trial-view notes** (added 2026-10-15)
*From notes 6 to 15 of the same record (decisions/questions/P-001-trial-view-notes-1.md), read as
its "How it is read" lines read them, and the owner's answer on note 7. Notes 10 to 13 and 15
change AC49, AC51, AC52 and AC53 in place; the two criteria below carry the rest. Where AC48 and
these differ, these govern.*

- AC55 [trace: V4 → AC55] — When the owner opens a project's Status screen in the sandbox, then a
  "Your last action" line, just above the bar that says whether anything waits on the owner, names
  the owner's most recent recorded action in that project, and "More detail" opens the Pipeline,
  Time asked and Agents views drawn in the new design inside the sandbox.
  - **"Your last action"** (note 6: "Right above that bar, can you record my last action or
    decision just so I can orient to it quickly").
    - **What it names:** the latest, by its time, of the owner's recorded actions in that project
      the desk reads:
      - **a card answer:** the dispatch log's `decision` entry (J2), in J9's decision words
        ("P-001: you answered dor-4 with build"), at that entry's `time`, linked to
        `decisions/<item>/<gate>-<card>.md`;
      - **a ruling:** a record `decisions/questions/<name>.md` whose line 1 starts `Ruling:`,
        or `Decision:` for a target-user profile approval (decisions/README.md), in its first
        line's words without that word (as AC52's "Done today"), at the time of its oldest
        commit (J2's history read, as AC20), linked to the file. Every other file there (a
        `-notes.md` file, a Critic's verdict such as
        `P-001-milestones-adoption-critic-1.md`) is not the owner's and is ignored, never shown
        (plan review round 1, B1).

      A record whose second line is a `Proxy:` line is marked "(proxy)": it was made at the
      owner's request, in the owner's words.
    - **Its form:** "Your last action: <words> · <time>", the time as J9 gives times, then the
      record's code. The words wrap.
    - **A long ruling line is clipped to three lines** (the owner's follow-up ruling,
      decisions/questions/P-001-design-lab-answers-followup.md, answer 2: "Desk clips long lines
      (Recommended)"; trigger: the owner's own record decisions/questions/P-001-design-lab-open.md,
      whose line 1 runs to about 1,800 characters). When a ruling's words take more
      than three lines at the line's width, they show their first
      three lines, faded at the cut, with the whole line one tap away, as AC54(a)'s summary does:
      - the cut never changes the words, only how many show: the page holds the whole line, word
        for word, nothing is shortened, reworded or given an ellipsis, and nothing scrolls inside
        the line. Whether it runs past three lines is judged at its width as drawn, and again when
        that width changes. The fade matches the background in both themes;
      - the time and the record's code still show, after the three lines and outside the
        button: they are not part of the control, and a tap on them does not open the bubble (C);
      - three lines or fewer: the words show whole, with no fade and no control, in the one-line
        form above, "Your last action: <words> · <time>" then the code, the time and code
        following the words on the same line as for a card answer (C, from 2026-10-16). A short
        line takes its own height; AC54's "every tile the same height" does not apply here (C);
      - the three lines are a control of their own, as AC54(a)'s are: a real button, in the tab
        order, opened by a tap, a click, or Enter or Space, with the accessible name "Show the
        whole of <record's code>" (C);
      - it opens a bubble holding line 1 only, the whole of it, word for word and never cut. Any
        "(proxy)" mark is shown outside the bubble, after the clipped words, never inside it (C).
        The bubble sits wholly inside the
        viewport at every width down to 360 px, placed above or below to fit, and clear of the
        project screens' bottom bar. It closes as AC56 requires of every sheet and bubble, and a
        second tap on the control also closes it. Only one bubble is open at a time;
      - **a bubble taller than the viewport** (C) is held to the viewport's height, less a margin,
        above the bottom bar, and scrolls up and down inside itself, never sideways. Its "Close"
        control stays in view while it scrolls. The tests use viewports of 360×740 and 414×896
        CSS pixels. AC54's "Latest" and whole-summary bubbles follow the same rule;
      - **while the bubble is open, a refresh leaves it as it is** (C; build review 1, B1): when
        a refresh would change the line, for example a poll bringing a new ruling whose time is
        not yet read, so that the line would show "not available yet", the open bubble stays on
        screen unchanged, at the same scroll position. It adds one plain line, "This has changed since you opened it. Close this to see the latest.", as a sheet says so when its subject leaves (AC56). It
        closes only by the owner's own ways (AC56's close rules, or a second tap on the control).
        Once it is closed, the line shows its new state;
      - the record is never edited: the desk writes nothing, and the clip is on display only.

      The same holds for each ruling row in AC52's "Done today", the hold included (C, draft
      5; build review round 2, R2-B1): while a row's whole-line bubble is open, a refresh that
      would remove or change that row leaves the bubble on screen, unchanged and at the same
      scroll position, with the same notice, "This has changed since you opened it. Close this to see the latest.".
      The named example is the day turning at midnight in `ownerTimeZone`, when the next poll
      would drop the row from the fold. It closes only by the owner's own ways; once the owner
      closes it, the fold shows its new state.
    - **The owner's merges are not in it:** no record the desk reads holds them (AC49; J1 reads
      only open pull requests). The reason is one tap away: "your merges aren't recorded in what
      the desk reads".
    - **"not recorded"** when no record gives an action. A v2.5 project shows "not recorded
      (operating model v2.5.1 records rulings in the ledger)", as AC20 does.
    - **Not yet read:** until every ruling's time in that project has arrived, the line shows
      "not available yet" rather than an older action that may not be the latest (AC41).
  - **"More detail", rebuilt in the new design** (note 7: "The more detail section on the bottom
    of that same page takes me back to an outdated version?"; the owner's answer: "Rebuild in new
    design (Recommended)").
    - **Changed by AC68 (trial-view notes 2, note 31, 2026-10-16):** for a v3 project, "More
      detail" no longer offers Pipeline; Time asked and Agents stay. A v2.5 project keeps its
      "Reduced view and waits" (AC68 says why).
    - Inside the sandbox, "More detail" opens three views, each drawn in the sandbox's shared
      look (AC48) under AC42's constraints, as a full screen in the project with its bottom bar
      and both back controls (AC56):
      - **Pipeline:** AC9's items (AC10's reduced view for a v2.5 project), with AC11's and
        AC12's waits;
      - **Time asked:** AC20's lists and summary line;
      - **Agents:** AC22's sessions and per-item timeline (it keeps its time axis) and AC23's
        usage and context figures.
    - **The same facts:** every value, word and "not recorded" those criteria give, from the same
      records, with nothing added or dropped. Only the look changes.
    - **No link from the sandbox to the accepted views.** Without `?sandbox` the accepted M1 and
      M2 views are exactly as they are (AC43), and AC36 still holds there.
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC55` checks:
    - "Your last action" for a fixture where a card answer is latest, one where a ruling is
      latest, a proxy ruling, a target-user profile approval, a project with neither ("not
      recorded"), a v2.5 project, and a project whose ruling times have not all arrived ("not
      available yet");
    - that a Critic's verdict file in `decisions/questions/` (line 1 `Verdict: FAIL`), newer than
      every ruling and card answer, is never named, and never shown in "Done today" (AC52);
    - that the line sits just above the waiting bar, with its link and code;
    - from 2026-10-16 (the follow-up ruling, answer 2): with the owner's real record
      decisions/questions/P-001-design-lab-open.md as the latest ruling, at 360×740 and 414×896,
      that "Your last action" shows the first three lines of its words, faded at the cut, then
      its time and code; that the control has its name and opens a bubble whose text equals the
      words exactly, from "11 Ruling: 1 I see no need…" to the end of line 1; that Escape, a tap
      outside, "Close" and a second tap each close it, with focus back on the control; that the
      bubble, where it is taller than the viewport, is held within it less a margin, clear of the
      bottom bar, scrolls up and down and never sideways, with "Close" in view at the top and
      after scrolling to the end; that the time and code are outside the control; and that a
      ruling of three lines or fewer shows whole, with no fade and no control;
    - that each rebuilt view, rendered from the fixtures AC9–AC12 and AC20–AC23's tests use,
      shows the same set of values and words as the accepted view does for those fixtures;
    - that no element of the sandbox links to an accepted M1 or M2 view.

- AC56 [trace: V1 → AC56] — When the owner moves about the sandbox, opens a sheet or bubble, and
  the desk refreshes, then a back control goes one screen back beside the one to Home, every sheet
  and bubble has a visible close control, and a refresh leaves the screen exactly as the owner left
  it.
  - **Back one screen** (note 8: "The back button takes you all the way back to the home page,
    please introduce a second back button that just brings you back 1 page if possible.").
    - Every screen but Home keeps its control to Home, and gains beside it a control that goes
      back one screen: to the screen the owner was on before, in the sandbox's own history of
      screens. Its accessible name is "Back" and the Home one's is "Home" (C).
    - Sheets and bubbles are not screens: opening one adds nothing to that history.
    - When there is no earlier screen in that history (the sandbox was opened on this screen),
      the control isn't shown (C).
  - **Every sheet and bubble can be closed** (note 9: "This popup screen has no easy way to just
    close out the pop up, please make sure that exist on every pop up").
    - This covers every pop-up in the sandbox: the response sheet, the pull request sheet, the
      summary sheet, the Working now sheet, the info screens, AC54's "Latest" bubble and AC54's
      whole-summary bubble on Home, and, from 2026-10-16, the whole-line bubbles of AC55's "Your
      last action" and AC52's "Done today".
    - Each has a visible close control at its top, with the accessible name "Close" and a touch
      target of at least 44 by 44 CSS pixels (C). Each also closes on a tap outside it and on
      Escape.
    - On closing, focus goes back to the control that opened it. For the "Latest" bubble that
      does not reopen it (AC54: closed with Escape, it stays closed while focus stays there).
      When a held whole-line bubble (AC55) closes and its control is gone, because the line or
      row now shows its new state, focus goes to the line or fold heading it belonged to: the
      "Your last action" line, or "Done today"'s header (C, draft 5).
  - **A refresh keeps the screen as the owner left it** (note 14: "it seems like every time the
    app refreshes it will revert popups in utilizing or it will close pages").
    - **A refresh is every redraw the owner did not cause** (plan review round 1, B2): the
      minute's poll; the sandbox's 15-second timer tick; a history read or any other read
      arriving; and a redraw with no new data at all.
    - **The 15-second tick updates only the text that depends on the time** ("time so far",
      "waiting 12 min", "last read 40 s ago" and the like), in place. It never redraws the whole
      view (this answers the plan review's open question; the sandbox's whole-view redraw every
      15 seconds is what this replaces).
    - Through it, these all stay as they were: the screen; any open sheet or bubble; the scroll
      position of the screen and of an open sheet; the focused control; anything typed in a text
      box, with its cursor; any picked choice; and every fold or group the owner opened or closed
      (AC54's groups, AC50's open phase, AC51's milestone details and standard milestones box,
      AC52's "Done today"; from 2026-10-16, AC57's folded phase boxes and AC57's and AC59's
      gathered standard milestones, and AC60's sprint lists; from draft 3, AC66's Context folds).
      The Timeline scrolls to Now only
      when the owner opens it (AC59), never on a refresh.
    - New data fills in around them. Only the owner closes what the owner opened.
    - **When the thing a sheet is about leaves** (its answer arrives, or a pull request closes),
      the sheet stays open and says so, in the tracker's words (AC52), until the owner closes it.
      What the owner typed stays.
    - Nothing reorders under the owner's thumb (AC40).
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC56` drives every kind of refresh with a fake clock and
    changed fixtures: the minute's poll, the 15-second tick, a history read arriving, and a redraw
    with no new data. It checks:
    - **back:** a path Home → Status → Queue → a sheet → Timeline; then "Back" twice gives Queue
      and then Status, "Home" gives Home, and no "Back" shows on a screen opened first;
    - **close:** for each sheet and bubble (from 2026-10-16 including both whole-line bubbles,
      "Your last action"'s and "Done today"'s), the close control is present at the top with its
      name, and each of the control, a tap outside and Escape closes it, with focus back on its
      opener;
    - **refresh,** in four steps, one per screen, since the states sit on different screens.
      In each step every kind of refresh above runs, and each state stays exactly as it was,
      with the changed data shown:
      1. Home: the Done group opened, the Active group closed, and a "Latest" bubble open;
         1b. Home again, the same groups, with a card's whole-summary bubble open in place of
         the "Latest" bubble (only one bubble is open at a time, AC54);
      2. Status: the screen scrolled, and focus on its "More detail" control;
         2b. Status again, with "Your last action"'s whole-line bubble open (from 2026-10-16);
         and, from draft 4, with that bubble open and scrolled part way, a poll bringing a new
         ruling whose time is still unread: the bubble stays open, unchanged and at the same
         scroll position, with the line "This has changed since you opened it. Close this to see the latest."; once
         the owner closes it, the line reads "not available yet", and then the new ruling once
         its time arrives;
      3. Queue: a response sheet open and scrolled, a choice picked, text typed with the cursor
         mid-text, and "Done today" opened; then a poll in which its item is answered: the sheet
         stays open, says so, and keeps its text;
         3b. Queue again, "Done today" opened, with a ruling row's whole-line bubble open and
         scrolled part way (from 2026-10-16); and, from draft 5, the same with the clock run past
         midnight in `ownerTimeZone` and a poll that drops the row: the bubble stays open,
         unchanged and at the same scroll position, with the notice; once the owner closes it,
         the fold shows its new state and focus is on "Done today"'s header. Step 2b's case
         likewise checks that focus goes to the "Your last action" line when the control is
         gone;
      4. Timeline: a milestone's detail and the standard milestones box opened. *From
         2026-10-16 (AC59, AC60):* the Timeline scrolled away from Now, one milestone's sprints
         opened and a "Not tied to a milestone" line's sprints opened: each stays as it was, and
         the screen does not scroll back to Now;
         4b. Status (AC57): one phase box folded and another opened from folded, and an earlier
         run's gathered standard milestones opened; each stays as it was, and the branch
         drawing's dots stay level with their phase names.

      It also checks that the 15-second tick changes only the time-dependent text: every other
      element of the view is the same element before and after it.

**The Status and Timeline screens, after the owner's trial-view notes 2** (added 2026-10-16)
*From the owner's notes of 2026-10-16, notes 1 to 27 of decisions/questions/P-001-trial-view-notes-2.md,
read as its "How it is read" lines read them, later readings replacing earlier ones where they say
so; and from the design the owner approved there, mockup version 6
(docs/design/mockups/status-and-timeline.html) and, for the Home tile,
docs/design/mockups/phase-branches.html. The owner's words are quoted exactly as written, typos
included. Where AC48, AC50 or AC51 and these differ, these govern. Every count, date and word
these screens show comes from a record the desk reads (J9, J10, J11, J12) or is one of the desk's
fixed labels quoted here; where a record is missing the screen says so (AC41). Nothing here is
built without `?sandbox`: the accepted M1 and M2 views stay exactly as they are (AC43).*

**Terms for these criteria:**
- **Run, on the branch drawing** (C): a column of phase runs. Take the model's timeline's phase
  runs in its order (J11). The first starts run 1. Each later phase run whose phase is the same as,
  or comes before, the phase of the run just before it starts a new run: the work went back. Every
  other phase run carries on in the run it follows. Runs are numbered from 1 in that order and
  shown as "Run <k>". For Service-Desk at 3117150 (J11's fixture): run 1 is Discovery i1, Design i1
  and Build i1; run 2 is Design i2, Build i2 and Launch i1.
- **Finished and standing:** a phase run before AC50's now run whose phase has no later run.
  At 3117150, Discovery i1.
- **Finished, then gone back over:** a phase run before the now run whose phase has a later run.
  At 3117150, Design i1 and Build i1.
- **Still to come:** a phase run after the now run. At 3117150, Build i2 and Launch i1.

- AC57 [trace: V4 → AC57] — When the owner opens a project's Status screen in the sandbox, then
  its phases read from the bottom up, Discovery at the bottom and Done at the top, each in its own
  box with its phase's colour, that folds on a tap and lists every run of that phase; one column of
  dots per run sits inside the boxes and branches right where the work went back; and every
  milestone of every run shows its state, in words, and its why line.
  - The owner's words:
    - note 1: "the app shows us back in a design iteration but also implies build is done?
      Chronologically that can’t be true", and its answer: "anywhere we have chronological phases
      like this, we should incorporate a branch style visual. By expanded out a second, third,
      fourth (however many are applicable) dot set to the right of the original milestone dot";
      on the mockup: "This is the exact picture I had in mind. Every box list it’s runs as well,
      but in smaller de emphasized text";
    - note 2: "when a phase was completed but branched off, make the dot fill lighter than a fully
      completed phase";
    - note 6 (the owner's point 4): "when we show the standard milestones that we DONT do for a
      particular project, it doesnt easily translate to what we DO take on - in chronologgical
      order. The other thing is that every milestone within the phases should have a short snippet
      of "why this phase was included or not included"";
    - notes 10 to 12: "maybe soften the text color for not utilized milestones and add a simple
      light directional line that ties the milestones/phases from the bottom to the top to
      indicate direction";
    - note 15: "I was always envisioning phases > milestones > tasks to read bottom up. So
      discovery would be at the bottom and the final thing done would be at the top.";
    - note 19: "reduce the weight on the line connecting things";
    - note 25: "Lets do B - simply because i like the ability to cleanly collapse and draw
      distinction between phases."
  - **Read from the bottom up** (note 15, replacing note 12's reading). From the top of the screen:
    Done, Launch, Build, Design, Discovery. Inside a box, the latest run sits at the top and the
    earlier runs below it. Inside a run, the first milestone sits at the bottom and the last at the
    top: the run's standard milestones at the bottom, in the model's order (`<Phase>-1` lowest),
    then the project's own milestones above them, in the order docs/PROJECT.md section 3 lists
    them.
  - **Each phase in its own box** (note 25, option B of note 20):
    - the box carries its phase's colour as a stripe down its left edge (note 13; AC48's phase
      colours), and its phase's name in words. Done's box is dashed with no stripe;
    - its header holds the phase's name and its state word (AC50, as changed by note 1): for
      Service-Desk at 3117150, Discovery "Finished", Design "Now · run 2", Build "Run 2 still to
      come", Launch "Not started", and Done "After the Launch gate" until the project is Done (C,
      the mockup's words);
    - the now phase's box keeps its place and is marked there, with a gold border and its state
      word (AC50's "emphasize it inline");
    - **it folds** (note 25): the phase's name is a real button, in the tab order, opened by a tap,
      a click, or Enter or Space, with the phase's name as its accessible name and its folded or
      open state as its accessible state and as a mark beside the name, never colour alone. It
      folds the box to its one header line and opens it again. Done has nothing to fold;
    - **how boxes start** (C, as the mockup draws them): open, except a phase whose every run is
      finished and standing (at 3117150, Discovery), which starts folded. Folding one box never
      moves or reorders any other, and a refresh keeps each box as the owner left it (AC56).
  - **The branch drawing** (notes 1, 2, 10, 16, 20 and 25):
    - **one column of dots per run** (Terms), run 1 furthest left and each later run one step to
      the right, at the same position across in every box. The dots sit inside the boxes, on the
      left (option B). Each dot is level with its box's phase name, centred on it (within 1 px),
      and stays so when any box folds or opens and when the width changes;
    - **which dots:** one for each phase run, in its run's column, in its phase's box. After the
      latest run's last phase run, that column carries on with a still-to-come dot in each later
      phase's box, through Done (C, as drawn: at 3117150, run 2 has dots at Design, Build, Launch
      and Done);
    - **each dot's mark, with its words in the run's line in the box (never colour alone):**
      - finished and standing: filled;
      - finished, then gone back over: a lighter fill with an outline (note 2), its run reading
        "finished, then gone back over";
      - the now run: a gold ring, when it holds the next milestone to reach (rule 2 below), so the
        ring means the same on a run as on a milestone. When AC50's now run comes from its second
        branch (every project milestone has work filed, so the now run is the one holding the
        latest recorded step), no milestone is next to reach: the now run's dot is then a
        half-filled circle, "now: work under way", and no gold ring shows (C; plan review round 2,
        N5);
      - still to come: an empty ring;
      - Done: an empty ring until the project is Done (AC50), then filled.

      With no now run (AC50's "not recorded"), every phase run's dot is a dashed ring and its words
      say "not recorded";
    - **the lines** (notes 12, 15 and 19): within a column, a thin, light line joins each dot to
      the next one up, crossing the gap between boxes, with a small arrowhead half way that points
      up. A line into a still-to-come dot is dashed. Where a run starts, one curve joins the last
      dot of the run before it to the new run's first dot, out to the right. There are no other
      slanted pieces. The lines are as thin and light as version 6 draws them after note 19: 1.25 px
      at 70% opacity (docs/design/mockups/status-and-timeline.html:581 and :587);
    - **each run's column** has its own shade (run 1 neutral, run 2 gold, as drawn), and is named
      in words on every run in the boxes ("Run 2"). The drawing itself is hidden from assistive
      technology: the words carry it. **A third or later run** (build review round 1, N4; C)
      takes a neutral-tinted shade that is none of AC48's 14 meaningful colours: run 3 a cool grey
      (light `#7b828a`, dark `#8d8d77`), run 4 a grey-olive (light `#7f8669`, dark `#c3d0c3`),
      and later runs these two in turn (run 5 as run 3, run 6 as run 4), their words telling them
      apart. Each is at least 10 by CIEDE2000 from all 14 colours and from the other in its theme
      (measured on 2026-10-16: lowest 17.7 light, 15.3 dark), and at least 3:1 in contrast against
      the page and card surfaces in its theme. AC48's test checks these four values against the 14
      and each other;
    - **a key** above the boxes, each entry with its mark (plan review round 2, B2): for the runs'
      dots, "finished · finished, then gone back over · now: holds the next milestone to reach ·
      still to come"; for the milestones, "reached · work under way · next to reach · no work
      filed yet · not reached · not taken on, or not recorded". The gold ring is named "next to
      reach" in both, never "now" alone.
  - **Every run in its box** (note 1's mockup answer 2):
    - each run opens with its tag, "Run <k>" (here and on every screen, <k> is the run in Terms
      above, the column, never the phase's iteration; plan review round 1, N4), then "<Phase> i<n> ·
      <state words>": "finished",
      "finished, then gone back over", "now", "still to come" or "not recorded" (for example
      "Run 1 · Build i1 · finished, then gone back over");
    - the latest run reads at full size; every earlier run is smaller and quieter: its text one
      role down on AC62's type scale and in the muted colour, never a size of its own.
  - **Every milestone of every run, in order** (note 6): the project milestones the model's
    timeline places in that run (J11), and every standard milestone of that phase, with its state
    for that run.
    - **Standard milestones:** a line "Standard milestones · <count by state>" ("4 reached · 4 not
      recorded"), then each one by name, its code after, and its state word: the model's (reached,
      not reached, not recorded, carried over or not needed), or "Skipped" where AC61's plan line
      says so. In the latest run of a phase every one is listed. In an earlier run those whose
      state is "not recorded" are gathered behind one line, "<n> not recorded, one tap away",
      which opens them in place on a tap and folds them on a second (C, as drawn for Build i1 and
      Design i1). Every count is a count of the model's lines.
    - **Project milestones:** each by its plain-English name, its code after, and its state words:
      "Work filed: <its sprints>" ("Work filed: PR #44, #52"), "No work filed yet" or "Dropped"
      (AC50), and, for the one milestone rule 2 below gives the gold ring, "· next to reach" after
      them ("No work filed yet · next to reach"; plan review round 2, B2). *Until 2026-10-17*
      never "reached" (AC50). From then a line carrying ` · reached:` (J11) reads "Reached
      <date>", then " · work filed: <its sprints>" when it lists any ("Reached 7 Oct · work
      filed: PR #40, #41"), and only such a line says "reached" (AC50). Its sprints are the
      plan's: a pull request that names the milestone only in its own `Milestone:` line (AC61
      (c)) never changes these words or its mark (AC60).
    - **Each milestone's mark, beside its words.** The rules are taken in this order, and the
      first that fits gives the mark (plan review round 1, N5), so no milestone fits two:
      1. skipped (AC61's plan line), not needed, or dropped: a dashed ring;
      2. **next to reach:** the gold ring, on one milestone only: the first, in section 3's order,
         of the now run's project milestones that are not dropped and have no work filed (at
         3117150, MD2; a milestone marked reached counts as work filed, AC50, from
         2026-10-17). This is AC59's meaning of the gold ring; the owner ruled it holds on every
         screen ("Next to reach, everywhere (Recommended)", 2026-10-16);
      3. any other milestone of a run still to come: an empty ring, its state word "Still to come"
         in place of the model's "not recorded" (C); from 2026-10-17 not a project milestone
         whose line carries ` · reached:`, which goes on to rule 5 (filled, "Reached <date>"),
         so its mark agrees with its words: the reached part is the owner's ruling, the record
         trial-view notes 2, question 4 (3), waits for ("unless a record says so") (C;
         plan review round 1, N8);
      4. reached or carried over, in a run gone back over, and a project milestone with work filed
         in a run gone back over: the lighter fill (note 2's dot, carried to milestones; its words
         never say "reached" for a project milestone, AC50, unless its line carries ` · reached:`,
         from 2026-10-17: then the lighter fill with "Reached <date>", as for a standard milestone
         reached in a run gone back over, so M1 in Build i1 once marked);
      5. reached or carried over: filled; from 2026-10-17 also a project milestone whose line
         carries ` · reached:`, in the now run, a run finished and standing, or a run still to
         come (rule 3);
      6. **work under way:** a project milestone with work filed and not marked reached, in the
         now run or a run finished and standing: a half-filled circle, as on the Timeline (the
         same ruling; the mockup's gold ring on MD1 is not carried);
      7. no work filed yet: an empty ring;
      8. not reached: filled red, with "Not reached";
      9. not recorded: a dashed ring.
    - **The tie line** (notes 12, 15, 19): a run's milestones are joined by a thin, light line with
      a small arrowhead at its top, pointing up: 1 px in the mockup's `--tie` grey, as version 6
      draws it (docs/design/mockups/status-and-timeline.html:87-88).
    - **Softer text** (note 11): a milestone whose mark comes from rule 1 or rule 9 above (skipped,
      not needed, dropped or not recorded) shows its name, code, state and why line in the softer
      text colour and a lighter weight. Its words still say which.
    - **Its why line** (note 6), for every milestone in every run, taken on or not: "why: <text>"
      from AC61's record, quoted as written, or "why: not recorded". It sits under the milestone's
      name in the quoted-record role (AC62), smaller in an earlier run.
  - **Kept from AC50:** no bar or track sized by a fraction of phases (AC41); "Phases not tracked"
    for a project with no phase records (a v2.5 project); "Done", never "Graduated"; the now
    run's rule; the info screen's numbered list of the six phases.
  - **Still and plain** (note 9's reading: the owner's snippet's "invented copy, its looping
    animation and its version badges are not carried over"; plan review round 1, B1): nothing on
    this screen loops or makes an entrance; nothing moves unless the owner acts (a fold opening, a
    sheet or bubble the owner opened, as AC54 to AC56 have them); there is no version badge and no
    text the records do not give. The mockups' pulsing "now" dot is not built (a known
    difference, "Where the build stands", trial-view notes 2). This holds on AC58's Home tile and
    on AC59's and AC60's Timeline too.
  - Every other part of the Status screen (the line under its title, "Your last action", the
    waiting bar, the queue and team buttons, "More detail") stays as AC48, AC55 and AC56 have it.
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC57` uses J11's fixture and checks:
    - the boxes' order from the top (Done, Launch, Build, Design, Discovery), each run's order in
      its box (latest first), and each run's milestones in the order above;
    - the runs: run 1 is Discovery i1, Design i1 and Build i1, and run 2 is Design i2, Build i2,
      Launch i1 and a still-to-come Done;
    - each dot's mark and words: Discovery i1 filled and "finished"; Design i1 and Build i1 the
      lighter fill and "finished, then gone back over"; Design i2 the gold ring and "now"; Build
      i2, Launch i1 and Done empty rings and "still to come";
    - the drawing's shapes: every straight piece vertical; one curve, from Build i1's dot to Design
      i2's; dashed pieces only into still-to-come dots; every arrowhead pointing up; each dot's
      centre level with its phase name's row (the CSS here, the browser's measure at 360 px and
      414 px in the pull request, as AC51's test does), and again after a box folds;
    - each header's state word, as listed above, and that no header reads "Finished" for Build;
    - folding: Discovery starts folded and the rest open; a tap, Enter and Space each fold and
      open a box, with its accessible state; no other box moves in order;
    - each box's stripe uses its phase's colour token, and its name is in words;
    - every standard milestone's state against the fixture's `NOTE:` lines, and the gathering of
      the "not recorded" ones in Design i1 and Build i1 but not in Design i2;
    - each milestone's mark by the ordered rules: MD2 alone has the gold ring, MD1 the half-filled
      circle, M1, M2 and M2B the lighter fill with "Work filed", Build i2's and Launch i1's
      milestones empty rings with "Still to come" (or "No work filed yet" for their project
      milestones), Design-6 in Design i1 filled red; and the softened text for rules 1 and 9;
    - MD2's words, "No work filed yet · next to reach", and the key's words for the gold ring,
      "now: holds the next milestone to reach" and "next to reach", with no key entry that names
      it "now" alone (plan review round 2, B2);
    - with AC61's fixture, where one pull request's body says `Milestone: MD3` and no plan line
      lists it, that MD3 still reads "No work filed yet" with an empty ring, and that the now run
      and MD2's gold ring are unchanged (B1);
    - a made-up project whose every project milestone has work filed: its now run's dot is the
      half-filled circle, "now: work under way", and no gold ring shows (N5);
    - the lines' width and opacity, and the tie line's, as cited above;
    - that no rule for these screens in `sandbox.css` sets an `animation` or a `transition` that
      runs without the owner acting, that none holds `infinite`, and that no element on them
      changes between two renders a minute apart with no new data (the fake clock);
    - "why: not recorded" on every milestone with J11's fixture, and each fixture why line, word
      for word, with AC61's fixture;
    - a made-up project of three runs (NOT a real record: the model's timeline lines for runs
      Discovery i1, Design i1, Build i1, Design i2, Build i2, Launch i1, Build i3 and Launch i2,
      with Build i3 now), giving three columns, run 3 branching right at Build;
    - from 2026-10-17, with J11's reached fixture (AC50's test): M1 in Build i1 the lighter fill
      with "Reached 7 Oct · work filed: PR #40, #41"; MD2, marked ` · reached: 2026-10-16` with
      no sprints, filled, "Reached 16 Oct" (the test's clock in 2026), and MD3 then the one gold
      ring; M3A in Build i2, a run still to come (J11's fixture, trace-notes.txt:139-145), its
      line ending ` · reached: 2026-10-16` in a copy of J11's reached fixture that AC57's test
      makes for this case alone, filled with "Reached 16 Oct", not "Still to come"; and the
      key's "reached" entry unchanged.

- AC58 [trace: V1 → AC58] — When the owner looks at a project's phases on its Home card, or on any
  other sandbox screen that lists the phases in order, then they show the same branch drawing as
  AC57, compact and read from the bottom up, with each phase's state in words.
  - **Changed by AC65 (note 28, draft 3, 2026-10-16):** on the Home card, each phase now sits in
    its own small box with its stripe and the circle icons, the row words are AC65's, a plain line
    says where the project is, and "<n> of 4 phases finished" goes. Where this criterion and AC65
    differ, AC65 governs; the branch drawing's columns, lines, curve and arrowheads, and "Where
    else", stay as below.
  - The owner's words: note 1, "anywhere we have chronological phases like this, we should
    incorporate a branch style visual"; note 15, as AC57 quotes it. The mockup's Home tile
    (docs/design/mockups/phase-branches.html, "The same on a Home tile") was drawn top down,
    before note 15; it is built bottom up (C).
  - **On the Home card, in place of AC50's checklist:** five rows, from the top Done, Launch,
    Build, Design and Discovery. Each row holds the phase's name and its state word exactly as
    AC57's box header has it, and nothing added to it: "Finished", "Now · run 2", "Run 2 still to
    come", "Not started", "After the Launch gate" (plan review round 1, N4). Beside them, AC57's
    columns, dots, marks, lines, arrowheads and curve, drawn smaller. No milestones are shown on
    the card.
  - **Kept from AC50:** the count in words, "<n> of 4 phases finished" (*removed by AC65 from draft
    3*), counting the phases whose state word is "Finished"; the
    now phase's gold mark and word; the "DONE" stamp; and "Phases not tracked" with empty rows.
    No bar.
  - **Where else:** these two are the screens that list phases in order today. The Timeline shows

      marked reached whose sprints have no date takes its reached date here, so it sits
      below Now (C, reading note 21's "chronological order of delivering those milestones":
      with no sprint dated, the owner's acceptance is the one dated record of its delivery). One
      whose sprints have dates keeps its latest sprint's date: the reached date records when
      the owner accepted the work, not when it was delivered, so marking a milestone reached
      never moves it;

      above the Now line.

    - **not recorded** (rule 9): a question mark in a dashed circle;

    badges are not carried over; the feed shows only the project's real records." So every sprint

    the owner acts, and no sprint carries a version badge (AC57's "Still and plain"; plan review
    round 1, B1).
  - **What a sprint is** (note 24): what a project milestone's `· sprints:` list in docs/PROJECT.md
    section 3 names (J11): a pull request (`PR #<n>`) or a pipeline item (a research run such as
    `Q-013`, or a `P-` or `E-` item), placed as the model's timeline places it. Also:
    - the sprints the model's timeline lists under `untagged` in a run (J11);
    - a merged pull request whose body's `Milestone:` line (AC61 (c)) names a milestone that the
      plan's `· sprints:` lists do not tie it to (plan review round 2, B1). Only merged ones are
      placed this way; an open one is in the Queue (AC52), as other open pull requests are (build
      review round 1, N6a). It is shown under each
      milestone it names, apart from the plan's sprints, in its own folded line, "<n> named by
      its pull request, not yet in the plan" ("1 named by its pull request, not yet in the
      plan"). **It changes nothing about that milestone:** the milestone's state, words, mark or
      icon (AC57, AC59), its place in the delivery order (AC59), AC50's now run and the next
      milestone to reach all stay as the plan and the model's timeline give them, because the
      model's timeline counts only the plan's lists (S-032 leaves it unchanged). Filing it in the
      plan's list is what makes it work filed. A project milestone it names sits where the model's
      timeline places that milestone; a standard milestone it names (`Milestone: Design-4`) sits
      in the run its merge time places it in, by the rule below, and that standard milestone then
      gets a row on the Timeline (AC59), keeping its own state ("not recorded" stays "not
      recorded"). An id that section 3 and the model's standard milestones do not hold places
      nothing: the pull request is untied, and its Technical bullet says "names <id>, which the
      plan does not hold" (C). Such a pull request is not counted under "Not tied to a
      milestone";
    - from J12, each pull request merged into the default branch that no milestone's list names
      and whose body names no milestone. It sits in the latest phase run, in the timeline's order,
      whose earliest time (AC59's "a run's times") is at or before its merge time, or in the first
      run when none is (C). An open pull request is not placed: it is in the Queue (AC52).
  - **Never a sprint: the asks-merge job's merges** (from 2026-10-17, the owner's answer
    "B. Off the Timeline (Recommended)", decisions/questions/P-001-asks-merge-on-desk.md: "once merged, the asks-merge job's pull
    requests no longer show as work on the desk's Timeline; while open, they still show as
    waiting on the owner"). A merged pull request that J12 marks as the job's (its merge
    commit's final trailer paragraph holds one `Merged-By: orchestrator`, and its author is

    - not placed by its `Milestone:` line, so no "named by its pull request, not yet in the plan"

    - not gathered under "Not tied to a milestone", and not counted in that line's "<n> sprints"
      or "for the agents to file" (AC69), so a run whose only untied pull requests are the job's

    - no part of a run's times (AC59).

    **Except where the plan lists it:** a plan line whose `· sprints:` names one still lists it,

    sprint's does (AC59) (C; plan review round 4, note 5); none does (docs/PROJECT.md section 3 lists PR #40, #41, #44,
    #52, #72, #74, #75 and #77 only). While open it is in the Queue as any pull request is (Terms,
    AC2, AC52), unchanged. The questions and answers it carried show in the Queue and in "Done
    today" from their own records (AC3, AC52, AC66), unchanged. A merge J12 has not yet read (its
    read failed, or it is older than the 300 kept) is placed as the rules above place any pull
    request J12 lacks: under a plan line that lists it, shown as J12's awkward cases show a
    listed pull request it lacks, and nowhere else, since its `Milestone:` line and merge time come from J12 (C).
  - **Folded under each milestone** (note 21: folded, one tap away): a line "<n> sprints feed
    this", "1 sprint feeds this", or "No sprints filed yet" ("No sprints planned yet" for a planned
    milestone). It is a real disclosure control, opened and closed by a tap, Enter or Space, with
    its open state as its accessible state. Every list starts folded, and stays as the owner left

  - **"Not tied to a milestone"** (note 21): in each run that has such sprints, one line with the
    slate filled diamond, the words "Not tied to a milestone" and "<n> sprints · for the agents to
    file" (slate, not coral, from draft 4: note 32, AC69), the fixed
    sentence "No milestone in the plan lists these sprints, and they name none." (C; plan review
