  - **Each sprint, top to bottom** (as drawn):
    - **its first line:** its date; its code ("PR #77", "Q-013") in the code role; and its marks
      on the right (note 8). The date, in `ownerTimeZone`: a pull request's merge date (J12); for
      a pipeline item, the date of its latest step in the model's timeline; otherwise "date not
      recorded";
    - **its marks,** each a mark and a word, never colour alone:
      - for a pull request: **merged** ("✓ merged" when J12 gives a merge time; "open"; "closed,
        not merged"); **review** ("✓ review" when the default branch's tree holds verdict files
        under `reviews/<n>/` and each decides PASS by reviews/README.md's rule, the first `Verdict:`
        at column 0, files whose names start `_` and README.md not counted; "✕ review" when one
        decides FAIL; "– review" with "review not recorded" when the tree holds none); **checks**
        ("✓ checks", "✕ checks" or "… checks" from J12's check state for the pull request's last
        commit: success; failure or error; pending; "– checks" with "checks not recorded" when J12
        gives none, and the reason one tap away: "the desk's token can't read checks" when J12's
        answer holds an error for that field (J12; plan review round 1, N7), otherwise "no checks
        recorded for this pull request");
      - for a pipeline item: **merged** ("✓ merged" when `status/merges.jsonl` holds the item, "–
        merged" with "not merged" otherwise); and, for a research run, **source-checked** ("✓
        source-checked" or "✕ source-checked" by its latest `source-checker` outcome's verdict,
        J9; "– source check not recorded" when none);
    - **its title** (note 8), in the item-name role:
      - a pull request: **its own title, shown as written**, character for character, never
        reworded, shortened or given words of its own (the owner's answer of 2026-10-16, "The PR's
        own title (Recommended)": "Says what was done, like the mockup did."). For pull request

      - a research run: "Research: " and the first sentence of its request's `question`
        (`intake/requests/<id>.toml`, J11): its text up to and including the first `.`, `?` or `!`
        followed by a space or the text's end, as written (C);
      - another pipeline item: J9's headline words for it, or "title not recorded";

      The title wraps whole: it is never cut;
    - **three bullets, in this order** (notes 8 and 14), each with its label, "Technical",
      "Context" or "Why", in its own colour (AC48's bullet colours: slate, dusty rose, muted violet) on the
      label and down the bullet's left edge, so the label says what colour alone does not:
      - **Technical** (note 17, "The phase and milestone move into Technical"; AC64's "one step
        down"): the phase run and the milestone it feeds ("Design i2 · MD1", or "Design i2 · not

        none. A pipeline item has no form for it yet, so it reads "not recorded" (C);
      - **Why:** AC61's `## Why` section, as written, in the quoted-record role; "not recorded"

    - **"Tasks in this sprint →"** (note 24), its last line, a link:
      - a pull request: its commits on GitHub, `https://github.com/<repo>/pull/<n>/commits`, with

    - the placing of untied merged pull requests by merge time, with one merged before every run,
      and that an open pull request is not placed;
    - that each list starts folded and opens and folds by a tap, Enter and Space;
    - each sprint's date, code, marks and words, a review that passed, one that failed and one not
      recorded, and each checks state with "checks not recorded";
    - each pull request's title against J12's fixture, character for character (#77's, and #40's,
      which starts with a code), and a research run's title;
    - a pull request placed by its `Milestone:` line alone (a project milestone, MD3, a standard
      one, an id the plan does not hold): shown in "1 named by its pull request, not yet in the
      plan" under MD3, with MD3's state, words, gold ring or other mark, delivery order, the now
      run and the next milestone to reach all as they are without it (B1), and the standard
      milestone's row keeping "not recorded"; one with `Milestone: none (<reason>)` under "Not
      tied to a milestone" with its reason and outside the "for the agents to file" count; and the
      fixed
      sentence;
    - the checks mark's reason when J12's fixture holds the made-up field error;
    - from 2026-10-17 (the owner's answer on the asks-merge job's merges), with J12's fixture for
      them: #84's merge (real-on-real, from 21bc250, its body `Milestone: none (…)`) on no
      milestone and under no "Not tied to a milestone" line, and giving its run no time; a
      made-up merge by the job whose body names MD3, not under MD3's "not yet in the plan" line;
      a made-up merge by the job that a made-up plan line lists under MD3, shown as MD3's sprint
      with its merge time among its run's times (the plan wins);
      a run whose only untied pull requests are the job's showing no "Not tied to a milestone"
      line; and each of J12's not-the-job's cases (a description quoting the line mid-text, the
      trailer with another author, another value) placed as any merged pull request;
    - that nothing on the Timeline loops, makes an entrance or moves without the owner acting
      (AC57's "Still and plain", the same CSS and fake-clock checks), and that it shows no version
      badge;
    - each bullet's label, colour token and order, the Technical words for a pull request and a
      research run, and "not recorded" for Context and Why with no section;
    - the tasks link for a pull request, a pipeline item with a merge line, and one without;
    - that no sprint shows a task or step of its own on the Timeline.

- AC61 [trace: V4 → AC61] — When a project's records carry why lines and a sprint's Context and
  Why, then the desk shows them on AC57, AC59 and AC60 exactly as written; and until a record
  carries them, the desk shows "not recorded" and never a why or a fact of its own.
  - The owner's answer on note 6: "A line in the project's plan (Recommended)" (option text: "Each
    milestone in the project's plan (docs/PROJECT.md) gets a short "why" line that the agents write
    when they plan it, and you can edit it. The standard milestones a project skips get one too.
    Until a line exists, the desk shows "why: not recorded". This is also a small change to the
    operating model, so every project does it."). Note 27's reading: "The agents write the
    Context facts into each sprint's record when they file it, and the desk only shows them".
  - **The forms: those of model S-032, frozen** ("What this spec does not cover";
    Personal-Org-Operating-Model specs/S-032-why-and-context.md at c816cec: AC4's why, AC5's
    standard lines, AC6's `Milestone:` line, Context and Why, and its joins J1 and J2 to the
    desk). S-032 froze on 2026-10-16 (24987db; merged at c816cec), and Service-Desk adopted it
    with model v3.4.0 on 2026-10-17 (L-0015). Until then these forms were those of S-032's
    draft 1 of 2026-10-16 (7108d1d). For a body in S-032's form, the desk reads what S-032's
    forms say. A body out of that form is read as "Where the desk differs from S-032" says,
    below (the desk follow-ups, 2026-10-17). The desk reads exactly these (plan review round 1,
    N1):
    - **(a) a project milestone's why:** a milestone line in docs/PROJECT.md section 3 may end
      with ` · why: <reason>`, after any ` · sprints:` and ` · dropped`, the reason at least one
      character, starting and ending with a non-space character and holding no ` · ` (S-032 AC4).
      The desk takes it off before it reads the rest of the line in J11's form, so a why never
      changes the milestone's name, "done when", phase, sprints or `· dropped` mark. A line whose
      why is empty, holds ` · `, or has another part after it (such as ` · dropped`) is out of
      form, as S-032's reader has it: skipped with J11's note, the rest still shown. Today the
      desk's reader fails every line with a why (src/lib/j11.js:161-163); from this change it
      reads them;
    - **(b) a standard milestone's line:** section 3 may hold one line per standard milestone,
      after an optional `- `: `<Phase>-<n> (standard) · taken on · why: <reason>` or
      `<Phase>-<n> (standard) · skipped · why: <reason>`, where `<Phase>-<n>` is one of the
      model's standard milestones (AC50; for example `Build-4`), and the reason is as in (a)
      (S-032 AC5). It holds for every run of that phase. The model's timeline is unchanged by it
      (S-032 AC5: a standard milestone's state still comes from its records alone), so the desk
      shows the line as follows: "taken on" leaves the shown state as it is; "skipped" shows
      "Skipped" where the model's state is not recorded or not needed, and otherwise the model's
      word with "planned as skipped" after it (C). A line that starts `<Phase>-<n> (standard)` but
      is not in this form, or names no standard milestone, is skipped with a note (J11's awkward
      case), and its milestone shows "not recorded" for its why. When two lines name the same
      milestone, the first counts and the second is noted;
    - **(c) a sprint's `Milestone:` line, Context and Why** (S-032 AC6), in a pull request's body
      after its `## What this means for you` paragraph:
      - **`Milestone:`** a line `Milestone: <ids>`, the ids one or more project milestone ids
        (`M<id>`) or standard ids (`Design-4`), separated by `, `; or `Milestone: none
        (<reason>)`. The first such line after the paragraph counts. AC60 places the sprint by it;
      - **the headings:** a `Context` heading and a `Why` heading, each at any level (`#` to
        `######`), with or without a colon after the word, case ignored. Each section runs from its
        heading to the next heading of any level, or the body's end; the first of each counts;
      - **Context** is a list of bullets. A bullet starts `- `; any indented line under it continues
        it, joined with one space. Each bullet, so joined, ends `(source: <record>)`: the source is
        the text inside the bullet's last ` (source: ` and its closing `)` at the end; the fact is
        the rest. The one exception bullet, `- none beyond the ruling quoted in Why` (case ignored,
        a final full stop allowed), is shown as written, with no source line. Any other bullet not
        in that form is shown as written with "source not recorded" (C, where S-032's check would
        fail it); a line in the section that is no bullet is not shown;
      - **Why** is the section's text, shown as written, its paragraphs kept, Markdown shown as
        plain text as AC54(a) reads a README.
    - **Where the desk differs from S-032** (rewritten 2026-10-17, the desk follow-ups; S-032's
      J2 now ties a merged pull request through the plan's `· sprints:` lists, as AC60 does, so
      that difference is gone). The desk checks no form; S-032's `pr-body` check does that in the
      project's own CI, here from model v3.4.0. So the desk shows bodies that check would fail:
      - **display leniencies, named:**
        - it accepts closing `#`s on a Context or Why heading (`## Context ##`);
        - it accepts no space after `Milestone:` (`Milestone:M2`);
        - it reads the `Milestone:` line, Context and Why in any order;
        - it shows a Context bullet without a source with "source not recorded";
      - **any other body the check fails is read as (c) reads it** (plan review round 1 of the
        desk follow-ups, B1). For example:
        - a plain line in Context is not shown;
        - an indented line after a blank line in Context is not shown;
        - `Milestone: Design-9` is read as the id `Design-9`, an id the plan does not hold;
        - a bare `Milestone: none` is read as the id `none`;
        - `(source: none)` shows the source "none";
        - the exception bullet beside another bullet is shown, with the other bullet;
        - the exception bullet with a Why that quotes nothing is shown as written;
        - a Why of only "TBD" shows "TBD";
      - **outside the bodies:** the desk reads research runs' Context and Why as "not recorded",
        since S-032 leaves them for later (its "out of scope");
      - **known limits, contrived, with no change to the desk** (S-032's build review round 1,
        N1 and N2, passed to this spec):
        - S-020's lead check accepts a lead heading written `##What this means for you`, with no
          space after the `#`s. The desk's reader needs the space, so it finds no lead and reads
          the body from the top. A lead line that starts `Milestone:` could then be taken as the
          milestone;
        - a "done when" holding ` · why: `, as in `… · done when x · why: y · phase: Build`.
          S-032's reader takes it as a line with no why. The desk's reader takes the first
          ` · why:` as the why, finds a part after it, and skips the line as out of form (a), with
          J11's note.

      A `Milestone:` line the plan does not back is shown but changes no state (AC60), as in
      S-032's J2.
  - **Where they are read:** (a) and (b) from docs/PROJECT.md on the default branch (J11);
    (c) from the pull request's body (kept on the device only as the parts the desk shows, J12): an open one's from J1's open list, which already holds it,
    and every other's from J12.
  - **Shown as written:** nothing is reworded, shortened or added. A why, a fact or a source that
    is empty shows "not recorded". The desk writes nothing to any record.
  - **Until the records carry them:** no connected project's records held these forms on
    2026-10-16. So on the preview the sandbox shows "why: not recorded" on Status, a flagged "Why
    not recorded yet" on the Timeline, and "not recorded" for each sprint's Context and Why until
    a record carries them. Service-Desk adopted S-032 on 2026-10-17 (L-0015). So its pull requests
    carry the `Milestone:` line, Context and Why from then on. Its plan's lines gain whys only when
    next drafted or reworded (S-032's "Adoption by projects"). A v2.5 project shows none of this
    (AC57, AC59).
  - **S-032 froze with these forms** on 2026-10-16 (24987db; merged at c816cec). Service-Desk
    adopted it on 2026-10-17 (L-0015; the desk follow-ups). This criterion follows the frozen
    spec. A later change to S-032's forms would be a new model spec, and this criterion
    would be revised to match before the Builder builds against it (C).
  - **The fixture** (NOT real records, written in the forms above, and listed so in
    `tests/desk/fixtures/README.md`): a copy of J11's docs/PROJECT.md with why lines on M2B and MD1,
    a standard line `- Build-4 (standard) · skipped · why: the desk runs no experiments; each
    change is checked by its own tests` (the mockup's made-up example), one for `Discovery-1`
    taken on, one out of form, one repeated, a why holding ` · ` and a why with ` · dropped`
    after it; and pull request bodies with a `Milestone:` line and both sections as `## Context`
    and `## Why`, one with `### Context:` and `why` as headings, one with `Milestone: Design-4`,
    one with `Milestone: none (<reason>)`, one with the exception bullet and a quoted Why, one
    with a bullet missing its source, one with an indented continuation line, and one with none
    of these.
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC61` checks each case above: the why text word for word on
    Status and Timeline; that a line with a why keeps its name, "done when", phase, sprints and
    dropped mark; "Skipped" and "planned as skipped"; the notes for the out-of-form and repeated
    lines and the two out-of-form whys; the `Milestone:` line read for each id kind; both heading
    forms; each Context fact and source, the exception bullet, the "source not recorded" case, the
    joined continuation line, and the Why text; and "not recorded" for each with J11's fixture as
    it is. From 2026-10-17 (the desk follow-ups) it also checks three of the display leniencies:
    a heading with closing `#`s, `Milestone:M2` with no space, and Why before Context. The two
    known limits are not pinned, since a later fix may change them (C).

- AC62 [trace: V1 → AC62] — When the owner reads any sandbox screen, then its spacing comes from
  one scale and its text from one type scale whose roles each look different, and every word the
  screen showed before is still there.
  - The owner's words: note 3, "the general spacing of things. I dont hate it, but there are spots
    that seem too spaced, and then some spots that seem too tight."; note 5, "There is a lot of
    text throughout the service desk - which i want specifically - however if all of the text is
    formatted almost identically it all kind of blurs together."
  - **One spacing scale** (note 3): 4, 8, 12, 20 and 32 px, as five tokens in `sandbox.css`, the
    mockup's `--s1` to `--s5` (docs/design/mockups/status-and-timeline.html:17). Every margin,
    padding and gap that sets the space between or around elements on every sandbox screen is 0 or
    one of the five, used so that related things sit close and separate groups sit further apart:
    4 and 8 inside an item, 12 between items of a group, 20 between groups, 32 between a screen's
    sections (C, as the mockup uses them). The only other values (C), each with a comment beside it
    in `sandbox.css` naming why, are: the page's 16 px side gutter with the safe areas (as the
    mockup draws it); the room kept for the bottom bar (`--sb-dock-h`) and the safe areas; the
    offsets that put a dot, icon or circle on its line; the 44 px touch targets (AC56); borders;
    a label chip's or tag's inner padding (as drawn, 1 px by 6 px: the mockup's `.bul .k` and
    `.bkey span`, :201 and :229); and the gap of 2 to 6 px between a mark or icon and its word
    inside one label, key entry or bullet (the mockup's `.key span` and `.bul li`, :56 and :200)
    (plan review round 1, N3). Every other spacing value in the mockups gives way to the scale.
  - **One type scale with distinct roles** (note 5): seven roles, each with its own look, their
    values the mockup's `--t-*` tokens (docs/design/mockups/status-and-timeline.html:19-25):
    - **screen title:** each screen's heading;
    - **section label:** small, upper case and spaced: group and fold headers, band names, run
      tags, the labels "Done when", "Why", "Technical" and "Context";
    - **item name:** the names of projects, phases, milestones and members, and sprint titles;
    - **body:** sentences;
    - **meta:** state words, times, dates, counts, sources and keys;
    - **quoted record text:** words quoted from a record: rulings, summaries, why lines, the
      Purpose quotes, a sprint's Why;
    - **codes:** ids such as `MD2` and `PR #61`, in monospace (AC48).

    Every font set in `sandbox.css` uses one of the seven. A role may change its colour, and an
    earlier run or a softened milestone may take the next role down (AC57), but nothing takes a
    size of its own: the mockup's own sizes (such as the sprint title's 1.04rem, :198, or the phase
    name's 1.12rem, :72) give way to the roles. The exceptions: AC63's text boxes, at least 16 px;
    and marks drawn as text, which are icons, not text roles (the ◆, ✓, ✕ and – glyphs, and
    `.sb-badge`'s count, sized to their icon, N3).
  - **Every word kept** (note 5: "which i want specifically"): on every sandbox screen other than
    the parts AC57 to AC60 and AC65 to AC68 rebuild or add to (the Status screen's phase list,
    Home's phase checklist, the Timeline, the Queue's rows and group icons, the Team screen's
    cards, the phase marker, "More detail"'s Pipeline link, the lines AC69 to AC72 add or
    reword, and, from draft 8 (build review round 1 of model 3.5.0's records, (b)), a v3
    project's Chief of Staff page's status line, Responsible-for groups and running log (AC49,
    J13)), the text is the same, word for
    word, before and after. Only the look changes.
  - Judged on the phone by the owner too: the sandbox pull request shows each screen at 360 px in
    the light and dark themes.
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC62` reads `sandbox.css`, as AC51's and AC54's tests do, and
    checks that every margin, padding, gap, row-gap and column-gap is 0, one of the five tokens,
    or a value with its comment; that every `font` and `font-size` uses a role's token, the text
    box rule excepted; and that the seven tokens differ from each other. It renders every sandbox
    screen from the fixtures, and checks each screen's text, outside the rebuilt parts, against a
    snapshot taken from the same fixtures at commit 2c1acb6 (committed under
    `tests/desk/fixtures/`).

    1. **the item's own `Milestone:` line**, in AC61 (c)'s form, in the question file
       (`questions/<name>.md`) or the card (`queue/<item>-<gate>-<n>.md`): its milestones, by
       plain-English name with the code after. S-032 added this line to pull requests only
       (AC61); model S-033 added it to questions (its AC2) and cards (its AC5), adopted here with
       model v3.5.0 (L-0016, 2026-10-17). Where each is read is J2's: for a question, its first
       `Milestone:` line in column 0; for a card, the line directly under its `answer:` line. A
       question or card written before the adoption has none;
    2. **for a card or gate card, its item:** when the plan's `· sprints:` lists name the card's
       item (for example `Q-013` under MD1), that milestone;
    3. **for a pull request in Merges:** the milestones whose plan lists name it; or, failing
       those, its body's `Milestone:` line, shown with "named by its pull request, not yet in the
       plan" as AC60 has it;
    4. otherwise "Unblocks: milestone not recorded".

    A `Milestone: none (<reason>)` line shows "Unblocks no milestone: <reason>".
  - **The milestone's why**, beside it: AC61 (a)'s why line, as written, with its source, or, for
    a standard milestone, AC61 (b)'s; otherwise AC59's slate "Why not recorded yet · for the agents
    to add" (AC69). With no
    milestone, no why is shown.
  - **"Context · the facts behind this ask"**, a fold under the row, closed to start, opened and
    closed by a tap, Enter or Space (AC56 keeps it through a refresh): the item's own Context
    facts, each with its source on its own line:
    - a question or card: its `## Context` section, in AC61 (c)'s form (model S-033 AC2 and AC5;
      J2 says where each starts and ends); a pull request: its body's Context (AC61 (c));
    - otherwise "Context: not recorded". The desk writes no fact of its own: the mockup's fact for
      the design-lab questions was drafted for the mockup and is not carried.
  - **Neutral group icons:** each group's header and rows lose their colours. A group holding
    anything that waits on the owner shows the coral diamond, "needs you", beside its title; every
    other group shows its own neutral mark in the muted grey (as drawn: ▲ Gate rulings, ✎
    Verdicts to write, ⇄ Merges, ▸ Deploys), and "Done today" the check. The groups are still told
    apart by their titles and marks, never colour (AC38, AC42).
  - **"Done today" links to the Timeline:** each row has a link, "On the Timeline", that opens the
    Timeline at the sprint the row belongs to, its milestone's sprints opened and the sprint in
    view:
    - a pull request: its own sprint (AC60). From 2026-10-17 one J12 marks as the asks-merge
      job's merge has none (AC60's "never a sprint"), so no link, with "not on the Timeline: merged
      by the automatic job", unless a plan line lists it: then the plan wins (AC60), and its row
      links to that sprint as any listed sprint's row does. The question or answer it carried
      keeps its own row and link. Until J12 has read that merge, the desk can't tell it is the
      job's. If a plan line lists it, its sprint is on the Timeline already (shown as J12's
      awkward cases show a listed pull request it lacks) and the row links there; otherwise no Timeline row holds it
      (AC60 places other merged pull requests from J12), so its row reads as any such row: no
      link, and "sprint: not recorded" (C);
    - a card answered: its item, when the item is a sprint on the Timeline;
    - a ruling: the milestone its `Milestone:` line names, at that milestone. From 2026-10-17
      the line is read, the first that gives one, from: the answer record's first `Milestone:`
      line in column 0 after line 1 (decisions/README.md:43; model S-033's J1), so a ruling
      whose question file has been tidied away still links; for an answer with no such line (one
      made through the accepted views' answer link, in J3's `Ruling: <letter>` and
      `Question: questions/<name>.md` form, or one written before the adoption), the named
      question file while the tree still holds it (J2); otherwise "sprint: not recorded" (a
      known limit, J2);
    - otherwise no link, and "sprint: not recorded".
  - The owner's tile on the Team screen and the phase marker read the same "Unblocks" (AC67, AC68),
    so the screens never disagree.
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC66` uses AC52's two-project fixtures with added records (NOT
    real records): a question with a `Milestone:` line and a `Context` section, one with
    `Milestone: none (<reason>)`, a card on an item the plan's lists name, a pull request named in
    a plan list and one named only by its body, and an item with none. It checks each "Unblocks"
    by the order above, each why and its flag, each Context fold and "Context: not recorded", the
    neutral marks and the coral diamond only on groups holding a waiting item (the CSS: no
    group or row rule uses a colour token other than the muted grey and `--you`), each "Done
    today" link's target and "sprint: not recorded", and, with the question file as it was filed, its frozen copy
    tests/desk/fixtures/trial-view/P-001-design-lab-open.md (L-0016 removed
    questions/P-001-design-lab-open.md), "Unblocks: milestone not recorded". From
    2026-10-17 it uses J2's S-033 fixtures (a question file `governance_checks questions` passes,
    a card written by `card_text` with a milestone and one without, and answer records in each
    form) and checks: "Unblocks" and the Context fold from the question's own lines; a card's
    from the line under `answer:` and its `## Context` before `## 8.`; a card with no
    `Milestone:` line falling to its item; and "Done today"'s link for a ruling from its answer
    record's `Milestone:` line with no question file in the tree, for an answer made through the
    accepted views' link from its question file while the tree holds it, and "sprint: not
    recorded" once it does not. From 2026-10-17 (the owner's answer on the asks-merge job's
    merges; plan review round 4, finding 1) it also uses J12's fixture for the job's merges and
    checks a job's merge seen leaving the queue (open in J1's list on one poll, gone on the next,
    with the page open): before J12 has read the merge, its "Done today" row reads "no longer
    open, time not recorded" with no link and "sprint: not recorded"; once J12's read on the head
    change marks it the job's, the same row has no Timeline link and reads "not on the Timeline:
    merged by the automatic job"; the question or answer it carried keeps its own row and link
    throughout; a merge J12 marks as not the job's (the description quoting the line
    mid-text) links to its sprint as before; and, from draft 7 (plan review round 6, note 2), a
    made-up job's merge that a made-up plan line lists under MD3, once J12 marks it the job's,
    has a "Done today" row that links to its sprint under MD3.

- AC67 [trace: V4 → AC67] — When the owner opens a project's Team screen in the sandbox, then each
  member's card shows its state with the Timeline's circle icons, the last sprint the member
  worked on and the milestone it feeds, each from a record, with a link into the Timeline at that
  sprint.
  - The owner's pick: "Team: what each is on", whose option text says: "Each agent card names the
    sprint it's working on and the milestone it feeds, with a link into the Timeline. Status shown
    with the same circle icons as the Timeline instead of bare dots."
  - **The state icon**, in a plain circle before the member's name, with AC49's status word beside
    it (a key above the cards names each, as drawn):
    - "Working": the half-filled circle (work under way);
    - "Just finished": a check (C: the mockup's key has no mark for it);
    - "Idle": an empty dashed circle;
    - "Waiting on you" (the owner's own tile): the coral diamond;
    - "not recorded", and the Chief of Staff's "No session recorded yet" (from 2026-10-17, J13;
      until then "Its sessions aren't recorded"): the question mark in a dashed circle.

    It replaces AC49's light, and the light's glow goes with it: nothing on the card moves.
  - **"last:"**, the member's latest recorded work: the item and J9's outcome words for the
    member's latest outcome entry, and its date in `ownerTimeZone` (for example "last: Q-013 …,
    8 Oct", in J9's words); "not recorded in what the desk reads" when no record names it (AC49).
  - **"feeds"**: the milestone that item feeds as a sprint, from the plan's `· sprints:` lists (as
    AC60 places it): "feeds <code> · <plain-English name>"; "feeds: not tied to a milestone" when
    no list names the item (P-001, the project item itself, is no sprint).
  - **"On the Timeline ›"**, when the item is a sprint on the Timeline: it opens the Timeline at
    that sprint, its milestone's sprints opened and the sprint in view. No link otherwise.
  - **The owner's tile** (AC49): "You · waiting on you: <n> in your queue" with the coral diamond,
    then its next lines, each with "· unblocks <code>" from AC66's "Unblocks", or "· unblocks:
    milestone not recorded".
  - Members whose work runs outside the pipeline (the Builder since the re-scope) keep AC49's "not
    recorded in what the desk reads"; the member's page (AC49's three parts) is unchanged. From
    2026-10-17 a helper line naming such a member is its recorded work (AC49, J13): "last: a
    helper session for the Chief of Staff, <date>", and "feeds: not recorded", since a helper
    line names no item.
  - **The Chief of Staff's card** (from 2026-10-17, J13): "last: <the latest piece's doing>,
    <date>", the `doing` as written and wrapping, and "feeds: not recorded", since its session
    lines name no item or sprint; no "On the Timeline ›" link.
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC67` uses AC49's fixtures and J11's, and checks each status's
    icon and word, that no light or glow is drawn (no `.sb-light` and no `infinite` animation on
    the Team screen), each card's "last:" and "feeds" against its records (Q-013's members feeding

    "unblocks". From 2026-10-17 it uses J13's fixtures too and checks the Chief of Staff's icon for
    each of AC49's status words, its "last:" and "feeds: not recorded", and a helper's "last:" on
    the Definer's card.

- AC68 [trace: V4 → AC68] — When the owner opens any of a project's screens in the sandbox (Status,
  Timeline, Team or Queue), then the same phase marker sits under the project's name: the current
  phase in its colour, its run, and the next milestone to reach; a tap on it opens the Timeline at
  Now; and "More detail" no longer offers the Pipeline view for a v3 project.
  - The owner's pick: "One phase marker everywhere", whose option text says: "Every project
    screen's header carries the current phase band (e.g. "Design · run 2" in Design's colour), so
    Status, Timeline, Team and Queue all say where the project is in the same way. The old
    Pipeline page under "More detail" folds into the Timeline, since they now show the same
    thing."
  - **The marker**, the same on all four screens, under the project's name and the screen's
    title, as drawn: a box with the now phase's stripe and chip; "<Phase> · run <k>" in the
    phase's colour (AC57's run; "<Phase>" alone in a project with one run); then "now · next to
    reach: <code>" from AC57's rule 2 (at 3117150, MD2; on the default branch since 680797b, MD3,
    because MD2's line now names its sprints); then "Timeline ›". Its other forms:
    - "now · next to reach: none, every milestone has work filed" under AC50's second branch (a
      milestone marked reached counting as filed, AC50, from 2026-10-17);
    - "Done" when the project is Done;
    - "Which phase is now is not recorded" when AC50 finds no now run;
    - "Phases not tracked" for a v2.5 project, with no colour and no link.
  - **A tap** on the marker, or Enter or Space, opens the Timeline scrolled to Now (AC59), as one
    screen step in the sandbox's history (AC56); on the Timeline itself it scrolls back to Now. It
    is a real link, named "Open the Timeline at Now".
  - **"More detail"** (AC55) for a v3 project offers Time asked, Agents and, when the project has
    any, **"Record notes"** (plan review round 3, N2): the project's record notes (`model.notes`,
    shown today only on the Pipeline view, src/public/sandbox.js:760-762), where AC31 names each
    record line or field skipped and AC61 names each out-of-form or repeated plan line. It opens
    as a full screen in the project, with its bottom bar and both back controls (AC56), listing
    the notes word for word, the count in its link ("Record notes (3)"). With no notes it is not
    offered. A v2.5 project keeps "Reduced view and waits", which holds its notes as today: its
    Timeline shows only "Phases not tracked", so nothing else holds those facts (C).
  - **The Pipeline view's code and route:** in the sandbox, a v3 project's Pipeline screen is
    removed (`renderPipeline`, src/public/sandbox.js:716-764, keeps only the v2.5 reduced view), and its route
    (`p/<name>/pipeline`) opens the Timeline at Now in its place, replacing the history entry
    rather than adding one, so "Back" never returns to it. The v2.5 reduced view stays. Without
    `?sandbox` the accepted Pipeline view is unchanged (AC43).
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC68` renders the four project screens from J11's fixture and
    checks that each holds one marker with the same words ("Design · run 2", "now · next to
    reach: MD2", "Timeline ›") and the Design colour token; each other form; that a tap, Enter and
    Space open the Timeline with the Now line in view and add one history step; that "More detail"
    offers no Pipeline link for the v3 fixture and keeps "Reduced view and waits" for the v2.5
    one; that, with a fixture holding a malformed record line (AC31) and an out-of-form plan line
    (AC61), "More detail" offers "Record notes (2)", whose screen lists both notes word for word,
    and that a project with no notes offers no such link; and that the Pipeline route opens the
    Timeline at Now without adding a history step.

**Four additions from the audit: notes 32 to 35** (added 2026-10-16, draft 4)
*From the owner's picks on the audit for missing elements (decisions/questions/P-001-trial-view-notes-2.md,
"The audit for missing elements", question 1, with the option texts quoted below; the audits are
docs/design/audits/2026-10-16-user-rep-missing-elements.md and
docs/design/audits/2026-10-16-gap-audit-against-goals.md) and the owner's approval of their mockup,
docs/design/mockups/audit-additions.html ("Approve as drawn (Recommended)", with "3 days
(Recommended)" for the stalled signal; same record, last section). Everything AC57 to AC68 set
holds here too.*

- AC69 [trace: V2 → AC69] — When the desk shows a gap that the agents fill, such as a missing why
  line or sprints not filed against a milestone, then it shows it in slate grey, saying who fills
  it, with a slate filled diamond where a diamond is used; and coral appears only on what waits
  on the owner.
  - The owner's pick: "Coral only for you (Recommended)", whose option text says: "Today every
    missing why line or "needs filing" flag shows in coral, your "needs you" colour, though agents
    fix them, not you. Dozens per project would read as false alarms. Make those neutral; coral
    stays only for things that need you." (V2 is the false-alarm target.)
  - **Coral (`--you`) is used only for** the owner's own things: an item in the Queue that waits on
    the owner, the "needs you" diamond on a group or a tile, "Waiting on you" in who has the ball
    and on the owner's Team tile, the queue's pill when something waits, and what AC48 already
    gives the owner (AC49's owner tile, AC52's rows that need the owner). Nothing an agent fills is
    coral.
  - **The agents' gaps, in slate (`--muted`), each saying who fills it** (as drawn):
    - a milestone with no why line: on the Timeline, "Why not recorded yet · for the agents to
      add" (AC59), and the same words in the Queue's "Unblocks" (AC66); on Status the quieter
      "why: not recorded" stays as AC57 has it;
    - sprints not filed against a milestone: "Not tied to a milestone · <n> sprints · for the agents
      to file", with a slate filled diamond (AC59, AC60);
    - the other agent-owned gaps on these screens, which carry no colour of their own and now say
      so in slate: the Queue's "Unblocks: milestone not recorded" and "Context: not recorded"
      (AC66), a sprint's Context and Why "not recorded" (AC60), the Team card's "feeds: not tied to
      a milestone" (AC67), and "Done today"'s "sprint: not recorded" (AC66).
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC69` renders Home, Status, Timeline, Team and Queue from the
    fixtures, and checks: that every element whose colour comes from `--you` is one of the owner's
    things listed above (none on a why line, a "Not tied to a milestone" line or any gap above);
    that each gap above shows its words and uses `--muted`; that the "Not tied to a milestone"
    diamond is filled with `--muted`; and the words "for the agents to add" and "for the agents to
    file".

- AC70 [trace: V4 → AC70] — When the owner looks at a place where a project went back and a new
  run began, on the Status screen's branch drawing or at the Timeline's band where that run starts,
  then the reason the run began is shown there, quoted from its record, with its source and one tap
  to the whole record; or "why it went back: not recorded" where no record gives it.
  - The owner's pick: "Why it went back (Recommended)", whose option text says: "At each branch
    point on Status and the Timeline, show the ruling that sent the project back (e.g. why Design
    ran a second time). Today the drawing shows that it branched, not why."
  - **Where the model records why a run began** (model S-023 AC6; found in
    governance/checks/orchestrator.py and v3_checks.py): an item's phase run starts again only
    after a *boundary* line in the dispatch log, an applied decision whose gate word sends the work
    back through a gate it did not pass (`BOUNDARY_WORDS`: `recycle` or `re-aim` at the `dor` gate,
    `recycle` or `revise` at `criteria` and `launch`; governance/checks/orchestrator.py:130 and :284-286). That line
    names its answer record (`record`, for example `decisions/<item>/<gate>-<n>.md`), whose
    `Reason:` line is the owner's reason (decisions/README.md: hold and recycle need one). A run
    the model's timeline builds from the plan's milestone order alone, with no line of its own
    (v3_checks.py, `place_milestones`), has no such record.
  - **The rule:** for each run that starts a new column (AC57's Terms, runs 2 and later), the
    reason is the latest boundary line of the run's item before the run's first routed line. The
    desk shows:
    - "Run <k> began · <date>" (the boundary line's `time`, in `ownerTimeZone`), in its run's own
      shade (AC57's run shades; build review round 1, N4);
    - the text of its record's first `Reason:` line, quoted as written; and when the record has no
      `Reason:` line, plain words instead, with the gate's code and word after in the code role
      (AC64; plan review round 3, N4): "sent back at a gate, with no reason written" then
      `<gate>: <word>` (for example `dor: recycle`) (C);
    - its source, "your ruling · <record path>" when the line is not marked `proxy`, otherwise
      "ruling by proxy · <record path>";
    - **one tap to the whole record:** the source is a link to the record on GitHub, by AC51's
      link rule.

    With no boundary line, or a record the tree does not hold: "Run <k> began · why it went back:
    not recorded" (slate, AC69).
  - **For Service-Desk, plainly:** at 3117150 and on today's default branch, run 2 (Design i2) is
    one the model's timeline builds from the plan's order (MD1 to MD3 follow M1 to M2B in
    section 3); no boundary line starts it (tests/desk/fixtures/j11-3117150/trace-notes.txt: Build
    i1 and Design i2 hold no decision line). So the desk shows "why it went back: not recorded"
    there. The mockup's quote of decisions/questions/P-001-design-step.md is an illustration: no
    record the desk reads ties that ruling to the run. A record that did (a model change, for
    example a run's start line naming its ruling) is listed in "What this spec does not cover".
  - **Where it shows:** on Status, in the box of the phase where the new run starts, at the top of
    that run, beside the curve; on the Timeline, at the top of that run's band, under its name. The
    words are the same on both.
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC70` checks "why it went back: not recorded" for Design i2 with
    J11's fixture, on both screens, and, with a made-up log (NOT a real record) holding a `dor`
    `recycle` line and its answer file with a `Reason:` line, a run 2 showing its date, that
    reason word for word, "your ruling · <path>" with its link, a proxy line's source, a record
    with no `Reason:` line giving "sent back at a gate, with no reason written" with `dor:
    recycle` after it in the code role, and a record the tree does not hold giving
    "not recorded".

- AC71 [trace: V1 → AC71] — When nothing has moved on an active project for 3 days or more and
  nothing in it waits on the owner, then its Home card says so in a slate line, "No movement for
  <n> days · last: <what>, <date>".
  - The owner's pick: "Stalled-project signal", whose option text says: "If nothing has moved on a
    project for a set number of days and nothing waits on you, its tile says so ("No movement for
    5 days"), so a quiet desk can't hide a stopped project." The number: "3 days (Recommended)"
    ("Long enough to skip a quiet weekend, short enough to catch a stop within the week.").
  - **What counts as moving**, each from a record the desk reads, the latest of these being the
    project's last movement:
    - the default branch's head commit, at its `committedAt` (J6's poll answer; every merge,
      filed question, filed card and committed agent record lands as a commit there);
    - a pull request merged into the default branch, at its `mergedAt` (J12);
    - an open pull request's `updated_at` (J1's open list), for work on a branch not yet merged
      (C);
    - a dispatch-log entry, at its `time` (J9): an agent's dispatch or outcome, a card raised, a
      decision.
  - **When it shows:** the project is in the Active group (AC54), was read successfully within the
    last 3 minutes (Quiet's rule), nothing in it waits on the owner (its pill's count is 0), and
    its last movement is at least 3 days (72 hours) before now. <n> is the whole number of days
    since the last movement.
  - **"last: <what>, <date>"**, the date in `ownerTimeZone`, the words for the latest movement:
    "PR #<n> merged" (J12), "PR #<n> updated" (J1), J9's headline words for a dispatch-log entry,
    or "a commit on <default branch>" for a head commit none of the others accounts for. When two
    have the same time, the first in this list is named (C).
  - **In slate, not coral** (AC69: it is a heads-up, not a decision for the owner). It sits on the
    card under who has the ball. It changes nothing else: the card's order, its group and "Quiet"
    stay as AC8, AC54 and AC1 to AC7 have them.
  - **Not shown** for a project in Done or Closed (C), for one that can't be read, while its reads
    are not all in ("not available yet" shows nowhere for it: the line simply waits), or when
    something waits on the owner. The mockup's "Moving: last change today." line for a moving
    project is not built: the owner picked only the stalled signal (C, a known difference).
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC71` drives a fake clock over fixtures: no line at 71 hours; the
    line at 72 hours with "3 days"; each kind of movement as the latest, with its words and date;
    the tie order; no line when something waits, for a Done project, or for one that can't be
    read; the line in `--muted`; and the card's order unchanged.

- AC72 [trace: V4 → AC72] — *Traced to V4 because the desk takes over the live address only through
  deploys the owner approves; none of V1 to V5 measures a deploy's safety itself (plan review round
  3, N4).* When a deploy waits for the owner's approval in a project's Queue, then
  its row says whether the commit it would deploy is the merge commit of a pull request the
  Reviewer passed, with the source, or that it is not and should not be approved, or that the desk
  can't check yet; the approval itself stays on GitHub.
  - The owner's pick: "Safe-to-approve on deploys", whose option text says: "Each deploy waiting
    for your approval shows whether it matches a pull request the Reviewer passed ("matches PR #77
    · Reviewer PASS"), so a wrong tap can't ship unreviewed code."
  - **Which commit** (plan review round 3, B1): a waiting deploy in AC52's Deploys group is a
    workflow run in J1's lists for the current head (AC52's known gap still holds: a deploy waiting
    on an earlier head drops out). The commit it would deploy is:
    - for a run started by a push (`event` `push`): its `head_sha`;
    - for a run started by hand (`event` `workflow_dispatch`, as every production deploy and every
      manual preview redeploy is): the commit the owner gave it, not `head_sha`, which is only
      main's head when it was started. J1's run list does not hold the run's inputs, but it holds
      its `display_title`, which desk-deploy sets to `deploy <sha> — <note>` for a hand-started
      run (.github/workflows/desk-deploy.yml:15, `run-name: deploy ${{ github.event_name == 'push' &&
      github.sha || inputs.sha }} — …`, and from Setup CS14 `deploy <sha>` alone for a push run;
      .github/scripts/desk_pick_commit.sh:18 deploys
      `INPUT_SHA`; `display_title` is in GitHub's run list, as the recorded
      tests/desk/fixtures/github/runs-e2ab9c5-dispatch.json shows). The desk takes the 40 lowercase hex characters straight after `deploy ` at the
      start of `display_title`;
    - when that cannot be read (another title, a short or missing sha, another workflow), the row
      says "Can't check this deploy yet", with the reason "the deploy's commit isn't readable from
      its title" one tap away.
  - **The run's title, from 2026-10-17** (the desk follow-ups):
    - **Why it changes:** since the merge setting changed, a merge commit's message is the pull
      request's title and whole description ("Facts relied on"). Until Setup CS14, a push run's
      title holds that whole message. AC52's Deploys row names a run by GitHub's run `name`,
      which is its title (src/lib/sandbox.js:416, from src/lib/records.js:810), so the row would
      show the whole description, run together.
    - **From CS14:** a push run's title is `deploy <sha>`, and a hand-started run's stays
      `deploy <sha> — <note>`.
    - **The parse holds for both:** each starts `deploy ` and the 40 hex characters, then ends
      or goes on with ` — `, which the parse allows (src/lib/feed.js:766). The desk reads the
      sha from the title only for a hand-started run; a push run's comes from `head_sha`, as
      above. The production job's preview rule reads the title too
      (.github/scripts/desk_preview_check.py:28, `^deploy ([0-9a-f]{40})\b`, used at
      desk-deploy.yml:132). It also matches both forms, so CS14 must keep a title that starts
      `deploy <sha>` (plan review round 1 of the desk follow-ups, N4).
    - **What the row shows for a push deploy:** "deploy <sha>: waiting for approval", with its
      run number, linked to the run. Under it is this criterion's line, which names the pull
      request: "Matches PR #<n>'s merge · Reviewer PASS", or another line below.
    - **No desk code changes for it.** A push run started before CS14 keeps its long title until
      the owner approves or rejects it. Its check is unchanged, since it is read from `head_sha`.
  - **The check**, from records the desk reads:
    1. the pull request whose merge commit is that sha: J12's `mergeCommit` of a merged pull
       request into the default branch (J12 adds the field);
    2. that pull request's `reviews/<n>/reviewer.md` on the default branch, decided by
       reviews/README.md's rule (the first `Verdict:` at column 0).
  - **What the row says** (as drawn):
    - both hold and the verdict is PASS: "Matches PR #<n>'s merge · Reviewer PASS", with the source
      on its own line, "reviews/<n>/reviewer.md · the merge commit on <default branch>", each
      linked by AC51's link rule;
    - otherwise: "Doesn't match a reviewed pull request: don't approve", with the reason on its own
      line: "no merge of a passed pull request has this commit" when no pull request's merge commit
      is the sha, "PR #<n>'s Reviewer verdict is FAIL" or "PR #<n> has no Reviewer verdict on
      <default branch>";
    - when a read it needs has failed or not yet arrived (J12, the tree, or the verdict's blob):
      "Can't check this deploy yet", with the reason one tap away (AC41). Never a pass by default.
      In particular (build review round 1, B1 and N6b), whenever J12's kept list is not current
      for the current head: a J12 failure is set, the first-page read at the current head has not
      answered, or a cold read is under way. A pull request outside the newest 100 that was kept
      as open and merged later is settled by the daily cold read (J12); until then the kept list
      may lack its merge, and the cases above cover the time it is being read.
  - **The desk only says.** It holds no write credential (mechanism): the row's link opens the run
    on GitHub, where the owner approves, as today. The row adds no colour: a "don't approve" row
    is marked by its words and a ✕ mark (AC42), and the Deploys group's own mark follows AC66.
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC72` uses J1's and J12's fixtures with a waiting deploy run
    (NOT a real record) on a head that is PR #77's merge commit with `reviews/77/reviewer.md`
    reading PASS; the same with a FAIL verdict and with no verdict; a head that is no pull
    request's merge commit; and J12's read failed, giving "Can't check this deploy yet". From
    draft 5 (B1) it adds a hand-started run (`workflow_dispatch`) whose `head_sha` is a
    records-only commit on main and whose `display_title` names PR #77's merge commit
    (`deploy <sha> — …`, its `inputs.sha` differing from `head_sha`), giving "Matches PR #77's
    merge · Reviewer PASS"; the same run with a `display_title` that names no full sha, giving
    "Can't check this deploy yet"; and a push run checked by its `head_sha`. From 2026-10-17 (the
    desk follow-ups) the push run's title is `deploy <sha>` alone, as from Setup CS14. The fixture
    sets both its `name` and its `display_title` to it, as GitHub gives them, since the row is
    built from `name` (plan review round 1 of the desk follow-ups, N8). Its row reads "deploy
    <sha>: waiting for approval" with "Matches PR #77's merge · Reviewer PASS"
    under it. The hand-started run keeps `deploy <sha> — <note>`. It checks each row's words,
    source and links, and that no row offers an approval of its own.

### N — Notifications the moment anything waits on you (first, from 2026-10-18)
*Covers AC45 as ruled on 2026-10-08 and widened by the owner's request of 2026-10-18 ("Notifications
the moment anything waits on you", draft 4), and the owner's cost instruction of 2026-10-18 (AC78).
Built first, ahead of M3A and of MD3's remaining work, in the desk as accepted, so it does not wait
for the trial view. Its senders are Workflows 3 and 4; its joins are J16 to J21; its experiment is
EXP-006. Not a milestone.*

- AC73 [trace: V1 → AC73] — When anything in a connected project, v3 or v2.5, starts waiting on
  the owner, then that project's sender counts it as waiting from the moment its record says so,
  by exactly the kinds below and no others: everything the desk counts as needing the owner
  (Terms, which from 2026-10-18 includes a waiting deploy in a project marked `deploysNeedOwner`,
  AC52), less the pull requests the asks-merge job may still merge (the owner's ruling,
  decisions/questions/P-001-notify-job-merges.md), less a question being asked in a session or
  whose answer is being recorded (AC77).
  - **The kinds** (each read as J2, J9 and J16 read it, on the project's default branch):

    | Kind (the words used) | Model | Its record | It starts waiting when | It stops when |
    |---|---|---|---|---|
    | a decision card | v3 | `queue/<item>-<gate>-<n>.md`, gate not `dor`, `criteria` or `launch` (J2) | the card file is on the default branch with no answer `decisions/<item>/<gate>-<n>.md` | its answer is there |
    | a gate ruling | v3 | the same, gate `dor`, `criteria` or `launch` (AC52) | as a decision card | as a decision card |
    | a held loop | v3 | a hold card `queue/ci-hold-<sha12>.md`, open by J2's rule (AC46) | it is on the default branch and open: the Orchestrator writes it after holding for over 60 minutes (Terms) | J2 says it is no longer open |
    | a merge to do by hand | v3 | a merge card `queue/<item>-merge-conflict.md` or `-merge-by-hand.md`, open by J2's rule (AC47) | as a held loop | as a held loop |
    | a card that went missing | v3 | `status/<item>.toml` in state `waiting-owner` with no card file (Terms) | that state is on the default branch with no card file | the card file arrives (then it is a card, under its own key) or the state changes |
    | a question | both | `questions/<name>.md`, not `_TEMPLATE.md` (Terms, AC3) | the file is on the default branch (v3: with no answer record), unless AC77 holds it; then when AC77's hold ends | v3: its answer is on the default branch, or an open pull request adds it (AC77); both: the file is gone |
    | a pull request to merge | both | an open, non-draft pull request into the default branch, not from an `item/` branch in a v3 project (Terms, AC2) | it is opened, reopened or marked ready; for a job candidate, when J16's rule says the job has left it | it is merged, closed or turned back into a draft |
    | a deploy to approve | v3, `deploysNeedOwner` only | a workflow run in the project whose `status` is `waiting` (AC52's Deploys, J1, J16) | the run turns `waiting` | it is approved, rejected or cancelled |

  - **Not counted, so never notified:**
    - **the asks-merge job's pull requests** while the job may still merge them (J16), as ruled.
      The question such a pull request files counts once it is on the default branch: that is a
      real ask;
    - **"Verdicts to write"** (C): no record the desk reads names one (AC52). The pull request it
      belongs to counts under "a pull request to merge";
    - **a waiting deploy in a project not marked `deploysNeedOwner`** (J7): shown in its Deploys
      line, as before; marking another project is the owner's ruling (the deploys ruling, "If
      another project adds deploys, it is put to you again");
    - **nothing outside the records:** a token about to expire, the owner's own setup steps;
    - everything Terms calls activity.
  - **One key per thing,** `<project>|<kind>|<id>`, where `<id>` is the record's path, the pull
    request's number or the run's id; stored only as J18's keyed hash. A thing that stops waiting
    and later starts again is waiting anew, once a run has seen it stop (J18; a known limit: a pull
    request turned into a draft and back, or closed and reopened, between two runs is not seen to
    stop).
  - **The sender's deploys and the desk's:** the sender lists every waiting run
    (`actions/runs?status=waiting`), the desk only those on the current head (AC52's known gap),
    so a deploy still waiting on an earlier head notifies though the desk no longer shows it.
  - **Both models.** A v2.5 project (Personal-Org-Operating-Model) has only questions and pull
    requests: it keeps no cards or status files (Terms), and has no deploy workflow or environment
    (its .github/workflows/ at 90f53e3).
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC73` runs the set step (J16) on fixtures:
    - Service-Desk's real records at ef8daf4 for the kinds they hold (cards and their answers,
      status files, a question and its answer, and pull requests from REST answers recorded
      once, as J12's are);
    - made-up records, each NOT a real record and labelled so in tests/desk/fixtures/README.md,
      for the kinds no record on main holds: a hold card, a merge card, a missing card, a waiting
      deploy, a job candidate in each of J16's states, a draft, an `item/` pull request, and a
      question held by AC77;
    - a v2.5 project in tests/desk/fixtures/poom/'s placeholder form (no file of that private
      project is copied).

    It checks each key and kind; that each thing not counted is absent; that a waiting deploy
    counts in Service-Desk and not in a project without `deploysNeedOwner`; and that the set equals
    the desk's flagged items (AC1 to AC3, AC46, AC47, AC52's Deploys) less J16's job candidates
    and AC77's held and being-recorded questions, for the same records, with every fixture's
    waiting deploys on the current head, so the desk's head-only list and the sender's list agree
    (a deploy waiting on an earlier head is the known difference above, tested on its own).

- AC74 [trace: V1 → AC74] — When something newly waits on the owner (AC73), then the owner's
  iPhone shows one notification for it, naming the project and what waits, and a tap on it opens
  that item on the desk; it is sent once, never repeated, and never for something that stops
  waiting.
  - **How soon** (stated honestly; E, measured by EXP-006):
    - **in Service-Desk, on an event** (a change landing on the default branch, by the owner, the
      Orchestrator or the asks-merge job; a pull request from the project's own branches opened,
      reopened or given a new head, which starts governance and pr-body runs; a pull request marked
      ready, turned back to draft or closed, which starts the no-secret ready workflow; a check
      finishing; a deploy requested): its sender runs when that event's run completes (Workflow
      3). GitHub usually starts it within a minute (not
      documented, C); the run takes under a minute (E); Apple's delivery time is not documented.
      So usually within about 2 to 5 minutes of the record, the governance run's own length
      included;
    - **a card or a held loop** arrives with the Orchestrator's push at the end of its run's
      commit job, so it notifies when that run finishes, a minute or two after the push (E);
    - **a job candidate the asks-merge job leaves** (J16): when the first asks-merge run after its
      checks finish has judged and left it, usually within a minute or two of its last check
      (Workflow 3 admits asks-merge's own `workflow_run`-started runs, review round 3, N2); when
      that run is the job's 15-minute fallback, up to about 15 minutes; when the job's reason is
      one it would retry and it lasts (a merge conflict, a file it cannot read as text), 15 to
      about 30 minutes, the time J16's rule (b) waits to see it again;
    - **a pull request from a fork:** the origin gate (Workflow 3) skips runs a fork's pull
      request starts, so the 5-minute check finds it: usually within 5 to 10 minutes. GitHub may
      run a schedule late (high load includes the start of every hour) or drop it (F-gh-12,
      F-gh-12b);
    - **a deploy:** when its run is requested, the sender waits up to 2 minutes for it to turn
      `waiting` (C), so within about 3 minutes (E);
    - **in Personal-Org-Operating-Model,** its own sender (Workflow 4) runs on its own pushes to
      main, its pull requests' `ci` runs and its no-secret ready workflow, about as fast as
      Service-Desk's; what no event catches, its 3-hourly check finds: up to about 3 hours, later
      if GitHub runs the schedule late (C, to spare the account's minutes);
    - **anything an event misses** is found by the next check, so nothing waits unnotified longer
      than that check's delay;
    - **a run stopped by the budget guard** (J16) leaves its work to the next run with budget: in
      the worst case, when the repository's workflow token is near its hourly limit, up to about an
      hour (review round 3, N7);
    - **if EXP-006 case 10's last fallback applies** (the triggering run's origin readable neither
      from the payload nor through REST), Workflow 3 admits no `workflow_run` run, and every event
      but the owner's own push waits for the 5-minute check: usually 5 to 10 minutes (Workflow 3).
  - **What it says** (C), plain words, no codes before the words:
    - title: the project's name ("Service-Desk");
    - body: "Waits on you: <kind words>: <what>", where `<what>` is the card's or question's
      title, the pull request's title, or the deploy run's title, cut to 140 characters with "…"
      ("Waits on you: a question: Who merges each new rough estimate?"). A held loop reads "Waits
      on you: a held loop: the default branch's governance run is not green";
    - **a pull request from outside the project** (its `author_association` is none of `OWNER`,
      `MEMBER` or `COLLABORATOR`) reads "Waits on you: a pull request from someone outside the
      project", with no title, so a stranger's words never reach the lock screen (C);
    - nothing else: no record text beyond the title, and no secret.
  - **The tap** opens `<deskUrl>/?open=<key>` (J7's `push.deskUrl`), where `<key>` is AC73's key,
    URL-encoded (C). The desk opens that project, scrolls to that item, marks it "Opened from a
    notification", and opens its view: a card's or question's full text (AC11, AC12), a pull
    request's row with its link to GitHub, a deploy's row with its run link. If it no longer
    waits, the desk says "That no longer waits on you" and shows the project. The sender only ever
    writes the desk's own address. On the service-worker path the worker also refuses a
    `navigate` on another origin (AC75); on the Declarative path nothing on the phone checks it,
    and whether iOS opens another origin's address is not documented (C; Risks). If
    Cloudflare Access asks the owner to sign in first, the desk opens at the same address after
    (EXP-006 checks it).
  - **Once, never repeated** (C): one notification per key for each time it starts waiting
    (J18). No reminder while it waits, and none when it stops. If the same key is sent again after
    a failure, the notification on the phone is replaced, not added to (J17's `Topic` and `tag`).
  - **Grouped** (C): when one run finds three or more things newly waiting, it sends one
    notification instead: title "Service Desk", body "<n> things now wait on you in <project>",
    and a tap opens Home. Each project's sender groups its own.
  - **The automatic job's merges** send nothing (AC73, J16): "Every notification means you have
    something to do" (the ruling's option text).
  - **The GitHub issues the Orchestrator already opens for cards** (AC45's baseline) stay as they
    are; with GitHub Mobile, a card may ping twice. The owner can turn off assignment pushes in
    GitHub Mobile (Q-011 G1).
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC74` drives the sender with a fake clock, a fake GitHub and a
    fake push service, and checks: one push per newly waiting key, with its title, body and tap
    address; no push for a key already sent, for one that stopped waiting, for a job candidate or
    for a held question; a key that stops and starts again, with a run between, sent again; three
    new keys giving one grouped push; a body cut at 140 characters; an outside author's pull
    request with no title; and that the desk opened at `/?open=<key>` shows the item, "Opened from
    a notification", and "That no longer waits on you" for a key that no longer waits.

- AC75 [trace: V1 → AC75] — When the owner wants notifications on the iPhone, then the desk,
  added to the Home Screen, offers "Turn on notifications" behind a tap, asks iOS for permission
  only on that tap, and shows the one value the owner copies into GitHub; and a test run from
  GitHub shows a notification.
  - **The Home Screen web app** (Q-011 W1, W3): `src/public/manifest.webmanifest` with `name`
    "Service Desk", `short_name` "Desk", `start_url` "/", `scope` "/", `display` "standalone",
    and icons at 180, 192 and 512 px from `src/public/icons/`; the page links it with
    `<link rel="manifest" href="/manifest.webmanifest" crossorigin="use-credentials">`, so the
    manifest is fetched with Access's cookie (Q-011 X9, C), and adds `<link rel="apple-touch-icon"
    href="/icons/desk-180.png">`. Web Push works only for the desk added to the Home Screen, on iOS
    16.4 or later (W1).
  - **Where the control is** (C): one line, "Notifications", in More detail in both views, and on
    Home's top line until notifications are on for this device. Its words by state:
    - not opened from the Home Screen (`display-mode` is not `standalone`): "To get notifications,
      add the desk to your Home Screen: Share, then Add to Home Screen, then open it from there";
    - the page has no public key (J7's `push.vapidPublicKey` missing): "Notifications aren't set
      up yet";
    - permission not asked: a button, "Turn on notifications";
    - permission refused: "Notifications are off for the desk. Turn them on in Settings,
      Notifications, Service Desk" (P2, P4);
    - on, and the value not yet marked copied: the value, a "Copy" button, and the steps (Setup
      N6), with "I've added it to GitHub", which only hides the steps on this device;
    - on: "On for this phone", with "Show the value again" and "Send a test from GitHub" (Setup
      N8's steps).
  - **On the tap** (J19): permission is asked only then, never on load (W4; P1); then the desk
    subscribes, through Declarative Web Push where the page offers it (iOS 18.4 and later, D1),
    with no service worker, or else through the service worker `/sw.js` (W6), with
    `userVisibleOnly: true` and J7's public key (W9, W12) (C). The value it shows is the
    subscription as JSON: its `endpoint` and its `keys` (V2). The desk sends it nowhere: it holds
    no write credential, and the endpoint must be kept secret (V9).
  - **The service worker,** `src/public/sw.js`, same-origin, served as JavaScript (X2): on a push
    it shows the notification from the payload's `notification` object, with its `tag` (J17); on
    a tap it opens or focuses a desk window at the `navigate` address, and only when that address
    is on the desk's own origin, else at the desk's root (X7) (C); on `pushsubscriptionchange`
    (V10) it shows "Desk notifications need turning on again: open the desk", since it cannot
    change a GitHub secret itself. Every push shows a notification, so Safari never revokes
    permission (W8).
  - **No other change to the desk** beyond AC77, AC78's line and the Deploys count (AC52): no
    package (AC42), no Content-Security-Policy source beyond `'self'`, and no new function call
    (J21's read rides the blob call).
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC75` checks the manifest's fields and the page's two links;
    each state's words with a faked `display-mode`, permission and public key; that permission is
    asked only inside the tap's handler; the Declarative path where `window.pushManager` exists and
    the service-worker path where it does not; the value's form (J19); and the service worker's
    push, tap (an address on another origin opening the desk's root) and `pushsubscriptionchange`
    handlers, with a faked `self`.

- AC76 [trace: V1 → AC76] — When a push fails, the subscription has expired, a project or the
  sender's state can't be read, then nothing that waits is lost or sent twice on that account, and
  the owner is told on GitHub when notifications have stopped for any reason the sender can see;
  a sender that cannot run at all is a known limit, below.
  - **A push refused for now** (429, or a 5xx; Q-011 V7): retried in the same run after 5 and 30
    seconds (C); still failing, it is not marked sent, so the next run sends it again. One unsent
    for 6 hours is given up, and counted in the issue below by project and number only (C).
  - **The subscription is gone or invalid** (410, the subscription expired; 404, which Apple gives
    for an invalid `:path`, an endpoint that is no longer right; Apple's page, Facts relied on;
    V7): the sender opens one issue in its own
    repository, or updates the open one, titled "Desk notifications have stopped", labelled
    `desk-notify`, assigned to the owner, saying why in plain words and giving Setup N5, N6 and N8's
    steps. It sends nothing more until `DESK_PUSH_SUBSCRIPTION` changes (J18 keeps its keyed
    hash), then resumes, sends "Notifications are back on", and closes the issue.
  - **The push service refuses the sender** (400, 401 or 403; Apple's page: "403 There's an
    error with your authentication", with a JSON `reason`): the same issue, saying why in plain
    words, and the sender keeps the item unsent and tries again each run. It mints a new JWT only
    when Apple's `reason` is `BadJwtToken` and its JWT was minted 60 minutes ago or more, never
    more often ("Don't refresh your JWT more frequently than once per hour", Apple's page); for
    `VapidPkHashMismatch` or any other reason it mints nothing and the issue says so (C). The
    first step checks that `DESK_VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY`'s public half equals J7's `vapidPublicKey`,
    and stops with the issue "the notification keys don't match" if not (J17). `test: fail`
    sends exactly one request, never retried and never re-minted (J17).
  - **A project unread** (its checkout or a REST read failing): its set is kept as the last run had
    it (J18), so nothing re-notifies; things that started waiting meanwhile are sent once it reads
    again. Unread for 30 minutes in a row (C), the issue says "Desk notifications can't read
    <project>", and closes once it reads again.
  - **The sender's state unreadable** (the artifact list or download failing, or the signature not
    matching, J18): the run sends nothing and uploads nothing, so the next run uses the last good
    state. A signature that does not match opens the issue at once ("the sender's memory could not
    be trusted"); a failed read, after 30 minutes in a row (C).
  - **The sender can't run at all** (Actions down, the workflow disabled, the switch off, 60 days
    with no activity in a public repository disabling its schedule, F-gh-12b, or the account's
    Actions minutes used up for the private project, Workflow 4): a known limit.
    Nothing on the phone says so; the desk shows everything when opened (AC1 to AC7), and GitHub's
    own failure notice for a failed run is the only signal (C).
  - **Nothing private in public places.** Service-Desk is public. Its sender reads only Service-Desk
    (Workflow 3), whose records are public already; the operating-model project's sender runs in
    that private repository (Workflow 4), with its state, logs and issues there. Even so, every
    key a sender stores is a keyed hash (J18), every issue names projects and counts only, every
    log line holds counts, reasons and keyed hashes only, no usage figure appears in a log, an
    issue or the state in clear (AC78), and no fixture copies a file of the private project
    (AC73).
  - A test marked `ac-test: S-001/AC76` drives the sender with a fake push service answering 201,
    429 then 201, 503 three times, 404, 410, 403 `BadJwtToken` then 201 after a new JWT, 403
    `BadJwtToken` over five runs in one hour (one new JWT only), 403 `VapidPkHashMismatch` (no new
    JWT), and `test: fail` (exactly one request), and
    with fake GitHub reads failing, and checks: the retries and their waits; unsent keys sent next
    run; the 6-hour give-up counted, not named; the issue opened, updated and closed, its body
    holding no title, path or number of an item; no push after 410 until the subscription's hash
    changes; 403 never stopping the sender and never minting more than once an hour; the key check at the first step; a project unread
    keeping its set, and the issue at 30 minutes; and the state read failing, and forged, each
    sending nothing.

- AC77 [trace: V1 → AC77] [trace: V2 → AC77] — When a filed question is being asked in a session,
  or answered there with its answer not yet recorded on the default branch, then the desk shows it
  as such, with no answer link, and no notification is sent for it, until the piece of work it was
  asked in ends or the answer's pull request opens; and when the owner taps any answer link, the desk first makes sure
  the item is still open. The gaps that remain are stated below.
  - The owner's words, recorded (docs/design/references/owner-report-2026-10-18-stale-answer-link.md,
    landing with this spec's pull request): "the question that came through here, that I answered,
    lingered in the service desk as a decision - with the option to merge in GitHub. Thinking it
    was a legit option I went through that path but it was stale". The windows the records show,
    from filing merge to answer merge: about 3, 4, 6 and 54 minutes, the last because "the answers
    came after the owner viewed the mockup" (same record). And the filing ruling: "the gap between
    an ask answered in the session and its record landing is to be closed, not the filing"
    (decisions/questions/P-001-file-only-required.md, line 1).
  - **Being asked** (from the moment it is on the default branch; C): the Chief of Staff writes, in
    a question it asks in a session, one line in column 0, `Asked in session: <session id>`
    (J13's id form, `[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,100}`): in a v3 question, the line directly after its
    `Milestone:` line; in a v2.5 question (no `Milestone:` line, J2), the first line after its
    `# Ruling needed:` line that is not blank (J2, CS17). The model's checks allow it as they
    stand. While held, the question reads "Being asked in
    your session: answer there", with its title and options to read and no answer link, no answer
    box and no "Open in GitHub" to answer; it is not counted as waiting (the pill, the counts, the
    Queue's Decisions, "Quiet") and is not notified.
  - **The hold ends** at the first of:
    - **the answer's pull request opening** (below): the question then reads "Your answer is being
      recorded";
    - **the end of the piece of work it was asked in** (J13; v3 projects, the only ones whose
      Chief of Staff sessions are recorded; Critic round 3, B1): an end line on the default branch,
      in that session's file `sessions/chief-of-staff/<session id>.jsonl`, in form (J13's rules),
      whose `time` is after the question's raised time (AC20's: the oldest commit of
      `questions/<name>.md`), compared as instants. That is the end line that closes the piece open
      when the question was raised (J13's pieces), or a later one. An end line whose `time` is at or
      before the raised time ends nothing: a session file holds several pieces of work, and the
      reported session's file already held an end line at 2026-10-18T03:08:21Z (its line 17) when
      every question in the owner's report was filed, after 11:26Z (ccf320f filed the first, with
      its piece's start line at 11:26:22Z). A start line ends nothing either. While the page has
      not yet read the raised time (AC20's history read), no end line ends the hold (C: the safe
      side; the 8-hour fallback below still runs). Once such an end line is there, the piece is over, so the question becomes an ordinary waiting one, with its link,
      and the sender notifies it;
    - **8 hours after its raised time** (AC20's; while that read has not arrived, the time this
      device first saw it), a fallback only (C: J13's own limit for a piece of work with no end
      line, so a session that never records its end does not hide an ask for longer); in a v2.5
      project, whose sessions are not recorded, this is the only end besides the pull request;
    - **"Answer it here instead"**, a tap that ends it on this device only.

    So the reported 54-minute case is held from filing until the answer's pull request opens, and
    then reads "being recorded" until it merges: no answer link shows in between, provided the
    Chief of Staff opens the answer's pull request before that piece's end line lands (CS17). An
    end line landing first, in a pull request opened earlier, would end the hold and show the link
    until the answer's pull request opens: CS17 forbids that order.
  - **Being recorded** (J20; C): an open, non-draft pull request into the default branch, from the
    project's own branches (a fork's pull request is never read as recording an answer, J20), whose
    files, in a v3 project, add `decisions/questions/<name>.md` (whether or not they also delete
    the question), or, in a v2.5 project, delete `questions/<name>.md` (there the answer goes to
    the ledger and the question file is deleted, Terms), for an open question `<name>`, makes that
    question read "Your answer is being recorded", with its title, a link to that pull request, and
    no answer link. It is not counted as waiting and not notified.
    When the pull request merges, the question is answered or gone, as today (J2, J9); when it
    closes unmerged, the question is open again and waits from that moment, with one notification.
  - **The gaps that remain** (stated in the lead and in the open question):
    - a question asked in a session without the line (the Chief of Staff's slip) shows its answer
      link, and notifies, until its answer's pull request opens;
    - a piece's end line that lands before the answer's pull request opens (the Chief of Staff's
      slip against CS17) ends the hold, so the link shows, and the question notifies, until the
      answer's pull request opens;
    - a question still held after 8 hours with no session end line and no answer's pull request
      becomes ordinary, with its link;
    - after the answer's pull request merges, the desk may show the old state for up to about a
      minute, until its next read; the re-read below covers a tap in that minute.
  - **The pull request itself** (the answer record's own): under the 17 October ruling
    (decisions/questions/P-001-asks-merge-on-desk.md, line 1: "while open, they still show as
    waiting on the owner"), it still shows in Merges and counts as waiting on the owner while
    open, and its row adds "Your answer to <question>: the automatic job merges it, usually within
    minutes". It never notifies while the job may still merge it (AC73). The open question asks
