          `stall_grace_minutes` (15). A session past that is activity: "running for <d>, past its
          limit".
        - The desk reads ROUTING.toml's `[limits]` and `[time_limits]` tables as `key = <int>`
          lines. If they can't be read, it uses 30 and 15 and notes it.
      - **Waiting on the owner:** `state = "waiting-owner"`, giving `gate` and `card`. It is linked to
        `queue/<item>-<gate>-<card>.md` and flagged per Terms.
      - **Next:** `state = "ready"` → "next: <role> (<stage name>)". `state = "returned"` → "next:
        the Orchestrator routes the <role>'s return (verdict <last_verdict>)".
      - **Finished:** `done` → "done". `closed` → "closed: <outcome>".
      - **As of:** the `time` of the latest dispatch-log entry with that `item`.
  - **v3 dispatch log.** Path `dispatch-log/<YYYY-MM>.jsonl`, one JSON object per line. The 192
    lines of `2026-10.jsonl` (2026-10-06) have exactly these shapes:
    - `"trigger":"request"`, with `result: "created"`: an item created.
    - `"action":"dispatch"`: a session starts.
      - Keys: `item`, `role`, `route`, `stage`, `spec`, `time`, `trigger`.
      - Its session id is `<item>:<route>:<time>`, the form S-014 AC10 fixes.
    - `"trigger":"outcome"` with `session`: a session ends.
      - Keys: `result` (`ok`, `error`, `timeout` or `usage-limit`, orchestrator.py:96), `verdict`,
        `record` and `time`.
    - `"action":"card"`: a card raised (J2).
    - `"trigger":"decision"`: an answer read (J2). It may carry `strike: true`.
    - `"action":"done"`: an item done.
    - `"trigger":"anomaly"` with `error`: shown as activity, with the error text.

    **The headline (v3)** is the latest entry, in the words for its shape below. `<t>` is HH:MM in
    `ownerTimeZone`, with the date in front when it isn't today.
    - **request:** "<item> created, <t>".
    - **dispatch:** "<item>: <role> started (<stage name>), <t>".
    - **outcome:** "<item>: <role> finished — <result>, <t>". When `verdict` is neither `none` nor
      null, it reads "— <result>, verdict <verdict>, <t>".
    - **card:** "<item>: waiting on you — card <gate>-<card>, <t>".
    - **decision:**
      - when `result` starts with `closed/`: "<item> closed: <word> (card <gate>-<card>), <t>";
      - otherwise: "<item>: you answered <gate>-<card> with <word>, <t>";
      - " (proxy)" is added when `proxy` is true.
    - **done:** "<item> done, <t>".
    - **anomaly:** "<item>: Orchestrator anomaly, <t>".

    Example: the last line of `2026-10.jsonl` (2026-10-06) gives "P-001 closed: re-scope (card
    stop-6), 16:31", at UTC−5. A line that isn't JSON, or matches no shape, is named in `notes` and
    skipped, and the headline falls back to the previous entry.
  - **v2.5 reduced view** (Personal-Org-Operating-Model at 677fe5a).
    - **Sprint:** the files `docs/sprints/*.md` other than `PROGRESS.md` and `SPRINT_PLAN.md`. The
      open one has a line `status: open`; its title is the first `# ` line.
      - Sample `docs/sprints/m1.4-v3-build.md`: `# m1.4 — v3 build: specs, tools, template and
        agent files` / `status: open`.
      - The other three say `status: closed`.
      - Zero or two open sprints: "sprint unclear", shown as a note, not a flag (AGENTS.md §0 says
        stop and ask; that is the project's business, not the desk's).
    - **Headline:** the last line in `docs/LEDGER.md` matching `^## (L-[0-9]+) — (.+)$`. Sample:
      `## L-0124 — Continue Service-Desk under the full process, with a checkpoint; S-017's
      supersession covers two more token pins`.
    - **Next:** that entry's `licenses_next:` line. Sample: `licenses_next: S-017 build review round
      2; then the owner's verdict on PR 20 and Service-Desk's adoption.`
    - **As of:** that entry's `date:` line.
    - **Items, stages and running now:** "not recorded in this repository's records (operating model
      v2.5.1)".
    - The ledger is 128,438 bytes. It is read only when its blob sha changes.
  - **v3 projects and sprints.** Sprints are retired in v3 (governance/checks/check_all.sh header:
    "Sprints are retired there"), so for `model: v3` the desk reads no sprint file. Service-Desk's
    `docs/sprints/m1.1-first-sprint.md` is the unfilled template (`# m1.1 — <name>`).
  - **Awkward cases:**
    - **A dispatch with no outcome while the item is no longer `dispatched`:** shown as "no outcome
      recorded". Real case: Q-006's dispatch at 2026-10-04T01:28:40Z; its outcome was logged under
      session `Q-006:research-to-source:2026-10-04T01:25:01Z`, and the Orchestrator logged an anomaly
      at line 121.
    - **An outcome whose session matches no dispatch:** shown as it stands.
    - **A retry:** a dispatch of the same item and role right after an outcome with
      `result: "error"`. Real case: the P-001 definer, 2026-10-05T22:50:13Z. Shown as activity,
      "retry".
    - **A status file that won't parse:** the item is shown with "can't read status" and is never
      dropped. If its card file is open, it is still flagged via J2.
    - **A file missing:** the field shows null with the note "not found". The project is not
      can't-read.
  - A test marked `join-test: S-001/J9` covers it, using the samples above committed as fixtures
    under `tests/`.

- **J10 — Session records → Agents view (M2, page side).**
  - **Timeline, from today's records.** Sessions come from the dispatch log (J9): each `dispatch`
    entry is joined to the `outcome` entry whose `session` equals `<item>:<route>:<time>`.
    - Start is the dispatch `time` and end is the outcome `time`.
    - `result` and `verdict` come from the outcome.
    - Sample pair: dispatch `{"action":"dispatch",…,"item":"P-001",…,"role":"critic",
      "route":"define-to-plan-review",…,"time":"2026-10-06T16:40:19Z",…}` and outcome
      `{"item":"P-001",…,"record":"reviews/S-001/plan.md","result":"ok","role":"critic",
      "session":"P-001:define-to-plan-review:2026-10-06T16:40:19Z","time":"2026-10-06T18:48:58Z",
      "verdict":"FAIL"}`. That gives a run time of 2 h 8 min 39 s.
  - **Usage figures, from `status/outcomes.jsonl`.** One line per session. The keys before model
    S-020 are `control`, `item`, `record`, `record_sha`, `result`, `role`, `session` and `verdict`
    (sample line 1: `{"control": "22dc…", "item": "Q-001", "record": "research/Q-001-memo.md", …,
    "session": "Q-001:ready-dispatch:2026-10-01T01:46:42Z", "verdict": "none"}`). Lines carry one
    more key, `usage`, from the first session after S-020's adoption (L-0005, 2026-10-08); older
    lines don't, and neither do the Orchestrator's own `error` lines or check runs (S-020 AC2).
    - **The form, frozen by model S-020** (AC2; this replaces the form J10 proposed to D7.1):
      `"usage": {"input_tokens": int|null, "output_tokens": int|null,
      "cache_read_input_tokens": int|null, "cache_creation_input_tokens": int|null,
      "num_turns": int|null, "duration_ms": int|null, "context_window": int|null,
      "autocompact_threshold": int|null, "context_peak": int|null, "derived": ["context_peak"],
      "would_have_stopped": true|false|null, "compactions": int|null,
      "cost_usd_estimate": number|null}`.
    - **Whole numbers or null:** `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cache_read_input_tokens`,
      `cache_creation_input_tokens`, `num_turns`, `duration_ms`, `context_window`,
      `autocompact_threshold`, `context_peak` and `compactions`.
    - **Three keys that are not whole numbers:**
      - `derived`, a list of figure names. Any figure named there is labelled "derived from
        per-turn usage" wherever it is shown (today always `context_peak`: the agent tool doesn't
        report it, EXP-003).
      - `would_have_stopped`: `true`, `false` or `null`. It records only; nothing stops a session
        for its context (L-0005).
      - `cost_usd_estimate`: a number or `null`, the agent tool's own estimate. Any view of it says
        "estimate", and it is never presented as a charge: the account is billed by subscription.
    - **Percentages:** `context_peak / context_window` and `context_peak /
      autocompact_threshold`, each one decimal, each needing both its figures and a divisor
      above 0; otherwise "not available" (AC23's rule), while the figures themselves still show. The second is the figure nearer the owner's answer 5 (stopping
      before compaction, P-001-build-path.md).
    - **Cost:** `cost_usd_estimate` is shown as "estimate $x.xx" (two decimals), "not available"
      when null (AC23).
  - **What the view shows for each case:**
    - **A line without `usage`:** "not recorded", whatever the reason (a session before S-020's
      adoption, an Orchestrator `error` line, or `usage` dropped by `record`; AC23).
    - **A key present with `null`, or a figure EXP-003 found the tool doesn't report:**
      "not available", including the threshold percentage, "would have stopped" and the
      compaction count (AC23).
    - **A session in the dispatch log with no `outcomes.jsonl` line:** timeline only.
    - **A `usage` key with a value of the wrong kind:** only that key is marked. It is named in
      `notes` and that figure shows "not recorded"; every other figure on the line still shows
      (not the whole `usage`).
      - For the whole-number keys above, the wrong kind is a non-integer or a negative number
        (`null` is not a wrong kind: it is "not available").
      - For `cost_usd_estimate`, it is anything but a finite number of 0 or more, or `null`.
      - For `derived`, anything but a list of strings; for `would_have_stopped`, anything but
        `true`, `false` or `null`.
      - These checks are deliberately looser than the commit job's: the desk refuses only what it
        can't display meaningfully (non-integers, negatives, non-finite numbers). The commit job's
        upper bounds (10^12 for the figures, 10^6 for the cost) and its exact `derived` value
        (`["context_peak"]`) stay the commit job's; the desk doesn't repeat them.
  - **Size:** `outcomes.jsonl` was 24,974 bytes on 2026-10-06 and is not rotated. It is read only
    when its blob sha changes (see Risks).
  - A test marked `join-test: S-001/J10` covers it, using the sample pair above and three
    `outcomes.jsonl` lines, each with its exact expected drill-down:
    - **Sample line 1** (real, no `usage`): every usage figure "not recorded".
    - **A fixture line in the frozen form. It is a fixture, not a real sample;** its figures are the
      ones S-020's own test synthesises from EXP-003's run 2:
      `{"control": "<fixture>", "item": "P-001", "record": "reviews/S-001/plan.md", "record_sha":
      "<fixture>", "result": "ok", "role": "critic",
      "session": "P-001:define-to-plan-review:2026-10-09T09:00:00Z", "verdict": "PASS",
      "usage": {"input_tokens": 10, "output_tokens": 707, "cache_read_input_tokens": 168160,
      "cache_creation_input_tokens": 11120, "num_turns": 7, "duration_ms": 18392,
      "context_window": 1000000, "autocompact_threshold": 784000, "context_peak": 36494,
      "derived": ["context_peak"], "would_have_stopped": false, "compactions": 0,
      "cost_usd_estimate": 0.0854731}}`.
      Expected: input 10, output 707, cache read 168,160, cache write 11,120; 7 turns; run time
      18 s; peak 36,494 tokens, derived from per-turn usage, 3.6 % of the window, 4.7 % of the
      compaction threshold; would have stopped: no; 0 compactions; estimate $0.09.
    - **A real line with nulls** (origin/main `status/outcomes.jsonl`, the source-checker session
      `Q-011:research-to-source:2026-10-08T16:59:31Z`, the first line after L-0005; quoted with
      `control` and `record_sha` shortened): `{"control": "2c0b…", "item": "Q-011", "record":
      "research/Q-011-source-check.md", "record_sha": "e60a…", "result": "ok", "role":
      "source-checker", "session": "Q-011:research-to-source:2026-10-08T16:59:31Z", "usage":
      {"autocompact_threshold": null, "cache_creation_input_tokens": 20367,
      "cache_read_input_tokens": 29181, "compactions": null, "context_peak": 20369,
      "context_window": 1000000, "cost_usd_estimate": 0.1560012, "derived": ["context_peak"],
      "duration_ms": 40999, "input_tokens": 8, "num_turns": 11, "output_tokens": 2418,
      "would_have_stopped": null}, "verdict": "PASS"}`.
      Expected: input 8, output 2,418, cache read 29,181, cache write 20,367; 11 turns; run time
      41 s; peak 20,369 tokens, derived from per-turn usage, 2.0 % of the window; % of the
      compaction threshold "not available"; would have stopped "not available"; compactions "not
      available"; estimate $0.16.

- **J11 — Project records → Home, Status, Team, Timeline and Queue (MD sandbox, page side; added
  2026-10-14).** v3 projects only; a v2.5 project shows J9's reduced view and "not recorded" for
  everything here, except `README.md`, which is read for every project (AC54, 2026-10-14).
  - **No new request form.** Every record below is a blob in the tree J1 already reads, fetched
    through J6's blob call by its sha, cached by sha, and fetched again only when its sha changes.
    J6's refusals and the function are unchanged. AC14's test is run with these reads included and
    still passes (AC42; A3 counts them).
  - **Optional, not needed:** none of these reads counts toward "read successfully" (Terms, AC4).
    A project whose J11 reads fail, or haven't arrived yet, is never "Can't read" for that reason:
    only the views that need them show "not available" until they arrive, and its waits and
    "Quiet" are judged on J6's needed reads alone. The new dispatch-log months are the exception:
    J6 already needs the latest two, and the older ones are optional.
  - **What the page adds to J6's blob list:**
    - `docs/PROJECT.md` (47,001 bytes at 3117150): section 3's milestone lines and section 9's line
      "The owner is the user.";
    - every `dispatch-log/<YYYY-MM>.jsonl`, not only the latest two months. A past month's sha
      doesn't change, so each is read once;
    - `status/merges.jsonl`, always (J6 reads it today only when a merge card is present);
    - the header lines of every `specs/S-*.md` (the whole blob is fetched; S-001.md is 173,985 bytes
      at 3117150 and is read only when its sha changes);
    - `intake/requests/Q-*.toml`, for the research question the model's timeline reads;
    - `governance/SURFACES.md`, for its roster block (the members, AC49);
    - `.github/workflows/orchestrator.yml`, for its `cron:` line (the Orchestrator's schedule);
    - for an open gate card, its case and the Critic's view, when the tree holds them (AC52).
    - *Added 2026-10-14 (AC54, trial-view notes 1):* `README.md` at the tree's root, for the brief
      project summary on each Home card. This one read is made for **every** connected project, a
      v2.5 project too; it is the only J11 read a v2.5 project makes. It is optional like the
      rest: it counts in A3's arithmetic, never toward "read successfully", and a failed or
      missing read shows only on the card's summary (AC54). Service-Desk's is 1,974 bytes at
      4433c4f.
    - *Added in draft 4 (AC70):* the answer record named by each boundary decision line that starts
      a run of AC57's runs 2 and later (`record`, for example `decisions/<item>/<gate>-<n>.md`),
      when the tree holds it: a few small blobs, read once per sha.
    - *Added 2026-10-16 (AC57 to AC61, trial-view notes 2):* every verdict file under
      `reviews/<n>/` in the tree, for each sprint pull request the Timeline shows (AC60's review
      mark): a small blob each, read once per sha. Names starting `_`, and README.md, are not
      read. Nothing else is added: docs/PROJECT.md, the research requests and
      `status/merges.jsonl` are already read, and the research memos are only looked for in the
      tree, never fetched.
  - **What the page reads from docs/PROJECT.md, from 2026-10-16** (AC59, AC61): besides section
    3's milestone lines and section 9's line, section 3's ` · why: ` ends and its standard-milestone
    lines (AC61 (a) and (b)), and the first two items of the list under `### Purpose` in
    `## Intent` (AC59's two cards). The why end is taken off a milestone line before the line is
    matched to its form, so a line with a why is never "out of form" for that reason. Until the
    model's own timeline (`v3_checks.py trace`) reads lines with a why, the fixture's `NOTE:`
    lines are made from J11's fixture as it is, and AC61's fixture, which has why lines, is
    checked against the same expected runs and sprints: a why changes neither.
  - **A milestone reached, from 2026-10-17** (model S-033 AC7 and its J4, adopted as L-0016).
    A project milestone line may carry ` · reached: <yyyy-mm-dd>`, after any ` · sprints:` and
    before any ` · dropped` or ` · why:`, exactly as the model's form has it
    (governance/checks/v3_checks.py:396-403, `OPTIONAL_PARTS_RE` and `MILESTONE_LINE_RE`). For
    example M1's line once marked: `- Every project's status visible on the phone (M1) · done
    when every connected project's status, and what waits on the owner, shows on the owner's
    phone · phase: Build · sprints: PR #40, PR #41 · reached: 2026-10-07`.
    - **The reader takes it** (src/lib/j11.js, `MILESTONE_LINE_RE` at :185-188 and
      `milestoneLines`, which skips a line it can't match at :236-239): the why end is taken
      off first, as AC61 (a) has it, then the line is matched with the reached part in that
      place, and the milestone carries `reached` (the date) beside `sprints` and `dropped`.
    - **Out of form, as the model has it:** a date that is not a real calendar date
      (`2026-02-30`; the model's `g.valid_date`, v3_checks.py:515), or the part in another place
      (before ` · sprints:`, after ` · dropped`, or after ` · why:`, where the why end, taken off
      first, holds it and is out of form for holding ` · `, AC61 (a)): the line is skipped with
      J11's note, the rest still shown, exactly where `v3_checks.py trace` fails it.
    - **Both reached and dropped** (the model reports it, v3_checks.py:1884): the line is read,
      shown as dropped (AC57's rule 1), and named in the project's notes ("marked both reached
      and dropped").
    - **What it changes:** only what AC50, AC57, AC59, AC65 and AC68 say: the milestone's state
      words and mark, and its counting as work filed for the now run and the next to reach. The
      model's timeline (`v3_checks.py trace`) places milestones and states the standard ones as
      before; it reads the part but does not change its `NOTE:` lines for it, so J11's fixture
      and its oracle stand.
    - **Who may write it:** only an owner's ruling, checked by the model's `v3_checks
      milestones` (S-033 AC7); the desk reads the line as the default branch holds it and
      checks no ruling (as for S-032's forms, AC61).
    - **Built first** (S-033's J4 and "Adoption by projects"): no plan line carries the part
      until this reader is merged and deployed to the preview by the owner's approval (the
      amendment of 2026-10-17). Until then today's reader would skip the line, and that
      milestone would drop off every screen.
  - **The timeline: the model's rules, not the desk's own.** Phase runs, their labels
    ("<Phase> i<n>"), the standard milestones' states, the project milestones' placing and sprints,
    and the Graduated mark follow model S-023 AC7's timeline. Its reference implementation is
    `governance/checks/v3_checks.py` (`Timeline`). It reads the same records:
    - `docs/PROJECT.md`, the dispatch log, the status files, `status/merges.jsonl`, the specs and
      the research requests;
    - a routed line's phase comes from its `stage` (1–2 Discovery, 3 Design, 4–5 Build, 6 Launch),
      and its iteration from the gate words that send work back through a gate, as the model's
      `iterations` does (governance/checks/orchestrator.py:299–317 and :127–130, model S-023 AC6).
    - `python3 governance/checks/v3_checks.py trace --root .` prints that timeline as `NOTE:` lines.
      For Service-Desk at 3117150 it gives the runs Discovery i1, Design i1, Build i1, Design i2,
      Build i2 and Launch i1, and the milestones placed in them (MD1, MD2 and MD3 in Design i2).
  - **The fixture (the test oracle).** The Builder commits, under `tests/desk/fixtures/`,
    Service-Desk's records at a fixed commit and the `NOTE:` lines that command printed for that
    commit. The tests for AC50 and AC51 check the desk's runs, labels, states and sprints against
    those lines.
    - The `NOTE:` lines mark no run as "now". So the fixture also holds the expected now run under
      AC50's rule, which is mine (C), not the model's: at 3117150 it is Design i2, because MD2 is
      its first project milestone with no sprint.
    - If the model's rules change in a later model version, the fixture is regenerated in the pull
      request that adopts that version.
  - **The Orchestrator's schedule.** The page reads the first `- cron: "<expr>"` line of
    `.github/workflows/orchestrator.yml` (today `*/30 * * * *`, line 17).
    - Only the form `*/<n> * * * *` is understood. The next run is the next UTC minute divisible by
      `<n>`, shown as "due <t> by its schedule": GitHub may start a scheduled run late (C).
    - Any other form, or a file it can't read, gives "due time not available".
  - **The roster.** The lines of `governance/SURFACES.md`'s ```` ```roster ```` block, each
    `<id> <class> <status> <authority>`, with `#` lines skipped. A missing or malformed block gives
    the members the dispatch log names, and a note.
  - **Links.** A file link is `https://github.com/<repo>/blob/<defaultBranch>/<path>`, made only for a
    path the tree holds. A pull request link is `https://github.com/<repo>/pull/<n>`. A merge's link
    is `https://github.com/<repo>/commit/<sha>`, with the `merge` sha from `status/merges.jsonl`. A
    workflow run link is that run's `html_url` from J1's list.
  - **Awkward cases:**
    - **No dispatch-log line for any project item:** no runs. The phases show "not recorded", as
      the model's timeline prints them with every standard milestone "not recorded".
    - **A milestone line out of form** in section 3: skipped with a note, as `v3_checks.py trace`
      fails it; the rest still show.
    - **A spec with no `milestones:` header:** it ties no merge to a milestone.
  - A test marked `join-test: S-001/J11` covers it, with the fixture above, a cron line in each
    form, and a malformed roster block. From 2026-10-14 it also checks that `README.md` is fetched
    for a v2.5 project as well as a v3 one, and that a failed README read leaves the project's
    "read successfully" and "Quiet" unchanged. From 2026-10-16 it also checks the why ends and
    standard lines of AC61's fixture, the Purpose items, and that the `reviews/<n>/` verdict files
    are fetched and the `_` files are not. From 2026-10-17 it uses **J11's reached fixture**
    (NOT a real record: J11's docs/PROJECT.md with M1's line ending ` · reached: 2026-10-07` and
    MD2's, which lists no sprints there, ending ` · reached: 2026-10-16`, the date AC50, AC57 and
    AC59 show as "16 Oct"; plus out-of-form lines: `2026-02-30`, the part before ` · sprints:`,
    after ` · dropped`, after ` · why:` (`… · why: <reason> · reached: 2026-10-07`, which the
    desk's `whyEnd` takes as a why holding ` · ` and the model's `MILESTONE_LINE_RE` fails; plan
    review round 1, N7)), with the model's verdict on each line recorded beside it
    (real-on-real: `milestone_lines` from governance/checks/v3_checks.py, run on the fixture, the
    command and its output listed in `tests/desk/fixtures/README.md`). The fixture is two files,
    since a well-formed line both reached and dropped changes `trace`'s `NOTE:` lines (draft 8;
    build review round 1, (a)):
    - tests/desk/fixtures/j11-reached/docs/PROJECT.md holds the lines above, and `v3_checks.py
      trace` on it prints J11's `NOTE:` lines unchanged;
    - tests/desk/fixtures/j11-reached/both/ adds MX5, a line with both ` · reached:` and
      ` · dropped`, and MX6, and the lines `trace` adds for them are recorded beside it in
      `tests/desk/fixtures/README.md`.

    It checks that the desk reads and skips exactly the lines the model's reader does, each date,
    a reached line with a why, the note for the line both reached and dropped (MX5), that `trace`
    on the first file prints J11's `NOTE:` lines unchanged, and that `trace` on the second prints
    them with exactly the recorded lines added.

- **J12 — Pull requests → Timeline sprints (MD sandbox; added 2026-10-16, AC60, trial-view notes 2).**
  v3 projects only. The Timeline needs, for each sprint that is a pull request, its title, body,
  state, merge time, head branch and check state, and the list of pull requests merged into the
  default branch, for those no milestone names (AC60). Today the desk reads only open pull
  requests (J1's `pulls?state=open`), so it has none of this for a merged one.
  - **Why not J1's REST list** (C): each pull request in GitHub's REST list is about 18 to 20 KB,
    because it carries the head and base repositories in full (tests/desk/fixtures/github/
    pulls-page1.json: two items of 17,602 and 20,114 characters). A hundred merged ones would be
    about 2 MB per page, through the function on every read. GitHub's GraphQL API returns only the
    fields asked for, a hundred to a page.
  - **The request:** one new request form, `POST https://api.github.com/graphql` with the read token
    (Setup G1). Its query text is fixed in the function; the page never sends query text. Its
    variables are the repository's owner and name and its default branch, all from J7, and
    `after`, a cursor the page passes back. It asks for the default branch's pull requests,
    newest first by creation, 100 to a page: for each, `number`, `title`, `body`, `state`,
    `createdAt`, `mergedAt`, `closedAt`, `headRefName`, `mergeCommit { oid }` (from draft 4 of
    trial-view notes 2, for AC72), from 2026-10-17 `mergeCommit { message author { name } }` as
    well (the owner's answer on the asks-merge job's merges, below), and the check rollup state of
    its last commit; and the page's `hasNextPage` and
    `endCursor`.
  - **Through the blob call** (J6), as an optional field: `"pulls": { "after": null | "<cursor>" }`.
    It counts 5 against the call's budget of 25 (C). The function refuses (400) a cursor that is
    not 1 to 200 characters of `A-Z`, `a-z`, `0-9`, `+`, `/`, `=`, `_` or `-`. It returns the
    answer's text raw, unparsed, as `"pulls": "<raw>" | null`, as it returns blobs; `null` is a
    failed read. Only a 401 stops the rest of the call; GraphQL's rate limit is the page's to judge
    (below; build review round 1, N1).
  - **When the page reads it:** after the needed reads, like J11's reads (optional; never counted
    toward "read successfully"):
    - **cold:** once when the project's records first load on the device, and again on the first
      load each day after midnight in `ownerTimeZone` (C; plan review round 2, N5: so a pull
      request outside the newest 100 that was kept as open and merged later reaches the Timeline
      within a day), following `hasNextPage` for at most 3 pages (300 pull requests, J1's
      `MAX_PAGES`), one page per blob call;
    - **on a head change** (a merge moves the head): the first page only, the newest 100. Each
      pull request on it replaces the one kept under its number; the older ones kept stay as they
      are (plan review round 1, N6; A3 counts this). So a body edited after its merge shows from
      the next head change only if the pull request is among the newest 100 (a known gap, C).
    An open pull request's body and title come from J1's open list on every poll, as today.
  - **What the page keeps** (N6): for each pull request, only what AC60, AC61 and AC72 use: its
    number, title, state, `mergedAt`, `createdAt`, head branch, merge commit, check state (or the
    field error's message), from 2026-10-17 whether its merge is the asks-merge job's (yes or no,
    below; never the message itself),
    and from its body only the `Milestone:` line and the Context and Why sections as AC61 (c)
    reads them; never the rest of the body. At most 300 are kept, the newest. They are kept on the
    device in a storage of their own, under the key `desk-pulls-v2` (from 2026-10-17, draft 5:
    the kept entry gains the job's yes or no, below, so the key moves on from `desk-pulls-v1`,
    which is removed on load as J6 drops its old keys, and every entry is read again from J12;
    an entry that still lacks the field is read as a merge J12 has not yet read: placed only
    under a plan line that lists it, never by its `Milestone:` line or under "Not tied to a
    milestone", and giving no run a time, until J12 reads it again (AC60; plan review round 4,
    note 3; round 5, note 2)), wrapped as J5's storage is
    but apart from the blob cache, so that a write that fails there (a full or blocked storage)
    turns off only this key, never the blob cache (src/lib/store.js turns off a whole store on
    one failed write); then they are held in memory for the open window and read again on the
    next load.
  - **What the page uses:** every pull request whose base is the default branch, whatever its
    state; a merged one is one whose `mergedAt` is set (N6). The check rollup state gives AC60's
    checks mark: `SUCCESS`; `FAILURE` or `ERROR`; `PENDING` or `EXPECTED`; none.
  - **The answer's own errors** (N6). The function does not parse the answer, so the page judges
    it:
    - a 200 answer whose `data` is `null` or missing, or that holds no `repository`, is a failed
      read, with the first error's `message` as the reason;
    - an error whose `type` is `RATE_LIMITED`, in a 200 answer or a 403, is a rate limit for J12
      only: the page makes no J12 read for 60 minutes, then reads again (C; build review round 1,
      N2: waiting for a head change too would starve a quiet project). The REST polls are not
      slowed by it;
    - **any other failed J12 read is tried again with a backoff** (from 2026-10-17, the desk
      follow-ups; build review round 2 of trial-view notes 2, N1). That covers a `null` answer,
      a refusal, a network failure, a blob call that fails, and the `data: null` answer above.
      Retrying on every cycle could double a project's function calls and break AC14's margin.
      - **The waits:** the next J12 read comes 5 minutes after the first failure, then 10, 20
        and 40 minutes after each further failure in a row, then every 60 minutes until a read
        answers.
      - **While it waits,** the page makes no J12 read, head change or not, as for a rate limit.
        The read made when the wait ends is whatever is due then: a cold read from the first
        page, or the first page for a head change.
      - **A read that answers resets it:** the next failure waits 5 minutes again.
      - **A `RATE_LIMITED` answer** keeps its plain 60-minute wait, and it neither advances nor
        resets the backoff (C). Since no wait in the backoff is longer than 60 minutes, a rate
        limit can only lengthen the waits.
      - **The backoff lives in memory** for the open window, not on the device, so each opening
        starts it at 5 minutes (C). A3 counts that.
      - **Testable:** the waits are measured on the scheduler's clock, so a test drives them with
        the fake clock the scheduler already takes (`ac-test: S-001/AC14` and
        `join-test: S-001/J12`, below);
    - an error whose `path` ends in the check rollup field, with the rest of `data` present: the
      rest is used, and each checks mark reads "– checks" with "the desk's token can't read
      checks" one tap away (AC60).
  - **The asks-merge job's merges** (from 2026-10-17, the owner's answer
    "B. Off the Timeline (Recommended)", decisions/questions/P-001-asks-merge-on-desk.md; AC60's "never a sprint", unless a plan line lists it).
    - **The rule:** a merged pull request's merge is the job's when both hold:
      - the merge commit's message has a final trailer paragraph, found as the model's
        `parse_trailers` finds it (governance/checks/govlib.py:660, following `git
        interpret-trailers --parse`; in short, the last paragraph after the title, every line of
        it a trailer or a continuation. That summary is partial: the function also turns CRLF
        into LF, skips trailing `#` comment lines, and accepts a final paragraph holding a
        git-generated line, such as `Signed-off-by:` or "(cherry picked from commit", when at
        least a quarter of it is trailers. The desk follows the function, not the summary), and
        that paragraph's `Merged-By` trailers (the key matched
        without regard to case, the value stripped) are exactly one, `orchestrator`, as S-033 AC4
        writes it and `merged_by_problems` checks it (governance/checks/asks_merge.py:371-382);
      - the merge commit's `author.name` is `github-actions[bot]`, as the model also requires
        (`BOT`, asks_merge.py:76, checked in `merged_by_problems`, :371-382). It comes in the same query, from the same `mergeCommit`
        object, so it costs nothing more. A merge with the trailer but another author is not the
        job's: the desk places it as any pull request, and the model's `record_checks merges`
        fails it on the default branch, so it shows red in CI, never silently.
    - **Not the job's:** a `Merged-By: orchestrator` line anywhere but the final trailer paragraph
      (for example a description that quotes it mid-text, or a final paragraph holding a line
      that is not a trailer), a `Merged-By` with another value, two `Merged-By` trailers, or a
      `null` merge commit. Each is placed as any merged pull request (AC60).
    - **Size, not count:** the repository's merge message is the pull request's description
      (docs/SETUP.md step 5), and the job writes its own the same way (S-033 AC4), so each merged
      pull request's description is sent twice in J12's answer, once as `body` and once in the
      merge commit's message. In the newest 100 at 21bc250, 77 merged descriptions total about
      127 KB, the largest 5,655 characters (plan review round 3 of model 3.5.0's records, N1), so
      a page grows by about that much. The function passes it on raw, as today. **No request is
      added**: the fields ride the same query, and GraphQL's cost counts connections, not fields,
      so a page still costs about 1 point; A3 and AC14 are unchanged. The page reads the message
      for its trailers and keeps only the yes or no.
    - **While open,** nothing changes: J1's open list is what the Queue shows (Terms, AC2, AC52).
    - **Not known yet** (C): GraphQL's `author.name` for the job's merges is taken to be the git
      author name REST gives (`github-actions[bot]` at 21bc250), since the agents' sessions can't
      reach GraphQL; Setup CS13's recording confirms it.
  - **Not known yet** (C): whether the read token's permissions (Setup G1: Contents, Metadata,
    Pull requests and Actions, each read) let GraphQL give a commit's check rollup. The desk shows
    which it is to the owner, on every checks mark, with its reason one tap away (AC60). The owner
    or the Chief of Staff records it in Setup CS13, outside these sessions (N7). No token
    permission is changed for it.
  - **Awkward cases:**
    - **More than 300 pull requests:** the older ones are not read, and the Timeline says so once,
      at the bottom: "older pull requests not read (over 300)". A milestone's sprint among them
      shows its code with "not available".
    - **A refusal, a rate limit or a network failure:** every pull request part of AC60 shows "not
      available", with the reason one tap away (AC41). The project is never "Can't read" for it.
    - **A body edited after the merge:** see "on a head change" above (a known gap, C).
    - **A sprint list naming a pull request J12 does not hold** (another base, or a number that is
      an issue): its code with "not recorded in what the desk reads".
  - **GitHub's limits:** GraphQL has its own hourly budget, apart from REST's (5,000 points); a
    page of this query costs about 1 point (E). Three cold loads an hour across 5 projects, at 3
    pages each, is at most 45 points.
  - **The fixture** (plan review round 1, B3): GraphQL is not reachable from the agents' sessions
    (`gh api graphql` answers 403 there: "GitHub GraphQL is not available from Claude Code
    sessions; use the REST API"). So the Builder builds J12's fixture in GraphQL's answer shape
    from REST answers for Service-Desk, a public repository: `gh api
    'repos/shumpmaster/Service-Desk/pulls?state=all&per_page=100'` (and its later pages), each
    pull request's fields mapped to the query's names (`merged_at` → `mergedAt`, `head.ref` →
    `headRefName` and so on), and the check state from `gh api
    repos/shumpmaster/Service-Desk/commits/<head sha>/check-runs` for the few pull requests the
    tests need. It is kept under `tests/desk/fixtures/github/` and listed in
    `tests/desk/fixtures/README.md` as **NOT as GitHub sent it**: built from REST answers, with
    the date and commands. The answers with an error (a `data: null` answer, a `RATE_LIMITED`
    one, and the field error for the check rollup) are **made up**, in the shape GitHub's
    GraphQL documentation gives, and listed so. A real recording replaces the built one once
    Setup CS13 has made it.
    - **For the asks-merge job's merges** (from 2026-10-17): **real-on-real**, #84's entry with its
      `mergeCommit` built from `gh api repos/shumpmaster/Service-Desk/commits/21bc250`
      (`commit.message` → `message`, `commit.author.name` → `author.name`), and the model's
      `govlib.parse_trailers` run on that message, its output listed beside it in
      `tests/desk/fixtures/README.md` (`[('Merged-By', 'orchestrator'), ('Delegation', 'L-0017'),
      ('Merged-Head', '6c34acb6a72d6d94c791c65e4084269d9a6a3974')]` expected); and **made up**,
      each with `parse_trailers`' output beside it: a description that quotes `Merged-By:
      orchestrator` mid-text with an ordinary final paragraph, the trailer paragraph with the
      author `shumpmaster`, `Merged-By: someone`, two `Merged-By` trailers, a final paragraph
      mixing the trailer with a line that is not a trailer (`parse_trailers` gives `[]`), a job's
      merge whose body names MD3, and, from draft 5 (plan review round 4, note 2), two the
      function still reads as the job's: a message whose trailer paragraph is followed by a `#`
      comment line, and a final paragraph holding `Signed-off-by: A <a@x>`, `Merged-By:
      orchestrator` and two lines that are not trailers (`[('Signed-off-by', 'A <a@x>'),
      ('Merged-By', 'orchestrator')]`), each with the author `github-actions[bot]`.
  - A test marked `join-test: S-001/J12` covers it, with that fixture: the refused cursor, the
    budget, `null` on a failed read, the three-page limit and its note, a head change reading the
    first page only, each of the answer's own errors above, the kept fields only (no body text
    beyond AC61's parts), a failed write to the kept list's key leaving the blob cache usable, and that
    the function sends the fixed query text only. From 2026-10-17 (the desk follow-ups) it also
    checks the backoff with a fake clock:
    - after failures in a row, the next J12 read comes 5, 10, 20 and 40 minutes later, then 60
      and 60 again;
    - no J12 read comes in between, with a head change in a wait included;
    - a read that answers resets it, so the next failure waits 5 minutes;
    - a `RATE_LIMITED` answer waits 60 minutes and leaves the step as it was;
    - a page reload starts the backoff again at 5 minutes.

    From 2026-10-17 (the owner's answer on the asks-merge job's merges) it also checks, with that
    fixture: the fixed query text asks for `mergeCommit { oid message author { name } }`; #84's
    merge is the job's; the description quoting the line mid-text, the other author, the other
    value, the two trailers and the mixed final paragraph are not; the message whose trailer
    paragraph is followed by a `#` comment line and the final paragraph mixing `Signed-off-by:`
    with two lines that are not trailers are the job's; the desk's yes or no agrees
    with `parse_trailers`' output and the author for every message in the fixture; the kept
    entry holds the yes or no and never the message; from draft 5 the kept list is under
    `desk-pulls-v2`, and `desk-pulls-v1` is removed on load and its entries read again from J12;
    and the number of requests per J12 read is unchanged.

- **J13 — The Chief of Staff's session lines → Team screen and who has the ball (MD sandbox, page
  side; added 2026-10-17, AC49, AC67; model S-033 AC6 and its J3, adopted as L-0016).** v3
  projects only; a v2.5 project keeps "not recorded in this repository's records (operating
  model v2.5.1)" (AC49).
  - **Where:** every path in the tree (J1) matching
    `^sessions/chief-of-staff/([A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,100})\.jsonl$`, one file per session, the group
    being its session id (the model's `SESSION_FILE_RE`, governance/checks/v3_checks.py:2053).
    Any other path under `sessions/` is ignored. Only the default branch's tree is read: a line
    in an open pull request shows once that pull request is merged (S-033's Known limits: "The
    desk shows a piece of work once its start line lands").
  - **No new request form.** Each file is a blob of the tree, added to J11's optional reads:
    fetched through J6's blob call by its sha, cached by sha, read again only when its sha
    changes, never counted toward "read successfully" (Terms, AC4). A failed or missing read
    shows only on the Chief of Staff's tile, log and sheet, as "not available".
  - **The lines** (S-033 AC6; the model's `SESSION_KEYS` and `REPORTED_KEYS`,
    v3_checks.py:2054-2057), one JSON object per line, with exactly these keys:
    - **start:** `{"event": "start", "session": "<id>", "time": "<UTC>", "doing": "<plain
      words>"}`. Real sample, the first start line on the default branch
      (sessions/chief-of-staff/session_012RJPVKAoLga2RkqLYRDcBY.jsonl, line 1, added by 21bc250,
      the asks-merge job's merge of pull request #84): `{"event": "start", "session":
      "session_012RJPVKAoLga2RkqLYRDcBY", "time": "2026-10-17T22:11:23Z", "doing": "Revising the
      desk plan (S-001) so the desk reads the records model 3.5.0 added: open questions with their
      milestone and facts, the Chief of Staff sessions, and milestones reached"}`;
    - **helper:** `{"event": "helper", "session": "<id>", "role": "<agent name>", "started":
      "<UTC>", "ended": "<UTC>", "reported": {"tokens": int|null, "tool_uses": int|null,
      "duration_ms": int|null}}`;
    - **end:** `{"event": "end", "session": "<id>", "time": "<UTC>", "usage": <S-020's usage
      object>}`, the usage object in J10's form, `duration_ms` the time from its start line and
      every figure the session cannot read `null`.
    - A time is UTC ISO-8601 as the model reads it: `yyyy-mm-ddThh:mm:ss`, an optional fraction,
      then `Z` or `+00:00` (`UTC_RE`, v3_checks.py:2058).
  - **Which of the model's findings the desk mirrors** (plan review round 1, N5). The model's
    `v3_checks sessions` (S-033 AC6: `_one_object`, `_count` and `session_line_problems`,
    governance/checks/v3_checks.py:2073-2140) fails a line in the project's own CI. The page
    reads each line with `JSON.parse`, and:
    - **mirrors these, naming the line in the project's notes (file, line, reason) and skipping
      it, as AC31 does, the rest of the file still used:**
      - a line that is not one JSON object (`NaN` and `Infinity` included: `JSON.parse` refuses
        them, as the model's `parse_constant` does);
      - `event` missing, not a string, or not `start`, `helper` or `end`;
      - a key missing or extra for its event;
      - `session` not a string, or not the file's id;
      - `doing` or `role` not a string holding a character other than white space;
      - `time`, `started` or `ended` not in `UTC_RE`'s form, or not a real date and time;
      - `reported` not an object, a key of it missing or extra, or a figure of it neither `null`
        nor a whole number of 0 or more (`true` and `false` are not figures, as `_count` has it);
      - a helper whose `started` is after its `ended`;
    - **reads leniently, by one rule, and does not skip:** an end line's `usage` (below);
    - **does not mirror, and reads as written** (each passes on the page and fails in the
      project's CI, which is where it is caught: a known limit, C):
      - a repeated key: `JSON.parse` keeps the last, where `_one_object` fails the line;
      - a whole number written with a fraction or an exponent, such as `1.0` or `1e3`:
        `JSON.parse` gives a whole number, where `_count`'s `type(v) is int` fails it; and a whole
        number above 2^53, which `JSON.parse` rounds;
      - a start or end line whose `time` is earlier than the previous start or end line's: the
        desk checks no order;
      - a line changed or removed since a base: the desk has no base;
      - a file under `sessions/chief-of-staff/` whose name is not a session id and `.jsonl`: the
        path rule above ignores it, where the model fails it;
      - a file that is not valid UTF-8: the page decodes it with replacement characters.
  - **An end line's `usage`: one rule, J10's** (plan review round 1, N5). The `usage` object is
    read as J10 reads a session's `usage`: a figure of the wrong kind marks only its key, which
    reads "not recorded" and is named in the notes; a key missing reads "not recorded" and is
    named; an extra key is ignored and named; and a `usage` that is not an object gives every
    figure "not recorded" and is named. J10's looser bounds hold (no upper bound, no exact
    `derived` value). In every case the end line still closes its piece of work at its `time`,
    since its `event`, `session` and `time` are in form: skipping the whole line would keep
    "Chief of Staff working" for up to 8 hours because of one figure, and who has the ball would
    be wrong. The model's check still fails such a line in CI (`usage_problem`,
    governance/checks/session_runner.py:1033-1059).
  - **Pieces of work** (S-033's J3), read in each file's line order:
    - a start line opens a piece of work; the next end line of the file closes it, at its
      `time`;
    - a start that follows a start with no end between them closes the earlier piece as
      "ended, time not recorded" (S-033 AC6);
    - **an end line with no open piece** (before any start in its file, or after an end with no
      start between, both of which the model allows): it closes nothing. It shows as its own row
      on the log's main line, "Handed back (no recorded start)", with its figures, and it counts
      for "just finished" (C: it records that the Chief of Staff handed work back to you at its
      `time`, whatever came before it);
    - a helper line belongs to the piece most recently opened before it in the file, and to
      none when no start comes before it (then it shows on its own row, "a helper with no
      recorded piece of work");
    - **under way:** a piece with no end and no later start, whose start `time` is less than
      **8 hours** before now. From 8 hours it reads "ended, time not recorded". 8 hours is this
      spec's limit, since S-033's J3 leaves it to the reader's spec ("A reader may treat a start
      older than its own limit with no end as ended, time not recorded; that limit is the reader's
      spec's"), my choice (C), a constant to tune in the lab like AC49's 30 minutes, which the owner
      kept (design-lab answer 8, "Keep", decisions/questions/P-001-design-lab-open.md): long
      enough for a long piece of work with its helpers within one sitting, short enough that one
      left open in the evening has stopped reading "Working" by the morning. A start `time`
      later than now (a clock ahead) is under way.
    - **just finished:** the latest end line's `time`, any end line's, is under 30 minutes old
      (AC49).
    - **For AC77** (from 2026-10-18, draft 4): whether the session's file holds an end line, in
      form by the rules above, whose `time` is after a given instant (a question's raised time).
      The same reader answers it, on the page and in a sender (J16).
  - **Figures** (AC23's exception for session lines): `null` reads "not recorded", never "not
    available", both in an end line's `usage` and a helper's `reported`. A whole number shows as
    AC23 shows it: `duration_ms` rounded to the second, `tokens` as one total (a helper reports
    no split into input and output, S-033's Known limits), `tool_uses` as a count. Any other
    `usage` figure that is not `null` shows as AC23 shows it, `cost_usd_estimate` as "estimate
    $x.xx", on the row's summary sheet and, once the piece has ended, on the Working now sheet's
    Cost (AC49). The Chief of Staff's own tokens and cost are always `null` today, so they read
    "not recorded".
  - **Times** show as J9's `<t>`, in `ownerTimeZone`. A duration shown is a figure from the
    line or now less a recorded time, labelled as AC49's Working now sheet labels it.
  - **Links:** a row's "From the record" opens its session file by AC51's link rule.
  - **Awkward cases:**
    - **No session file in the tree:** "No session recorded yet" (AC49), and nothing in who has
      the ball.
    - **Two sessions under way at once:** each is a lane in the log; who has the ball says
      "Chief of Staff working" once; the tile names the piece started last (AC49).
    - **An end line that rides a later pull request** (S-033 AC6, "When the lines land"): the
      piece reads "Working" until its end line lands or the 8 hours pass, a known limit (C).
    - **A helper line naming a roster member** (`role` equal to a roster id, J11's roster):
      also that member's recorded work (AC49, AC67).
  - **The fixture:**
    - **real:** the start line above, at 21bc250 (the first session file on the default
      branch);
    - **real-on-real** (NOT real records, but the model's own check passes them, the command and
      its output listed in `tests/desk/fixtures/README.md`): session files written in these forms
      and passed by `python3 governance/checks/v3_checks.py sessions` in a fixture root, covering
      one piece ended, one under way, one followed by a second start, one past 8 hours, two
      sessions under way at once, an end line before any start and one after an end, helpers
      with figures and with `null`s (a `definer` helper and one whose role the roster lacks), and
      an end line's usage with `duration_ms` and every other figure `null`;
    - **out-of-form lines, each with the model's failure recorded beside it** (the same command's
      output): one for each finding the desk mirrors, which the desk names and skips; one for each
      it does not (a repeated key, `1.0` for a count, a time earlier than the line before), which
      the desk reads as written; and an end line whose `usage` has a figure of the wrong kind and
      one whose `usage` is not an object, which the desk reads by J10's rule.
  - A test marked `join-test: S-001/J13` covers it, with that fixture and a fake clock: each
    piece's state at 7 h 59 min and at 8 h; each line's words and figures; "not recorded" for
    every `null`; each mirrored out-of-form line named in the notes and skipped while the rest of
    its file is used; each line the desk does not mirror read as written, with the model's
    failure in the fixture beside it; an end line with a wrong-kind usage figure closing its
    piece, that figure alone "not recorded" and named; an end line with no open piece giving its
    own row and "just finished"; the path rule (a file under `sessions/` outside
    `chief-of-staff/`, and a name with a `.`, ignored); and that a failed session blob leaves
    "read successfully" and "Quiet" unchanged.

- **J16 — Project records → a sender's waiting set (Workflows 3 and 4; added 2026-10-18, draft 3,
  AC73, AC77).**
  - **Who reads:** `src/notify/run.mjs`, a Node program with no dependencies (Toolchain), run by
    each project's own workflow for that project alone: Workflow 3 for Service-Desk, Workflow 4 for
    Personal-Org-Operating-Model. It builds the set with the desk's own readers in `src/lib/`
    (J2's, J9's, J13's and J20's), so the set is the desk's, not a second reckoning (C). It never
    calls the desk's function, which is behind Access, and never reads another project.
  - **How it reads** (C): its own project from the workflow's checkout of the default branch, with
    its history (`fetch-depth: 0`), never a pull request's head; then, with the workflow's own
    token, REST: the open pull requests (J1's list) and, for each from the repository's own
    branches, its files (J20); the workflow runs whose `status` is `waiting`
    (`actions/runs?status=waiting`); the latest `governance.yml` run on the default branch's tip,
    for whether a hold card is open (J2's rule); and, for each job candidate (below), its check
    runs, statuses and workflow runs at its head, and the latest asks-merge runs and their jobs.
    Whether an open merge card's tip is merged (J2's rule) is answered from the checkout (`git
    merge-base --is-ancestor`). About 10 to 20 requests a run (E), against the workflow token's own
    hourly limit, not the owner's (A3).
    - **The budget guard** (review N4; C): the workflow token's limit is per repository and shared
      with the asks-merge job and the Orchestrator. The sender reads `X-RateLimit-Remaining` on its
      first answer; below 300, it makes no further read this run, sends nothing and uploads no
      state, so the next run, with the same last state, does the work; the runs themselves are
      bounded by Workflow 3's and 4's gates and one-at-a-time concurrency. Worst case, every run in
      an hour reading its full 20 is about 60 runs (one finishing a minute) × 20 = 1,200, which the
      guard cuts off at the limit's last 300.
    - Questions' raised times (AC77) come from the checkout's history: the oldest commit of the
      file, `git log --diff-filter=A --format=%cI -- questions/<name>.md`, its last line (AC20's
      "oldest commit"); and the end lines that end a hold from the checkout's
      `sessions/chief-of-staff/` files, read by J13's reader, an end line counting only when its
      `time` is after that raised time (AC77).
  - **The set:** AC73's kinds, each with its key. A v3 project's cards, hold cards, merge cards,
    status files, questions and answers are read as J2 reads them; a v2.5 project's questions as
    J2's owner questions read them in either model; pull requests by Terms' rule; deploys by `status` `waiting`, only where J7 marks
    the project `deploysNeedOwner`; and AC77's held and being-recorded questions left out.
  - **The asks-merge job's pull requests** (the owner's ruling,
    decisions/questions/P-001-notify-job-merges.md). In a project whose default branch holds
    `.github/workflows/asks-merge.yml` and a standing asks-merge grant in docs/LEDGER.md (a
    `type: delegation` entry whose `decision:` begins `asks-merge:` and that no later entry
    supersedes, as `find_grant` reads it, asks_merge.py:334), a pull request is a **job
    candidate** when its head branch is in the repository, its base is the default branch, and
    every path in J20's file list is one of the four kinds by path (asks_merge.py:29-38, with
    `QUESTION_RE`, `ANSWER_RE` and `SESSION_RE` at :81-83): an added `questions/<name>.md`, a
    deleted `questions/<name>.md`, an added `decisions/questions/<name>.md`, each `<name>` holding
    no `/` and not starting with `_`; or an added or changed
    `sessions/chief-of-staff/<id>.jsonl`. This mirrors the job's kinds by path only (C); its finer
    rules are left to the job itself, and seen through its runs, (b) below.
    - **A job candidate waits on the owner** from the first of these (C):
      - (a) the latest run of a counted check on its head (one per key, as `check_state` keeps
        only the latest, asks_merge.py:236-247) finishes with any conclusion but `success`, or the
        latest status of a counted context turns `failure` or `error`, counted as the job counts
        them: every check run but those of `.github/workflows/orchestrator.yml`, every status but
        `owner-questions` (asks_merge.py:74-75);
      - (b) **the job has left it** (review B2 and N8; C): every counted check on its head has
        completed with `success`, and an asks-merge run that started after the last of them
        finished (started by `workflow_run`, `schedule` or `workflow_dispatch`) has a `merge` job
        that ran (read from the run's jobs; a run whose job was skipped or cancelled does not
        count, since asks-merge.yml:37 skips every run not caused by a pull request) and whose log
        holds the line `PR #<n>: waits: <reason>` for this pull request (the job's own words,
        asks_merge.py:632, read from the job's log, `GET
        /repos/<repo>/actions/jobs/<job id>/logs`, with `actions: read`).
        - **The log's form** (review round 3, N3; C): GitHub's job log is plain text, and each line
          starts with a timestamp, such as `2026-10-18T12:50:29.1234567Z `, before the program's own
          output. The sender takes each line's text after a leading `^\d{4}-\d\d-\d\dT[0-9:.]+Z `
          (or the whole line when it has none) and matches `^PR #(\d+): waits: (.+)$` on that
          text. Its test reads one real asks-merge job log (CS18).
        - **The reason, at once:** a reason the job will not clear by itself leaves the pull
          request at once: anything but a reason holding "cannot be read", one starting "it could
          not be judged" or "GitHub refused the merge" (the job skips those until its next run,
          asks_merge.py:49), "no counted check run on the head yet", or a check run or status not
          yet completed.
        - **A retried reason that lasts** (review round 3, N4; C): one of those the job retries (a
          reason holding "cannot be read", such as "cannot be read as text" for a file that is
          not text, asks_merge.py:149, one starting "it could not be judged", or "GitHub refused
          the merge", which a merge conflict gives on every run, asks_merge.py:636-641) leaves the
          pull request when a judging run that started 15 minutes or more after the first run
          that gave a reason of these three for it on the same head gives one again. J18 keeps when
          a run of this sender first read such a reason for that head. The job's 15-minute
          schedule makes such a later run likely within 15 to 30 minutes.
        - A run that judged nothing (the open pull requests unread, no grant: `return 0` with no
          `PR #` line, asks_merge.py:609-620) does not count, and nor does a log that cannot be
          read. The pull request must still be open with the same head;
      - (c) 30 minutes with no counted check started on its head, counted from the later of the
        head commit's time and the first run of this sender that saw that head (kept in J18);
      - (d) the grant no longer stands.

      Until then it is not in the set. A new head starts it again. A pull request that is no job
      candidate (any other path) waits from its opening, as any pull request. The sender never
      merges, and never says a pull request "will be merged".
    - In a project with no grant (Personal-Org-Operating-Model at 90f53e3 has neither the
      workflow nor a grant), no pull request is a job candidate.
  - **Awkward cases:**
    - **a read fails:** the project's set is "not read" this run, and J18 keeps its last set
      (AC76);
    - **J20 lists 300 files or more** (the job's own cap, `COMPARE_FILE_CAP`): no job candidate,
      so it waits from its opening;
    - **a record that can't be parsed:** activity, as on the desk, never in the set;
    - **a deploy run `queued` when it is requested:** read again every 15 seconds for up to 2
      minutes (C); still not `waiting`, the next run finds it;
    - **a pull request turned into a draft and back, or closed and reopened, between two runs:**
      not seen to stop, so not new (a known limit, AC73).
  - A test marked `join-test: S-001/J16` covers it: with Service-Desk's real records at ef8daf4 as
    a checkout fixture, the set the sender builds equals the set the desk's page builds from the
    same records through J2 (real-on-real: the same `src/lib` readers, two input layers); the job
    candidate's four kinds against the model's `classify` (asks_merge.py:117) on the same paths,
    every case `classify` reports no problem for being a candidate (real-on-real; the command and
    its output in tests/desk/fixtures/README.md); rule (b)'s reading of the job's log against the
    model's own `run` (asks_merge.py:600), driven by a fake API to give each of its waits reasons,
    each read as leaving it or not as above (real-on-real), with each line written into a log in
    GitHub's form, a timestamp before it; one real asks-merge job log, recorded once (CS18) and
    listed as real in tests/desk/fixtures/README.md, read with no `PR #` line mistaken and, where
    it holds one, that line found; a made-up log built from the real one's form with a `waits`
    line added (NOT real), found; a log line with no timestamp, found; a retried reason given
    twice on the same head 14 minutes apart (not left) and 15 minutes apart (left), on a new head
    (starting again), and a lasting conflict (left); each of (a) to (d) with made-up runs,
    jobs, logs and statuses (NOT real records), including a skipped and a cancelled asks-merge run,
    a run that judged nothing, a transient reason, a cancelled check re-run to success, and a head
    commit older than the pull request; the budget guard at 299 remaining; `find_grant`'s verdict on the real ledger at ef8daf4
    matched; a v2.5 project in poom/'s placeholder form; and a failed read.

- **J17 — A sender → the push service → the desk on the phone (Workflows 3 and 4; added
  2026-10-18, draft 3, AC74, AC76).**
  - **The subscription:** the secret `DESK_PUSH_SUBSCRIPTION`, the JSON the desk showed (J19):
    `{"endpoint": "<https URL>", "expirationTime": null, "keys": {"p256dh": "<base64url>", "auth":
    "<base64url>"}}` (V2). A value that isn't that form stops the run at its first step, naming
    the secret, never printing it.
  - **The keys' check, first** (B1's fix): the public half of `DESK_VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY` (a PEM of a
    P-256 key, Setup N1) must equal J7's `push.vapidPublicKey`; if not, the run stops and opens
    AC76's issue, "the notification keys don't match".
  - **VAPID** (V1; RFC 8292): a JWT signed ES256, its `aud` the endpoint's origin, its `sub` the
    secret `DESK_VAPID_SUBJECT` (`mailto:<the owner's address>`, a secret so the address is in no
    public file), sent as `Authorization: vapid t=<jwt>, k=<J7's public key>`.
    - **Refreshed at most hourly** (V8, B: Apple asks senders to refresh the JWT at most hourly)
      (C): a run mints a JWT only when the state holds none, or one minted 60 minutes ago or more;
      its `exp` is 2 hours after it was minted (within V1's 24 and within Apple's "more than one
      day into the future", which is a `BadJwtToken`). It is kept in J18's state, encrypted with
      AES-256-GCM under a key derived from `DESK_NOTIFY_STATE_KEY`, so the public artifact does
      not expose it. Apple's page, read 2026-10-18: "Don't refresh your JWT more frequently than
      once per hour" (Facts relied on). A 403 whose `reason` is `BadJwtToken` mints a new JWT only
      if the last was minted 60 minutes ago or more (AC76). EXP-006 case 8 checks that Apple
      accepts a reused JWT.
  - **The payload,** encrypted for the subscription with `aes128gcm` (V3; RFC 8291), with Node's
    built-in crypto only, in the Declarative Web Push form (D1), which the service worker also
    reads (C): `{"web_push": 8030, "notification": {"title": "<title>", "body": "<body>",
    "navigate": "<deskUrl>/?open=<key>", "tag": "<topic>"}}`, under 4,096 bytes (V4). The `tag`
    sits inside `notification`, so it reaches the phone on both paths, if Declarative Web Push
    honours it; EXP-006 case 6 checks it, and if it does not, a repeat on that path may add a
    notification rather than replace one (a known limit).
  - **Headers** (V5, V6): `TTL: 86400` (C), `Urgency: high` (C), `Topic: <topic>`, where `<topic>`
    is the first 32 characters of the base64url HMAC of the key (J18), so a resent push replaces
    one still waiting in the push service (V6); `Content-Encoding: aes128gcm`.
  - **The answers** (V7; Apple's page): `201` sent; `429` and `5xx` retried (AC76); `410` the
    subscription expired and `404` an invalid `:path`, both treated as the subscription gone;
    `400`, `401` and `403` the request or the signature refused, with Apple's JSON `reason` read
    when present (AC76, never a stop); `413` too large, sent again once with the body cut to 60 characters. The push service
    never sees the subscription's `p256dh` or `auth` (RFC 8291), so a wrong one gives 201 and a push
    the phone cannot read; the sender never sends one.
  - **The tests from GitHub:** `workflow_dispatch` with `test: true` sends "Test from your desk:
    notifications work", opening Home; with `test: fail` it makes exactly one request, a push
    signed with a throwaway VAPID key made in that run and sent as `k=`, with no retry, no new JWT
    and no other send in that run, so the push service refuses it (on Apple, 403 with a `reason`
    such as `VapidPkHashMismatch`) and nothing reaches the phone; AC76's issue opens, marked
    "(test)", and closes on the next good push (C). It never sends a push the phone cannot read
    (W8).
  - A test marked `join-test: S-001/J17` covers it: the encryption against RFC 8291's worked
    example in its appendix, byte for byte, where the RFC gives one (C: the Builder confirms it;
    otherwise a round trip, decrypting with Node's crypto and the subscription's private key); the
    JWT's header, claims and an ES256 signature that verifies with the public key; minting at most
    hourly, and a new JWT after a `BadJwtToken` 403 only when an hour has passed; the keys' check;
    `test: fail` making exactly one request; the headers; the payload's form and size;
    each answer code; `test: fail` signing with a key that is not J7's; and that no log line holds
    the endpoint, a key, the JWT or the subject.

- **J18 — A sender's memory between runs (Workflows 3 and 4; added 2026-10-18, draft 3, AC74,
  AC76).** No database and no Cloudflare service, as ruled.
  - **Where** (C): one workflow artifact, `desk-notify-state`, uploaded at the end of each run that
    built a state (the upload step runs only when the program wrote the file, so a run stopped at
    its first step uploads nothing), kept 7 days.
  - **Taken only from the sender's own runs** (round 1, B3; round 2, Critic B3; research Q-014):
    a `workflow_run`-started run has `GITHUB_REF` main and runs main's file (Q-014 F1.1, F1.3, A),
    and the runs list files it under main (F1.4 and F3.1, C, observed; the REST `branch=` filter
    giving the same is F3.3, UNKNOWN, EXP-006 case 10). So the lookup does not rest on a branch
    filter. The next run lists its own workflow's runs by its file
    (`actions/workflows/desk-notify.yml/runs?status=success&per_page=100`, one page only, so the
    search is bounded: C), newest first. `status=success` lists only runs that concluded
    `success` (GitHub's REST reference for listing a workflow's runs takes a conclusion as
    `status`; C, EXP-006 case 10 checks it), so the many runs whose job the origin gate skipped,
    which conclude `skipped`, never fill the page (review round 3, N5); with `status=completed`
    as its fallback if the filter is refused. It takes the newest run that: has `path`
    `.github/workflows/desk-notify.yml`; has `repository` and `head_repository` this repository;
    has `head_branch` `main`; has an `event` among the workflow's own triggers; and uploaded
    `desk-notify-state`. It reads that run's artifact (`actions/runs/<id>/artifacts`), checking the
    artifact's `workflow_run` id against the run. None in that page, with the list answering, is
    "no state" (below). The HMAC is the real guard: a run elsewhere, even one passing those
    checks, cannot sign without `DESK_NOTIFY_STATE_KEY`, which only `notify` jobs hold.
  - **Signed and parsed with limits:** the state is a JSON file and an HMAC-SHA-256 of its bytes
    under `DESK_NOTIFY_STATE_KEY`; a state whose HMAC does not match is not used (AC76). The zip is
    read only if under 1 MB, the JSON under 256 KB, with at most 5,000 keys; anything else is not
    used.
  - **What it holds:** its form version; the run's time; the keyed hash of the subscription; the
    encrypted JWT and when it was minted (J17); whether the project was read, and since when it has
    been unread; and the keys waiting then, each as HMAC-SHA-256 of AC73's key under
    `DESK_NOTIFY_STATE_KEY`, first 32 hex characters (B4's fix), with when it was first sent, or
    "unsent since <t>"; for each job candidate (J16), the keyed hash of its key and head with when a
    run first saw that head, and, if any, when a run first read one of rule (b)'s retried reasons
    for it (draft 4, review round 3, N4); and the usage part (J21, AC78), encrypted as the JWT is:
    the UTC year and month, the last step notified, when the usage was last read and since when it
    has been unread. No title, path, number, endpoint, key or usage figure in clear.
  - **New** means a key in this run's set and not in the last state (C). Sent keys are kept while
    they wait; a key that leaves the set is dropped, so it is new again if it returns. A project
    not read this run keeps its last keys.
  - **No state and an unreadable state are different** (N7's fix): the artifact list or download
    failing, or a forged state, is "unreadable": the run sends nothing and uploads nothing (AC76).
    Only when the list answers and holds no artifact (the first run, or none for 7 days) is it "no
    state", and then the run sends one summary, "Notifications are on: <n> things wait on you now",
    opening Home, and records every key as sent (C).
  - **Duplicates:** a run that dies after sending and before its upload means the next run sends
    those keys again; the `Topic` and `tag` replace rather than add where the first is still
    waiting or showing (J17). A known limit, rare.
  - A test marked `join-test: S-001/J18` covers it with a fake artifact store: new keys against the
    last state; a project not read keeping its keys; a key leaving and returning; the bootstrap
    summary; "unreadable" for a failed list, a failed download, a forged HMAC, an artifact from
    another workflow, from a branch, from another repository, and an oversized one, each sending
    nothing; a valid state on the second page not looked for; a listing whose newest 150 runs were
    skipped still finding the last good state, since only `success` runs are listed; a job
    candidate's head and retried reason kept across runs; the usage part unreadable without the
    key; the subscription's
    hash changing; and that no key can be matched to its item without `DESK_NOTIFY_STATE_KEY`
    (hashing every made-up key with a wrong key finds none).

- **J19 — The desk page → a push subscription → the owner's GitHub secrets (added 2026-10-18,
  AC75).**
  - **The public key:** J7's `push.vapidPublicKey`, the 65-byte uncompressed P-256 public key as
    base64url with no padding, 87 characters (Setup N1). It is public, so it is committed in J7,
    not a secret.
  - **Subscribing** (C): on the tap only (W4). If `window.pushManager` exists (Declarative Web
    Push, iOS 18.4 and later, D1, single page B), `window.pushManager.subscribe({userVisibleOnly:
    true, applicationServerKey})`, with no service worker; else
    `navigator.serviceWorker.register('/sw.js', {scope: '/'})`, then that registration's
    `pushManager.subscribe` with the same options (W6, W9, W12). A failure shows its words: "The
    desk couldn't turn notifications on: <the browser's reason>", with EXP-006's fallback named
    when the reason is the service worker's script.
  - **The value:** `JSON.stringify(subscription)` (V2), shown in a box the owner can select, with
    "Copy", and again from `getSubscription()` on "Show the value again". It is kept by the browser
    only; the desk stores nothing else and sends it nowhere. The owner puts the same value in both
    projects' `notify` Environments (Setup N6), since one phone receives both.
  - **A new subscription** (a new phone, the desk removed from the Home Screen and added again,
    `pushsubscriptionchange`, or the address changing at launch, Setup N10) means a new value: the
    owner updates the secret in both projects (N6). AC76's issue says so when the old one is gone.
  - A test marked `join-test: S-001/J19` covers it with a faked `window.pushManager`,
    `navigator.serviceWorker` and `Notification`: the key's decoding to 65 bytes starting `0x04`;
    each path; the value's form, matching what J17's first step accepts (the same parser, run on
    the page's output); and the failure words.

- **J20 — Open pull requests' files → "Your answer is being recorded" and the job candidates
  (desk page and senders; added 2026-10-18, draft 3, AC77, J16).**
  - **What is read:** for each open pull request in J1's list whose head is in the project's own
    repository, its changed files, `GET /repos/<repo>/pulls/<n>/files` (at most 3 pages of 100),
    with `filename` and `status` (`added`, `removed`, `modified`, `renamed` with
    `previous_filename`, split as asks-merge splits it). A pull request from a fork is read for
    nothing more: it is never a job candidate and never records an answer.
  - **Its size** (review N5): one request per page, usually one (a pull request with up to 100
    files), at most three; each page counts 1 of the blob call's budget of 25, as a blob does (C).
  - **On the page** (C): the read travels in J6's blob call, as J12's query does: one to three
    requests per open pull request, made again only when its head sha changes, its answer kept by head sha in
    the page's memory. Service-Desk usually has none to three open (E), so a few requests a day.
    It is optional, like J11's reads: a failure leaves the question as an ordinary waiting one,
    with its link, and never touches "read successfully".
  - **In a sender:** a REST read with the workflow's token, each run (J16).
  - **What it gives:** an open question `<name>` whose answer `decisions/questions/<name>.md` an
    open, non-draft pull request adds (v3), or whose file it deletes (v2.5 only; in v3 a deletion
    alone, such as a withdrawal, is not an answer, review round 3, N9), is **being recorded**
    (AC77); and J16's job candidates.
  - A test marked `join-test: S-001/J20` covers it with REST answers recorded once from
    Service-Desk's pull requests (as J12's fixture is) and made-up ones: an added answer record; a
    renamed file split in two; a deleted question file (v2.5, poom/'s placeholder form) read as
    being recorded, and the same deletion alone in a v3 project not; a fork's
    pull request ignored; a head change reading again, an
    unchanged head not; three pages; and a failed read leaving the question ordinary.

- **J21 — GitHub's billing usage → the desk's function and Service-Desk's sender (added
  2026-10-18, draft 4, AC78; research Q-015, graded, not yet source-checked, CS19).**
  - **Who reads, and why both** (C, weighed as asked):
    - **the sender** (Workflow 3, Service-Desk's only) reads it for the $50 notifications, which
      must come when the desk is closed. Its runs are free, as a public repository's, and run at
      least every 5 minutes, so an hourly read costs nothing. Workflow 4 never reads it: its
      minutes are charged, and one reader is enough for one account;
    - **the desk's function** reads it for the bar. The sender's state is a public artifact in
      Service-Desk, so the figures may not sit there in clear (AC76); for the desk to read them
      from it, the function would need a second key shared with the sender, an artifact download
      and a zip to unpack, more parts than one read with a token held as a secret;
    - **not the page itself:** a read from the browser would put a billing token in the page, and
      whether GitHub's CORS preflight lets `X-GitHub-Api-Version` through is unknown (Q-015 F6.1,
      U-5). Both reads are server side; no figure passes between them, and both use the same
      reader in `src/lib/` (C), so they agree.
  - **The token** (Setup N12; the owner's, by hand; no agent holds it): one fine-grained personal
    access token of the account, repository access "Public repositories (read-only)", account
    permission Plan: Read-only and nothing else, with an expiry the owner chooses. GitHub's REST
    data says such a token works (Q-015 F2.1, A); its tutorial says these endpoints do not take
    fine-grained tokens (F2.2, A): UNKNOWN which holds (U-1), so EXP-006 case 11 tries it once.
    Fallback: a classic token, whose required scope is not documented (U-2): the owner tries no
    scope, then `read:user`, then `user`, keeps the first that answers 200, and records which;
    Risks states a classic token's wider reach. The workflow's own token cannot call these
    endpoints (F2.4, A), and the desk's read token is not widened. It is set twice, with the same
    value: `DESK_BILLING_TOKEN` in Service-Desk's `notify` Environment, and the Pages secret
    `GITHUB_BILLING_TOKEN` (Production) on the preview, and on `needs-you` at launch (C6).
  - **The reads** (fixed in the code; the page sends no address, account or month), each with
    `Authorization: Bearer <token>`, `Accept: application/vnd.github+json` and
    `X-GitHub-Api-Version: 2022-11-28`, `<y>` and `<m>` taken once per read from the UTC date:
    1. `GET /users/<account>/settings/billing/usage/summary?year=<y>&month=<m>&product=Actions`
       (Q-015 F1.2): its `usageItems`, for the estimate;
    2. `GET /users/<account>/settings/billing/usage?year=<y>&month=<m>` (F1.1): its `usageItems`,
       for the minutes by repository, and the estimate when the summary does not answer in form;
    3. `GET /users/<account>/repos?type=owner&per_page=100`, at most 3 pages: the account's public
       repositories (a user's repositories listed by name show only public ones; C), matched
       against `repositoryName` as `<account>/<name>` or `<name>`, since its form is not
       documented (EXP-006 case 11 records it).
  - **What is kept from them:** each item's `product`, `sku`, `unitType`, `quantity` or
    `netQuantity`, `pricePerUnit`, `netAmount` and `repositoryName`, and the public repositories'
    names; AC78 computes from these alone. Anything else is dropped.
  - **On the page** (C): an optional read riding J6's blob call, as J12's query does, made at most
    once an hour per device and again on a cost notification's tap, using 3 to 5 of the call's
    budget of 25. The function answers `{"state": "ok" | "not-configured" | "refused" |
    "not-found" | "unavailable", "asOf": "<UTC>", ...}` with the kept fields only, never the
