    token. It never touches "read successfully", the counts or "Quiet".
  - **In the sender:** in a run whose J18 usage part shows the last read 60 minutes ago or more,
    with `DESK_BILLING_TOKEN` passed through `env:` to the one step that sends. Its requests count
    against the owner's own hourly limit, not the workflow token's, so J16's budget guard is not
    touched.
  - **The owner's limit:** a user's tokens share one hourly limit (C), with the desk's read token
    among them: at most 5 requests an hour from the sender and 5 an hour per open device, a few
    dozen a day (A3).
  - **Awkward cases:** an empty `usageItems` (a month's first hours): 0 minutes and $0.00; the
    month turning during a read: the year and month taken once; the summary answering in another
    form: `/usage` used (AC78); `repositoryName` missing: counted (AC78); figures lagging by an
    unknown time (U-3): "as of", never "now"; the old `/settings/billing/actions` endpoint never
    called (F1.7, F1.8).
  - A test marked `join-test: S-001/J21` covers it with made-up answers in the documented form
    (NOT real, labelled so; no real billing figure enters this public repository): the three
    requests' fixed form; each state from 200, 401, 403, 404, 5xx and a body in another form; the
    same reader giving the same figures on the page and in the sender; the kept fields only in the
    function's answer, and the token in no answer or log line; public repositories matched in both
    name forms; three pages of repositories; the at-most-hourly reads on both sides; and the read
    leaving "read successfully" and "Quiet" unchanged.

## Toolchain and deploy route
- **Layout.** Everything the Builder writes for the desk lives under `src/`, and deploys run from
  `src/`.
  - `src/package.json`: Node 20 or later, ES modules. Wrangler is a devDependency pinned to an exact
    version.
  - **No runtime dependencies (decided here; the Reviewer's round-2 non-blocking 2):**
    - `src/package.json` has no `dependencies` block, and the build fails if one appears.
    - The Functions and the page import only from `src/functions/`, `src/lib/` and `src/public/`.
    - So the bundle wrangler makes of `src/functions/` at deploy time contains only reviewed
      repository code. No separate `wrangler pages functions build` step is needed.
    - The deploy job installs nothing under `src/`. A package import in a Function would therefore
      fail the deploy-time bundle, which enforces the rule a second time.
  - **No lifecycle scripts:** `src/package.json` has no `preinstall`, `install`, `postinstall`,
    `preprepare`, `prepare`, `postprepare`, `prepack`, `postpack`, `prepublish`,
    `prepublishOnly`, `publish` or `postpublish` script. desk-build fails the PR otherwise.
  - `src/package-lock.json`.
  - `src/wrangler.toml`: only the keys `name`, `pages_build_output_dir = "public"` and
    `compatibility_date`; desk-build fails the PR on any other key. The
    project name is passed on the command line (below).
  - `src/functions/api/poll.js` and `src/functions/api/blobs.js`: Pages Functions, by file-based
    routing (C).
  - `src/lib/`: the record parsers, shared by the page and the tests.
  - `src/public/`: the page.
  - `src/config/projects.json` (J7).
- **Tests.** They live in `tests/desk/`, with fixtures in `tests/desk/fixtures/`, using Node's
  built-in runner.
  - **Build command:** `npm --prefix src ci && npm --prefix src run build`. The build also checks
    J7.
  - **Test command:** `npm --prefix src test`, which runs `node --test ../tests/desk/`.
  - **No live access:** the tests use the real samples quoted in J2, J9 and J10 and a fake `fetch`.
    No test needs a token or the network.
- **Who holds secrets: nobody but the owner and GitHub/Cloudflare** (PROJECT.md §4 line 115;
  AGENTS.md §1).
  - **The Builder** holds neither the Cloudflare API token nor the read token. It never deploys,
    and it never calls the live function.
  - **The read token** exists only as a Pages secret.
  - **From 2026-10-18 (notifications, draft 2):** the VAPID private key and subject, the push
    subscription and a state key exist only as secrets of each project's GitHub Environment
    `notify` (Workflows 3 and 4; Setup N1 to N10). No token crosses projects. The Builder holds
    none of them. From draft 4, the billing-only token (J21, Setup N12) exists only as
    `DESK_BILLING_TOKEN` in Service-Desk's `notify` and as the Pages secret
    `GITHUB_BILLING_TOKEN`.
  - **The Cloudflare API token** exists only as a secret of the GitHub environments `preview` and
    `production`.
  - The owner sets each by hand (Setup).
- **Workflow 1, `.github/workflows/desk-build.yml`** (the Chief of Staff's).
  - **Trigger:** `pull_request`, and pushes to `main` that touch `src/**` or `tests/**`.
  - **Access:** `permissions: contents: read`, and no secrets. It uses no environment.
  - **Steps:**
    - `npm --prefix src ci --ignore-scripts`, then the build and test commands.
    - A config check (a script in the Chief of Staff's lane, `.github/scripts/`). It fails the PR if
      `src/wrangler.toml` holds any key other than `name`, `pages_build_output_dir` and
      `compatibility_date`. It also fails if `src/package.json` has any lifecycle script (listed
      under Layout) or a `dependencies` block.
    - It uploads no artifact and hands nothing on to any other workflow.
  - **Workflow files:** a change to `.github/**` outside the Chief of Staff's lane already fails CI
    through `surface_guard.py diff --range`, because `.github/**` is the Chief of Staff's surface in
    governance/SURFACES.md. desk-build doesn't repeat that check.
  - **Branches:** the Builder's milestone branch, `build/builder/<task>`, is pushed to origin,
    which the pull request needs. desk-build is the only desk workflow that runs on it.
- **Workflow 2, `.github/workflows/desk-deploy.yml`** (the Chief of Staff's).
  - **Trigger:** only `push` to `main` with paths `src/**`, and `workflow_dispatch`. It never runs
    on `pull_request` or `workflow_run`. No pull-request branch name or artifact is ever used.
  - **Access:** `permissions: contents: read` for the whole workflow.
  - **Why `main` alone isn't enough (the Reviewer's round-3 R3-B1):**
    - `main` is unprotected, and agents push under the owner's admin account. So "code on `main`
      is reviewed" isn't enforced by GitHub.
    - So **every deploy job runs in an environment whose required reviewer is the owner.** That
      covers `preview` and `production`, and `exp-002` too.
    - GitHub holds each job until the owner presses Approve, which the GitHub mobile app offers.
  - **Each approval is tied to the commit it deploys (R4-B1).**
    - **Push runs** check out exactly `${{ github.sha }}`, never "the tip of `main`".
    - **`workflow_dispatch` runs** require an input `sha`.
      - The first step receives it through `env:`, never interpolated into the shell.
      - After `git fetch origin main`, it fails unless all three of these hold (R5-B1):
        1. the input matches `^[0-9a-f]{40}$`;
        2. `git merge-base --is-ancestor "$SHA" origin/main` succeeds, so the commit is on `main`;
        3. `git diff --quiet "$SHA" origin/main -- src .github/workflows .github/deploy-tools
           .github/scripts` succeeds, so nothing deployable has changed on `main` since that commit.
      - It then checks out and deploys exactly that SHA.
      - **What this allows:** Orchestrator and `decisions/` commits landing on `main` after the
        reviewed merge don't block a dispatched preview re-deploy or the launch deploy.
      - **What it still refuses:** an unmerged commit, and an older reviewed commit whose deployable
        code newer commits have replaced.
    - **`run-name`** was `deploy ${{ github.event_name == 'push' && github.sha || inputs.sha }} —
      ${{ github.event.head_commit.message || inputs.note }}`. From Setup CS14 (the desk
      follow-ups, 2026-10-17) a push run's title is `deploy <sha>` alone, and a hand-started
      run's stays `deploy <sha> — <note>`. For example (C, the Chief of Staff's to write):
      `run-name: deploy ${{ github.event_name == 'push' && github.sha || format('{0} — {1}',
      inputs.sha, inputs.note) }}`.
      - The approval screen shows the full SHA. GitHub expressions have no substring function, but
        its first 7 characters are the short SHA on the PR page.
      - **The merge commit's message is no longer used.** Since 2026-10-17 the merge setting is
        "Pull request title and description" (docs/SETUP.md step 5; L-0015). So a merge commit's
        first line is `<pull request title> (#<n>)` and its body is the pull request's
        description. It no longer begins `Merge pull request #<n>`.
      - Until CS14, a push run's title held that whole message, and GitHub's expressions cannot
        take its first line. From CS14 the desk's Queue names the pull request a push deploy
        carries, by AC72's line.
      - A dispatch carries a `note` input, in which the Chief of Staff names the PR.
      - The note is a hint only. The owner matches the SHA. AC72's line on the desk makes the
        same check from the records, and the approval stays on GitHub.
  - **The owner's approval rule:**
    - Approve only when the run's SHA equals the merge-commit SHA shown on the page of a pull
      request that the Reviewer passed. Fix-forward PRs need the Reviewer's PASS too.
    - Reject every other run.
    - The actor name can't separate the owner's runs from an agent's: agents act under the owner's
      account, so both show as `shumpmaster`. The SHA is the only thing to check.
    - **Production:** approve only a SHA that the preview has already deployed and that the owner
      has accepted on the preview.
    - **`exp-002`:** the owner approves it when they or the Chief of Staff asked for it. It deploys
      nothing.
  - **Concurrency:** each job has its own `concurrency` group, `desk-deploy-preview`,
    `desk-deploy-production` or `desk-deploy-exp-002`, with `cancel-in-progress: false`. So two
    approved runs never deploy over each other, and a waiting run is not silently cancelled.
  - **Every deploy job:**
    - checks out the approved SHA, as above;
    - runs no tests (desk-build already ran them on the same commit);
    - re-runs the `.github/scripts/` config check on that checkout before wrangler: the
      `wrangler.toml` keys, and no lifecycle scripts or `dependencies` in `src/package.json`;
    - installs nothing under `src/`.
  - **The wrangler install step:**
    - It installs the deploy tool in a clean directory outside `src/`, from the Chief of Staff's
      pinned `.github/deploy-tools/package.json` and its lockfile. The command is
      `npm ci --ignore-scripts --prefix .github/deploy-tools`, which pins wrangler at `<x.y.z>`
      and its transitive dependencies.
    - The registry is pinned by a committed `.github/deploy-tools/.npmrc` holding
      `registry=https://registry.npmjs.org/`, so no other registry setting can redirect the
      install.
    - Whether wrangler runs fully without its packages' install scripts is C. The first deploy
      shows it. If it doesn't, the Chief of Staff names the one package whose script must run, in
      a reviewed change to that directory.
  - **The deploy step:**
    - Its `working-directory` is `src`. It calls
      `"$GITHUB_WORKSPACE/.github/deploy-tools/node_modules/.bin/wrangler" pages deploy public
      --project-name <project> --branch <branch>`. Nothing from `src/node_modules` exists or runs.
      wrangler reads `src/wrangler.toml`, whose keys are limited as above, and bundles
      `src/functions/`.
    - `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` and `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` are in that step's `env:` only, not the
      job's.
  - **Job `preview`:**
    - It runs on every push to `main` touching `src/**`, and on dispatch, in environment `preview`.
    - It deploys to `service-desk-preview` with `--branch main`.
    - `main` is the preview project's production branch (Setup C1). So each approved deploy
      updates `https://service-desk-preview.pages.dev`, a fixed URL, behind its Access application
      (F-cf-04, A).
    - The job writes the URL to its summary. There is no pull-request comment step.
  - **Job `production`:**
    - It runs on `workflow_dispatch` only, from `main`, in environment `production`, with the owner
      as required reviewer.
    - Beyond the dispatch check above, it fails unless the most recent successful `preview` job of
      desk-deploy ran on the same SHA. It reads that with `GITHUB_TOKEN`.
    - Its job-level block is `permissions: { contents: read, actions: read }`. A job-level block
      replaces the workflow-level one, so both are listed.
    - It deploys to `needs-you`, a direct-upload project (EXP-002), with `--branch` set to its
      production branch as EXP-002 records.
    - It is used at M3 and never run before AC28. The owner approves it only after disabling
      Operations-Hub's `needs-you-page` workflow, which would otherwise redeploy the old page over
      it within an hour (AC28, Setup C8).
  - **Job `exp-002`:** it runs on `workflow_dispatch` only, in environment `preview` (owner
    approval). It runs the same pinned wrangler's `pages project list`, read-only, with the token in
    the step's `env:` only.
  - **Possible later hardening, not relied on — option (b):** a ruleset on `main` blocking direct
    pushes to `src/**` and `.github/**`. Whether rulesets are available on this repository's plan
    is C. The owner's approval gate above is the control.
  - **What is still trusted, stated plainly:**
    - **The Reviewer's check:** the Reviewer's reading of `src/package.json`, `src/wrangler.toml`
      and `src/package-lock.json` on each pull request is a security control, not just a quality
      check. desk-build's config check backs it up for the keys and scripts it knows.
    - **wrangler's own dependencies:** wrangler and its transitive dependencies are trusted by
      pinning only, through the Chief of Staff's lockfile. Nobody reviews their code.
    - **The approval click:** the owner's click is the last gate. A mistaken approval of an
      unreviewed push would deploy it.
    - **The gate itself is not airtight (R4-B2).** Agents act under the owner's admin account. An
      agent could, through the API, remove an environment's required reviewer or change its secrets,
      and GitHub would show the change as the owner's.
      - The environment gate stops accidents and unreviewed runs. It doesn't stop a credential that
        can administer the repository.
      - The **recommended backstop** is CS9: at every milestone acceptance, the Chief of Staff shows
        the owner both environments' protection settings, and every audit-log entry for environment
        changes since the last milestone.
      - The **alternative** is to narrow the agents' credential to one without Administration
        permission.
      - The owner chose the backstop, (a) (closed owner question 4, P-001-s001-owner-questions.md).
- **Workflow 3, `.github/workflows/desk-notify.yml` in Service-Desk** (the Chief of Staff's; added
  2026-10-18, draft 3; AC73 to AC77, J16 to J18; Setup CS15).
  - **What research Q-014 found** (research/Q-014-memo.md, on the branch orch/s001-notifications,
    graded, not yet source-checked): a run started by `workflow_run` has `GITHUB_REF` the default
    branch and runs the default branch's file at its tip (F1.1, F1.3, A); an Environment's branch
    rule is matched against `GITHUB_REF`, so a main-only `notify` admits every such run, whatever
    branch or fork the triggering run came from (F2.1, F2.2, A by deduction); such a run gets
    secrets even when the triggering run was a fork's pull request (F4.1, F4.3, A); the triggering
    run's branch and repository are only in `github.event.workflow_run` (F1.5, A/C); and GitHub
    says workflows on this trigger "must not explicitly check out untrusted code" and should treat
    other workflows' artifacts with caution (F4.4, A). So the Environment rule is no gate on where
    a run came from, and the gates below are.
  - **The switch:** the job runs only when the repository variable `DESK_NOTIFY_ON` is `true`,
    which the owner sets last (Setup N7) and clears to stop it (rollback) (C).
  - **The origin gate** (C; Q-014's pattern (b)): the job's `if:` also requires
    `github.event_name != 'workflow_run' || github.event.workflow_run.head_repository.full_name ==
    github.repository`, and, for `workflow_run`, `github.event.action == 'completed' ||
    github.event.workflow_run.name == 'desk-deploy'`, and either `github.event.workflow_run.event`
    one of `push`, `pull_request`, `workflow_dispatch` or `schedule`, or (draft 4, review round 3,
    N2; Critic round 3, note 1) `github.event.workflow_run.event == 'workflow_run' &&
    github.event.workflow_run.path == '.github/workflows/asks-merge.yml'`: asks-merge's judging
    runs are themselves started by `workflow_run`, and such a run always runs the default branch's
    file (Q-014 F1.3, A), so admitting it is safe; without it, a job candidate the job leaves
    would wait for the 5-minute check. A run a fork's pull request started is skipped, so no secret
    reaches it; the 5-minute check finds that pull request instead (AC74). The field name is
    community knowledge, not documented (Q-014 F6.2, C): EXP-006 case 10 checks it on a real run.
    - **If the field is absent or wrong there** (Critic round 3, note 6): the `head_repository`
      test moves into the program, which, before it reads any secret's value, reads the triggering
      run through REST with the workflow's own token, `GET /repos/<repo>/actions/runs/<id>` (the
      id from the payload through `env:`), and requires its `head_repository.full_name` to be this
      repository; that is a second signal, from GitHub's REST answer rather than the event's
      payload (C; EXP-006 case 10 reads it too).
    - **If neither carries it,** the gate admits no `workflow_run` run at all: every event but the
      owner's own push and a dispatch waits for the 5-minute check, usually 5 to 10 minutes, and
      AC74 states that wait (C).
  - **Triggers** (C):
    - `workflow_run` of `governance`, `orchestrator`, `asks-merge`, `pr-body`, `desk-build`,
      `desk-deploy` and `desk-notify-ready`, types `completed` and `requested`, with `branches-ignore`
      none: a pull request's head branch varies, so branches are not filtered; the gate above and
      the program decide. Pushes made with a workflow's own token start no `push` run (L-F8; Q-014
      F5.2, A), and the Orchestrator's and the asks-merge job's pushes to main are made so; both end
      their runs and start a governance run, which these events catch. Chain depth (pull_request →
      governance → asks-merge → desk-notify) is three, GitHub's limit (Q-014 F5.3, A);
    - `push` to `main`, for the owner's own commits;
    - `schedule`, `2-59/5 * * * *`: every 5 minutes, off the top of the hour;
    - `workflow_dispatch`, with an input `test` (`""`, `true`, `fail` or, from draft 4, `usage`:
      one J21 read, its log giving only each request's HTTP status, whether the fields AC78 uses
      were present, and whether `repositoryName` carries the owner's name before a `/`, never a
      figure or a name; EXP-006 case 11);
    - **no `pull_request_target` and no `pull_request`.**
  - **The no-secret ready workflow, `.github/workflows/desk-notify-ready.yml`** (C; review N13's
    idea, here as well as there): `on: pull_request: types: [ready_for_review,
    converted_to_draft, closed]`, `permissions: {}`, one job with one step that runs `true`, no
    checkout, no secret, no Environment. Its only purpose is to finish, so Workflow 3's
    `workflow_run` sees a pull request marked ready, turned back to draft or closed within minutes
    (closing review N6's gap). Its runs are free in a public repository.
  - **Access:** `permissions:` `contents: read`, `pull-requests: read`, `actions: read`,
    `checks: read`, `statuses: read` and `issues: write` (AC76's issue), nothing more. The job runs
    in the GitHub Environment `notify`, deployment branches `main` only (Setup N4): it keeps the
    secrets from a dispatch or a copy of the file on any other branch (Q-014's opinion (a)), not
    from what reaches main.
  - **Secrets** (the owner's, by hand, in `notify`): `DESK_VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY`,
    `DESK_VAPID_SUBJECT`, `DESK_PUSH_SUBSCRIPTION` and `DESK_NOTIFY_STATE_KEY`, and from draft 4
    `DESK_BILLING_TOKEN` (J21, Service-Desk only), each passed through `env:` to the one step that
    sends. No other token: the workflow's own reads its own repository, and the billing token reads
    only the account's billing use.
  - **Nothing from the triggering run is used** (Q-014 F4.4; C): no checkout of its ref, no
    download of its artifacts, no `${{ }}` expression in any `run:` line or block; every value from
    the event or a secret reaches the program through `env:`, and the program rebuilds the whole
    set from the API and the default branch's checkout, reading titles and branch names as data
    only.
  - **Concurrency, on the job** (draft 4, review round 3, N5; Critic round 3, note 1; C): the
    `notify` job, not the workflow, holds `concurrency: {group: desk-notify, cancel-in-progress:
    false}`, and the workflow has none: one job at a time. A job whose `if:` is false is skipped
    without entering the group (C, as GitHub evaluates a job's `if:` before queuing it; EXP-006
    case 5 would show otherwise), so the many skipped runs (the `requested` runs of seven
    workflows, a fork's, the gated-out events) never take a pending real job's place. GitHub keeps
    one pending job in the group and replaces an older pending one; a sweep or event job loses
    nothing, since every run rebuilds the whole set (J16) against the last state (J18), but a
    pending `test` job can be replaced, so Setup N8 says to run it again if none appears.
  - **Steps:** check out the default branch with its history and no token left on disk
    (`persist-credentials: false`); set up Node with `actions/setup-node`, pinned by commit, at
    one exact Node version (C, review N1); run `node src/notify/run.mjs --project Service-Desk`
    with no install step (no dependencies, Toolchain); upload `desk-notify-state` only if the
    program wrote it, kept 7 days. Every action is pinned by commit, as desk-build's are.
    `timeout-minutes: 10`.
  - **Exit codes:** 0 when the run did its work, sends that failed and a budget stop included
    (AC76, J16); 1 when its first step stops it (a secret missing or malformed, the keys not
    matching); 2 for an internal error. J1 shows its runs as activity, never as CI.
  - **Logs:** counts, reasons and keyed hashes only (AC76).
  - **Its own check** (review B3; C): CS15's pull request also adds
    `.github/scripts/desk_notify_check.py`, run by desk-build on every pull request, which reads
    `.github/workflows/desk-notify.yml` and `desk-notify-ready.yml` and fails when: a trigger other
    than those above appears, `pull_request_target` or `pull_request` among them in desk-notify;
    the permissions differ; the Environment, the switch or the origin gate is missing, or the gate
    admits a triggering event beyond those above; a workflow-level `concurrency` appears, or the
    job's is missing; a checkout has `persist-credentials` other than false or a `ref` other than
    the default branch; an action is not pinned by a 40-character sha; any `run:` line or block
    holds `${{`; or desk-notify-ready holds a secret, an Environment, a checkout or permissions
    other than `{}`. It ships in the same pull request as the workflow, so it never tests a file
    that is not there.
    - **Its own test** (draft 4, review round 3, N6): `.github/scripts/test_desk_notify_check.py`,
      run by desk-build beside it with Python's own `unittest`, with bad files under
      `.github/scripts/fixtures/desk-notify/` (not workflows: GitHub runs only files directly in
      `.github/workflows/`), each a copy of the real file with one fault, failing with its named
      reason: a `pull_request_target` trigger; a `pull_request` trigger; `contents: write`; no
      Environment; no switch; no origin gate; a gate admitting `workflow_run` from any workflow; a
      workflow-level `concurrency`; `persist-credentials: true`; a checkout `ref` of
      `${{ github.event.workflow_run.head_sha }}`; an action pinned by tag (`@v4`); `${{` in a
      one-line `run:` and in a `run: |` block; and a ready workflow with a secret, with a checkout,
      and with `permissions: read-all`. The real two files pass.
  - **Cost and known limits:** Actions is free for public repositories on standard runners
    (GitHub's billing documentation, Facts relied on), so its 288 checks a day, and its hourly
    usage reads (J21), cost nothing. GitHub
    disables a public repository's schedules after 60 days with no activity (F-gh-12b), which
    Service-Desk's daily commits prevent. Each run makes a deployment record in `notify`, about
    300 a day; the desk reads none of them.
- **Workflow 4, `.github/workflows/desk-notify.yml` in Personal-Org-Operating-Model** (that
  project's own, written and reviewed there under its own process; added 2026-10-18, draft 3; the
  fast-path ruling, decisions/questions/P-001-notify-poom-fast-path.md; Setup CS16, N9).
  - **What it is:** Workflow 3's form, for that project alone, run from that private repository:
    the switch `DESK_NOTIFY_ON`, the origin gate, the Environment `notify` limited to its `main`
    with the same four secret names (its own state key), the same permissions, nothing from the
    triggering run used, no `${{ }}` in `run:`, the same exit codes, logs, budget guard and state
    (J18, kept there); no `DESK_BILLING_TOKEN` and no usage read (J21). The account is on GitHub Pro
    (docs/design/references/owner-github-plan-2026-10-18.md), so an Environment with a branch rule
    is available in a private repository (docs/SETUP.md:9; Personal-Org-Operating-Model
    specs/S-016-hardening.md:74, :91).
  - **Its sender's code** (review N1; C): a second checkout of Service-Desk, which is public, so no
    token is needed, at one full commit sha written in the file (`repository:
    shumpmaster/Service-Desk`, `ref: <40-character sha>`, `fetch-depth: 0`, `persist-credentials:
    false`); a step then checks that the sha is on Service-Desk's main (`git merge-base
    --is-ancestor <sha> origin/main` in that checkout) and stops the run if not, since a commit of
    a fork can be fetched through the same repository's name; then Node at the same pinned version;
    then `node <that checkout>/src/notify/run.mjs --project Personal-Org-Operating-Model --root .`.
    A change to the sender reaches it only when a reviewed pull request there moves the sha, its
    review reading Service-Desk's diff between the two shas for `src/notify/`, `src/lib/` and
    `src/config/` (CS16).
  - **Triggers, cut as far as safety allows** (draft 4, the owner's billing answer: every minute
    is charged; C). That project has only two kinds that wait, questions and pull requests (AC73),
    and no job candidates (no grant, J16), so only these events can make something newly wait: a
    question landing on main, and a pull request opened, reopened or marked ready. A pull
    request's new head, a merge, a close or a turn to draft changes nothing that notifies (a thing
    that stops waiting sends nothing, AC74), so none of them starts a run:
    - `push` to `main` with `paths: ['questions/**']`: a question filed, directly or by a merge;
    - `workflow_run` of its own no-secret ready workflow (below), type `completed`, with the origin
      gate; not of `ci` (draft 3's trigger), which ran the sender on every new head and on `ci`'s
      own pushes to `build/**`;
    - `schedule`, `7 */3 * * *`, every 3 hours, kept as the floor: it bounds anything an event
      misses (a run GitHub drops, a pull request closed and reopened between runs, J16's known
      limit). A 6-hourly check would save about 120 minutes, about $0.72, a month, and double that
      worst case, so it is not taken;
    - `workflow_dispatch` with `test` (`true` or `fail`; not `usage`, which only Workflow 3 has).
  - **Its run's length:** `timeout-minutes: 3`, not 10, so a hung run costs at most 3 minutes;
    the two checkouts keep `fetch-depth: 0`, which the questions' raised times and the pin's
    `merge-base` check need, and nothing is installed, so a run is expected to take under a
    minute, which GitHub bills as one (E; EXP-006 case 9 records it). The concurrency is on the
    job, as Workflow 3's. The job does not ask the triggering run's conclusion: a skipped ready
    job concludes `skipped`.
  - **Its no-secret ready workflow,** `.github/workflows/desk-notify-ready.yml` there:
    `pull_request` types `opened`, `reopened` and `ready_for_review` only (not `closed` or
    `converted_to_draft`, which only stop things waiting), `permissions: {}`, one job whose `if:`
    is `false`, so it is skipped and runs on no runner, using no minutes (C), and the run still
    completes, which starts Workflow 4's `workflow_run` (C: that a run whose only job was skipped
    sends `workflow_run` `completed` is not documented; EXP-006 case 9 checks it). If it does not,
    the job runs one step, `true`, as Service-Desk's does: one more minute for each such event,
    each job being rounded up to a whole minute (GitHub's billing documentation).
  - **Its check:** the same rules as Workflow 3's check, in that project's own review of its pull
    request, or its CI if its process puts it there; no copy of its files comes to Service-Desk.
  - **Its cost, stated plainly** (review N2, Critic B2):
    - the minutes are the account's: GitHub Pro includes 3,000 a month for the whole account,
      shared by every private repository, public repositories using none; each job is rounded up
      to a whole minute; with no payment method on file, use is blocked once the allowance is used
      up, in every private repository; with one, extra minutes are charged and budgets can limit
      them (GitHub's billing documentation, Facts relied on);
    - the account is past its included minutes this month, with a card on file (the owner's
      billing answer, 338496b), so every minute is charged, at $0.006 a minute for standard Linux
      runners from 2026-01-01 (research Q-015 F4.1, A; not yet source-checked);
    - its use after the cuts (E, not measured): the sender about 1 minute a run, at about 6 pull
      requests opened, reopened or marked ready and 2 questions landing a day, and 8 checks a
      day, so about 16 runs, about 500 minutes a month, about $3.00; about 690 minutes, about
      $4.15, if the ready workflow's job must run (above); draft 3's triggers came to about 730,
      about $4.40. EXP-006 case 9 records a week's real figure, read in the account's billing
      settings and on the desk (AC78);
    - nothing here asks the owner to approve a cost (the owner's cost instruction): the desk shows
      the account's use and its cost (AC78), and GitHub's own e-mails at 90% and 100% of the
      included use are a complement the owner may turn on (Setup N11);
    - outside this spec's scope: the operating-model project's existing `ci` appears to be most of
      the account's use (plan review round 3, N1; C), and is not changed here;
    - its delays: about as fast as Service-Desk's for what its events catch; up to about 3 hours,
      later if GitHub runs the schedule late, for anything they miss.
  - **Its deploys:** none. It has no deploy workflow or environment, and J7 does not mark it
    `deploysNeedOwner`.
  - **At launch** (Setup N10): its pin moves to the Service-Desk commit whose J7 holds the
    `needs-you` `deskUrl`, in a reviewed pull request there; until then its taps open the preview.
- **Milestone acceptance:**
  1. The Reviewer passes the milestone's pull request.
  2. The owner merges it.
  3. The owner approves desk-deploy's `preview` job, which deploys `main` to the preview.
  4. The owner tries the preview and accepts the milestone.

  A problem found on the preview is fixed by a fix-forward pull request, not by reopening the
  merged one.

## Setup outside the Builder's surface
*None of this is in `src/**`, `tests/**` or `docs/handover/**`, and the Builder does none of it.
The owner does everything touching Access, tokens and Cloudflare or GitHub settings by hand,
following the Chief of Staff's steps below, which the Chief of Staff writes out for the owner with
the current screen names. The Chief of Staff does the repository tasks.*

  the owner merges.

  question 0: build from the approved brief and the draft spec, outside the pipeline. The entry

- CS9. **At every milestone acceptance** (R4-B2's backstop), before the owner accepts.
  - **The owner checks it themselves, as the evidence** (about 2 minutes on the phone):
    - **The settings page:** they open Service-Desk → Settings → Environments → `preview` and
      `production`. They check that each has the owner as required reviewer, and the deployment
      branch `main` only. *From 2026-10-18* also `notify`, in Service-Desk and in
      Personal-Org-Operating-Model: deployment branch `main` only, and its four secrets the ones
      the owner set (their names and update dates; GitHub never shows their values), with

      the owner's token settings: its permissions still Plan: Read-only alone.
    - **Their security log:** they open their own GitHub security log (Settings → Security log), filtered

**The owner, by hand, before M1's merge** (about 45 minutes once, E, counted against the pre-launch
2 hours a week):
- C1. **Create the preview project.** Cloudflare → Workers & Pages → create a Pages project by
  direct upload, named `service-desk-preview`, with a placeholder page. Its production branch is
  `main`, because desk-deploy deploys it with `--branch main`.
- C2. **Protect the previews.** In that project's settings, turn on "Enable access policy", which
  covers preview deployments (F-cf-04). In Zero Trust → Access → Applications, set the policy it
  created to Include → Emails → the owner's address. Not One-time PIN alone, which lets anyone in
  (F-auth-02, A).
- C3. **Protect the `pages.dev` address.** Edit that Access application, delete the wildcard in its
  Subdomain field, save, and turn "Enable access policy" on again. That gives two applications
  (F-cf-04, A). Set the second one's policy to the owner's email too.
- C4. **Enter the Access settings.** Pages project → Settings → Variables and secrets →
  **Production** → add `OWNER_EMAIL`, `ACCESS_TEAM_DOMAIN`, and `ACCESS_AUD` holding both
  applications' audience tags, comma-separated (J8).
  - The preview project's live deployment is its production-branch (`main`) deployment, so its
    Production variables are the ones used.
  - That makes the Reviewer's round-2 non-blocking 1 (which variable set the preview uses) moot.
  - No branch preview deployments are made.
- C5. **Create the deploy token.** Cloudflare → My Profile → API Tokens → create a custom token
  with Account → Cloudflare Pages → Edit, for this account only. Copy it once, for G2.
- G1. **Create the read token.** GitHub → Settings → Developer settings → Fine-grained tokens →
  generate one with:
  - repositories: only Service-Desk and Personal-Org-Operating-Model;
  - permissions: Contents: read, Metadata: read, Pull requests: read, and Actions: read (for CI,
    J1 and AC29). A fine-grained token offers no Checks permission, which is why M1's CI line
    reads "can't read checks" on the private repository (EXP-004 Q4);
  - an expiry the owner chooses, with renewal in AC27's notes.

  Paste it straight into the Pages project → Variables and secrets → Production → secret
  `GITHUB_READ_TOKEN`. Never paste it into a chat, a file or GitHub.
- G2. **Create the GitHub environments.** Service-Desk → Settings → Environments:
  - `preview`: required reviewer the owner, deployment branch `main` only, with secrets
    `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` (from C5) and `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID`;
  - `production`: required reviewer the owner, deployment branch `main` only, with the same two
    secrets.
  - **On both:** tick "Required reviewers" and add the owner's account. GitHub then holds every job
    in either environment until the owner approves it (R3-B1). Leave "Prevent self-review" off,
    because the owner approves runs their own merges start.

    Builder's sender pull request opens, so every change under `src/notify/`, the first included,
    carries the owner's verdict as well as the Reviewer's; the owner's minutes for it sit in the
    time table's "review, merge" line.
  - **One real job log for J16's test** (draft 4, review round 3, N3): record, once, the log of one
    real asks-merge `merge` job in Service-Desk (`gh api
    repos/shumpmaster/Service-Desk/actions/jobs/<job id>/logs`, a public repository's job), from a
    run that left a pull request if one exists, else any run that judged one, and hand it to the
    Builder, who keeps it under `tests/desk/fixtures/github/` and lists it as real in
    tests/desk/fixtures/README.md with the date and the command. If the agents' sessions cannot
    reach that endpoint, the owner runs the command on their own computer, as for CS13 (about 3
    minutes).
- CS19. From 2026-10-18 (draft 4, Critic round 3, note 6): have research Q-014 source-checked by the
  Source checker, as Q-011 was (research/Q-011-source-check.md), **before this spec's pull request
  merges**, since Workflow 3's origin gate and J18's lookup rest on its A-graded findings (F1.1,
  F1.3, F2.1, F2.2, F4.1, F4.3, F5.3); and research Q-015 before the Builder's pull request that
  carries AC78 and J21 opens. A finding the check changes goes back to the Definer as a revision
  of this spec before the work that rests on it is built. Record each check's verdict and path
  in that pull request.

**The owner, by hand, for notifications** (from 2026-10-18, draft 4; about 50 minutes in all, E, in
this order; no agent holds any of these values):
- N1. **Make the key pair** (5 minutes), on your own computer, in a terminal, before the receiving
  side is built:
  1. `openssl ecparam -name prime256v1 -genkey -noout -out desk-vapid.pem` makes the private key.
  2. `openssl ec -in desk-vapid.pem -pubout -outform DER | tail -c 65 | base64 | tr '/+' '_-' | tr -d '=\n'`
     prints the public key: 87 characters. It is public: send it to your Chief of Staff (CS18).
  3. Keep `desk-vapid.pem`'s text for N4 and N9, then delete the file (`rm desk-vapid.pem`), or
     keep it in your password manager.
- N2. **Two more values** (2 minutes): your contact for the push service, `mailto:<your email>`;
  and a state key, from `openssl rand -hex 32`, for Service-Desk, and a second one for the
  operating-model project (N9). The other three secrets are the same in both projects.
- N3. **Approve the deploy** of the notifications pull request to the preview, as for any merge
  (Setup's deploy route).
- N4. **The Environment and three secrets in Service-Desk** (5 minutes): Settings, Environments,
  New environment `notify`; Deployment branches and tags, "Selected branches", add `main`; no
  required reviewers. Then Add environment secret, three times: `DESK_VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY` (the
  whole text of `desk-vapid.pem`), `DESK_VAPID_SUBJECT` (N2's `mailto:` address) and
  `DESK_NOTIFY_STATE_KEY` (N2's state key).
- N5. **On the iPhone** (5 minutes): open the preview in Safari and sign in through Access; Share,
  Add to Home Screen; open the desk from its new icon, signing in again if asked; in More detail,
  tap "Turn on notifications", then Allow; tap "Copy".
- N6. **The subscription secret** (3 minutes): in Service-Desk, Settings, Environments, `notify`,
  Add environment secret `DESK_PUSH_SUBSCRIPTION`, paste (later, after a new subscription: update
  it). Then "I've added it to GitHub" on the desk.
- N7. **The switch** (1 minute), once the Chief of Staff's Workflow 3 is merged (CS15): Settings,
  Secrets and variables, Actions, Variables, New repository variable `DESK_NOTIFY_ON` = `true`.
- N8. **A test** (2 minutes): Actions, desk-notify, Run workflow, `test` = `true`; when the run has
  finished, the iPhone shows "Test from your desk: notifications work"; tap it. If no run appears
  within a minute, run it again (a pending run can be replaced, Workflow 3). Then EXP-006.
- N9. **The operating-model project** (10 minutes), once its own Workflow 4 is merged there
  (CS16): in Personal-Org-Operating-Model, the same as N4 (with N2's second state key), N6 (the
  same subscription value) and N7, then N8 there. Your account is on GitHub Pro, so the
  Environment's branch rule works in that private repository.
- N10. **Only if EXP-006 needs it, and at launch.**
  - **The Access exception** (only if EXP-006's cases 1 or 2 fail on a sign-in page): in
    Cloudflare Zero Trust, Access, Applications, add one self-hosted application for each of
    `service-desk-preview.pages.dev/manifest.webmanifest`, `/sw.js` and `/icons/*`, each with one
    policy whose action is Bypass, for Everyone. Do not change or bypass the desk's own
    applications (Setup C2 and C3). That a more specific path's application takes precedence over
    the desk's whole-host one is C, and EXP-006's rerun confirms it. These three files hold no
    records.
  - **At launch** (5 minutes, after AC28's step 3): the desk's address changes, so the old
    subscription stops. Add the `needs-you` desk to the Home Screen, repeat N5, update
    `DESK_PUSH_SUBSCRIPTION` in both projects (N6), run N8 in both, and remove the preview's icon.
    The Builder changes J7's `push.deskUrl` in M3's launch pull request (AC28; the brief's M3
    line), and the
    operating-model project's pin moves to that commit in a reviewed pull request there (CS16);
    until it moves, that project's taps open the preview. Any Access exception is added for
    `needs-you` too, and N12's Pages secret is set on `needs-you` (C6).
- N11. **Optional, GitHub's own usage e-mails** (2 minutes), a complement to the desk's bar and its
  $50 notifications (AC78): in GitHub, Settings, Billing and licensing, turn on "Receive alerts
  when my included usage reaches 90% and 100%" (research Q-015 F7.2). GitHub gives no "every $50"
  alert; a dollar budget e-mails at 75%, 90% and 100% of its amount, and is yours to set or not
  (F7.1). Your card is on file and October is already past the 3,000 included minutes (your
  answer), so nothing stops at 100%: the extra minutes are charged. Nothing here needs your
  approval.
- N12. **The usage token** (5 minutes; draft 4, AC78, J21), on your phone or computer, after N4:
  1. GitHub, Settings, Developer settings, Fine-grained tokens, Generate new token: name "Desk:
     Actions use", resource owner your account, an expiry you choose, Repository access "Public
     repositories (read-only)", Account permissions, Plan: Read-only, and nothing else.
  2. Paste it, once, into Service-Desk, Settings, Environments, `notify`, Add environment secret
     `DESK_BILLING_TOKEN`, and into the preview's Cloudflare Pages project, Settings, Variables and
     secrets, Production, secret `GITHUB_BILLING_TOKEN`. Never paste it into a chat, a file or a
     commit.
  3. Actions, desk-notify, Run workflow, `test` = `usage`, then open the desk's More detail: the
     line "GitHub Actions this month" shows a figure, or why not (EXP-006 case 11).
  4. If the run's log says the token was refused (401 or 403), GitHub's tutorial was right that
     these endpoints take only a classic token (research Q-015 U-1): make a classic token instead
     (Personal access tokens, Tokens (classic)), with no scope ticked, and repeat 2 and 3; if
     still refused, with `read:user`; if still refused, with `user`. Tell your Chief of Staff which
     worked (it is recorded in EXP-006's result). A classic token with `user` can also change your
     profile, so it is the last resort (Risks).

- **Track B (Personal-Org-Operating-Model, not the desk):** queue the owner's answers 5 and 6
  (safe stopping point before compaction; PRs lead with a practical explanation) with the
  discovery-step model change.
- CS7. Ongoing from M2: after the owner answers a question from the desk, delete its
  `questions/<name>.md`, as today. Until that deletion, `review_check.py questions` (model S-004
  AC3; governance/checks/review_check.py:29–30) still lists the question as open in the owner
  signal. The desk itself already shows it answered (AC3). From 2026-10-17 that deletion goes in
  a pull request of its own that the asks-merge workflow may merge (model S-033 AC4, kind (d);
  L-0017), so it usually lands within minutes of its checks passing.

**Before M3's launch:**
- C6 (owner). Put Access applications on `needs-you.pages.dev` and each custom domain it already
  has (if any), and set its Production variables and the `GITHUB_READ_TOKEN` secret (G1's
  permissions, Actions: read included), and, from 2026-10-18, the `GITHUB_BILLING_TOKEN` secret
  (N12, J21).
- C7: no longer needed. EXP-002 found `needs-you` is direct upload, so there is no new project,
  custom domain or Bulk Redirect (P-001-m1-accept.md).
- C8 (owner), in this order (AC28; about 5 minutes):
  1. In Operations-Hub, disable the `needs-you-page` workflow (Actions → `needs-you-page` →
     "…" → Disable workflow), and check no run of it is in progress. It deploys to `needs-you`
     on cron `5 0-5,12-23 * * *` and would overwrite the desk within an hour.
  2. Approve the `production` job.
  3. Open `needs-you` and check it serves the desk; then archive Operations-Hub.

## Facts relied on (each with evidence)
- Pages Functions run on Workers, and their requests count against the Workers quota. Identical
  runtime limits are not documented. Workers Logs and Tail Workers are marked Workers-only. —
  evidence: library/facts/F-cf-06.md:4 (A; the compatibility matrix is one page, B). So EXP-001
  runs on a Pages project, and it may have no per-request CPU log.
- Free plan: 100,000 requests a day, and Error 1027 beyond that — evidence:
  library/F-cf-workers-05.md:8 (A).
- Free plan: 10 ms CPU per request, with network waits excluded, and Error 1102 beyond that —
  evidence: library/F-cf-workers-01.md:8 (A). Occasional overages are tolerated — evidence:
  library/F-cf-workers-08.md:9 (B).
- 50 external subrequests per invocation on Free, and redirect hops count — evidence:
  library/F-cf-workers-02.md:8 (A).
- Six connections may wait for headers at once — evidence: library/F-cf-workers-03.md:8 (A). A
  seventh queues — evidence: library/F-cf-workers-07.md:9 (B). F-cf-workers-03 lists the seventh
  as NOT filed, so it supports only the six.
- No wall-time limit for an HTTP request on Free is filed — evidence: library/F-cf-workers-03.md:8
  (it files Paid's only). EXP-001 measures it.
- A 304 sent with an Authorization header doesn't count against GitHub's primary limit, and a PAT's
  limit is 5,000 an hour — evidence: library/Q-004-facts.md:9 and :8 (A).
- Webhooks can be minutes late and aren't redelivered, so the design polls — evidence:
  library/Q-004-facts.md:6, :14, :15 (A, B).
- `?filename=` prefills the new-file name, and no content parameter is documented — evidence:
  library/F-gh-05.md:8 (B). The owner has used content-prefilled links from the phone's browser and
  the app — evidence: docs/PROJECT.md:318-320 (C, observed by the owner).
- Over-long query links on other GitHub pages return 414, and no limit is documented for the
  new-file page — evidence: library/F-gh-06.md:8 (A). AC17's 6,000 cap is our own margin (E),
  checked in EXP-004.
- The commit step offers the current branch or a new one. Push rulesets may block creating a file,
  and protected branches block web edits — evidence: library/F-gh-07.md:8 (A), library/F-gh-10.md:9
  (A).
- Access: validate the JWT with the public key, preferring the header — evidence:
  library/F-auth-04.md:8 (A; it supports the public-key validation only, and the `exp`, `aud`
  and `email` checks are our design, C). A policy whose only rule is One-time PIN lets anyone in, and policies
  can name emails — evidence: library/F-auth-02.md:8 (A). Pages' "Enable access policy" covers only
  previews; `*.pages.dev` and each custom domain need their own policies — evidence:
  library/facts/F-cf-04.md:4 (A).
- A production deployment changes what `pages.dev` and the custom domains serve, and rollback is
  instant — evidence: library/facts/F-cf-02.md:4 (A). A project can't switch deployment method —
  evidence: library/facts/F-cf-03.md:4 (B). A `pages.dev` address can be 301-redirected to a custom
  domain with Bulk Redirects — evidence: library/facts/F-cf-07.md:4 (A). That was O5's fallback
  route, no longer needed: `needs-you` is direct upload — evidence: EXP-002's run, reported in
  decisions/questions/P-001-m1-accept.md (C until the result file is recorded in M2).
- Fine-grained tokens offer no Checks permission, so a private repository's check runs answer 403
  to the read token — evidence: EXP-004 Q4 on the preview, Personal-Org-Operating-Model's CI line
  "can't read checks" (decisions/questions/P-001-m1-accept.md, "Carried to M2"; C). Actions: read
  is offered and covers `actions/runs` — not in the library (C); M2's join test and the preview
  confirm it.
- Service-Desk's Orchestrator workflow runs on a 30-minute schedule — evidence:
  `.github/workflows/orchestrator.yml:16` (`cron: "*/30 * * * *"`). Its scheduled runs carry the
  default-branch head as `head_sha` — evidence: `gh api …/actions/runs?head_sha=<38722d3…>` on
  2026-10-08 listed a `schedule` run (C).
- On a head the Orchestrator pushed, governance runs by `workflow_dispatch`, not `push` —
  evidence: governance.yml:1–7, orchestrator.yml:459–465, and `gh api …/actions/runs?head_sha=<e2ab9c5…>`
  on 2026-10-08, which listed governance and orchestrator runs with event `workflow_dispatch` and
  none with `push` (C).
- EXP-001's measured load: maximum wall time 1,938 ms, p95 at most 1,413 ms, no 1102 or 1027 —
  evidence: docs/handover/experiments/EXP-001-result.md:8–10.
- Prefilled new-file links opened with their content at every tested length up to 8,000 characters
  on the owner's phone — evidence: EXP-004, reported in decisions/questions/P-001-m1-accept.md (C
  until the result file is recorded in M2).
- A fine-grained PAT is limited to the chosen repositories and permissions — evidence:
  library/facts/L-F5.md:4 (B).
- These GitHub REST behaviours are used but not in the library (C). Each is confirmed against the
  live API by M1's join tests and first deploy:
  - `per_page` up to 100;
  - `Link: rel="next"` paging;
  - check-run `status` and `conclusion` values (M1), and workflow-run `status`, `conclusion`,
    `head_sha` and `event` filters, and latest-attempt listing (M2);
  - `compare/{base}...{head}` giving `status` `ahead`, `identical`, `behind` or `diverged`, and
    taking `per_page` (M2, AC47);
  - `git/trees?recursive=1` with `truncated`;
  - the raw media type for blobs;
  - `commits?path=` listing a deleted path's history;
  - `branches/{b}` giving `commit.commit.tree.sha`.
- Record formats: J2 and J9 quote real files and code lines from Service-Desk at e2ab9c5 and
  Personal-Org-Operating-Model at 677fe5a. These are observations (C), not library facts.
