  - **a job's `if:` is evaluated before its job-level concurrency group is entered**, so a skipped
    job takes no pending place (C, not documented as such; EXP-006 case 5);
  - **GitHub's billing documentation**, read by the Definer on 2026-10-18 from the docs' own source
    files (github/docs at dae83f7, the repository docs.github.com is built from; one publisher, B,
    not yet source-checked): content/billing/concepts/product-billing/github-actions.md:24 (free
    for public repositories on standard runners), :26 ("For private repositories, each GitHub
    account receives a quota of free minutes ... depending on the account's plan"), :28 (minutes
    charged to the repository owner), :121 ("the minutes used by the account are reset to zero"
    each month) and :139 ("If your account does not have a valid payment method on file, usage is
    blocked once you use up your quota"), :210 (e-mails at 90% and 100% of included use);
    data/reusables/billing/actions-included-quotas.md (Pro: 3,000 minutes a month, 1 GB artifact
    storage); data/reusables/billing/default-over-quota-behavior.md (with a payment method,
    spending may be limited by budgets); content/billing/reference/actions-runner-pricing.md:14
    ("rounds the minutes and partial minutes each job uses up to the nearest whole minute");
  - **Apple's page** "Sending web push notifications in web apps and browsers"
    (developer.apple.com/documentation/usernotifications/sending-web-push-notifications-in-web-apps-and-browsers,
    read by the Definer and by plan review round 2 on 2026-10-18; B): "Don't refresh your JWT more
    frequently than once per hour"; 403 "There's an error with your authentication", with a JSON
    `reason`; `BadJwtToken` (the JWT missing, signed with the wrong private key, a bad subject or
    audience, or an expiration "more than one day into the future"); `VapidPkHashMismatch` (the
    subscription's VAPID key does not match the request's); 404 "The request contains an invalid
    :path value"; 410 "The device token has expired"; 429 "too many requests for the same
    destination";
  - **research Q-014** (research/Q-014-memo.md, on the branch orch/s001-notifications, graded, not
    yet source-checked): `workflow_run` runs have `GITHUB_REF` main and run main's file (F1.1,
    F1.3, A); a main-only Environment admits them, fork-triggered ones included (F2.1, F2.2, A by
    deduction; F4.1, F4.3, A); the runs list files them under main (F1.4, F3.1, C); the triggering
    run's branch and repository are only in the payload (F1.5, A/C); the `head_repository` field
    is community knowledge (F6.2, C); asks-merge's judging runs are `workflow_run`-started and run
    main's file (F1.3, A; asks-merge.yml:13-16, :37); REST `branch=` equivalence (F3.3) and the fork-approval
    gate's effect on `workflow_run` (F4.7) UNKNOWN; chain depth at most three (F5.3, A);
  - the iPhone and Web Push facts: research/Q-011-memo.md, revision 6, source check PASS
    (research/Q-011-source-check.md): W1 (A, F-webpush-01b), W3 (B), W4 (A, F-webpush-01), W6 (A,
    F-webpush-07), W8 (A/B, F-webpush-04), W9, W12, D1 (A, F-webpush-08; its payload B), V1 to V10
    (F-webpush-02, -02b, -03, -05, -06; V8's "refresh the JWT at most hourly" B), X1, X2, X7, X9,
    X10 (Access and the service worker, the manifest and the tap: not documented), T8 (F-gh-12,
    F-gh-12b), T10 and T10b (`repository_dispatch` needs Contents write on a
    fine-grained token, B), G1 and G5;
  - events made with a workflow's own token start no new run, except `workflow_dispatch` and
    `repository_dispatch` (library/facts/L-F8.md, B);
  - Service-Desk is public and Personal-Org-Operating-Model private (`gh api repos/<repo>`,
    2026-10-18, observed, C);
  - the Orchestrator's commit job pushes with the workflow's own token, then starts a governance
    run (.github/workflows/orchestrator.yml:416-480); the asks-merge job merges, then starts one
    (asks_merge.py:645-650); its job runs only for runs caused by a pull request
    (.github/workflows/asks-merge.yml:37), and a refusal, a failed read among them, skips a pull
    request until its next run (asks_merge.py:49); every desk-deploy job waits for the owner
    (.github/workflows/desk-deploy.yml:5-7);
  - asks-merge's kinds and checks (governance/checks/asks_merge.py:27-38, :74-75, :81-83,
    `classify` :117, `check_state` :226 and its latest-per-key at :236-247, `find_grant` :334);
  - governance and pr-body run on `pull_request` (opened, synchronize, reopened; pr-body also
    edited), not on `ready_for_review` (.github/workflows/governance.yml:10-14, pr-body.yml:8-10);
  - Personal-Org-Operating-Model at 90f53e3: no asks-merge workflow or grant, no deploy workflow
    or environment; its workflows `ci` (push to main and `build/**`, and pull_request,
    .github/workflows/ci.yml) and `pr-body` (pull_request opened, edited, reopened, synchronize);
    neither runs on `ready_for_review`;
  - asks-merge's log line for a pull request it leaves, `PR #%d: waits: %s` (asks_merge.py:632),
    and its runs that judge nothing (`return 0`, :609-620);
  - the owner-signal job posts only two commit statuses per repository
    (governance/checks/owner_signal.py:1-30; .github/workflows/governance.yml:127-154);
  - `governance_checks questions` requires its seven labels and, in a v3 project, the `Milestone:`
    line, the `## Context` section and the reversibility word, and nothing else
    (governance/checks/governance_checks.py:104-105, :276-296 and :337 on);
  - tests/desk/fixtures/README.md:33-36: Personal-Org-Operating-Model's files are not copied into
    the fixtures; poom/ holds placeholders in their real form.
- **Request arithmetic for AC14 (our estimate, E).** These assumptions can be checked against
  Cloudflare's request count on the preview after M1:
  - **A1:** open windows total at most 16 hours a day across all devices. Two devices open at once
    count twice.
  - **A2:** one poll call per project per 60 s, and no retry sooner than the next poll.
  - **A3:** a poll cycle makes at most 6 blob calls per project (`MAX_BLOB_CALLS_PER_CYCLE`,
    src/public/lib/scheduler.js), each with a budget of 25 (J6: a blob counts 1, a history read 2,
    a compare 1). The needed reads come first; the optional ruling reads (AC20's "Time asked")
    then use what is left of the 6. Ruling reads that don't fit fill in on later cycles, and each
    is made once per device: the page keeps history times and blobs by path and sha.
    - **Service-Desk today:** about 2 calls of needed reads (13 status files, ROUTING.toml, 2 log
      months, outcomes, open cards, unlogged answers, the newest hold card), then its 18 rulings'
      history reads, their questions' history reads and the ruling blobs (about 90 of budget, so
      at least 4 calls). On today's tree a cold load takes about 5 to 6 calls (E, counted from the
      tree, not measured), so the optional reads use most or all of the first cycle.
    - **Personal-Org-Operating-Model:** 5 (the ledger and the 4 sprint files), plus any open
      questions: 1 call.
    - **From MD (J11, 2026-10-14):** Service-Desk's cold load adds about 18 blobs at 3117150 (13
      research requests, docs/PROJECT.md, S-001.md, SURFACES.md, orchestrator.yml and
      merges.jsonl), so about one more call (E). They are optional reads, like the ruling reads:
      they come after the needed reads, fill in on later cycles if the 6 are used, and are made
      once per device. The 12 calls counted per opening below still cover them. A
      v2.5 project makes none of these but one: README.md (AC54, 2026-10-14).
    - **From AC54 (2026-10-14):** every connected project adds one optional blob, its
      `README.md`, read once per device and again only when its sha changes. Service-Desk's
      cold load goes to about 19 added blobs, still about one more call (E);
      Personal-Org-Operating-Model's goes from 5 to 6 blobs, still 1 call. The 12 calls counted
      per opening still cover it, so the total below is unchanged.
    - **From AC60 (J12, 2026-10-16):** Service-Desk's cold load adds one to three pages of its
      pull requests (about 80 today, so one page), each counting 5 of a blob call's 25, and the
      `reviews/<n>/` verdict files (38 small blobs at 2c1acb6), so about two more
      calls (E), filled in on later cycles like the other optional reads. Each head change adds
      the first page again to that minute's blob call (A4), and the first load each day repeats the
      cold read (at most 3 pages, within that load's 12 calls). Personal-Org-Operating-Model makes
      none of these. The 12 calls counted per opening still cover it, so the total is unchanged.
    - **J12's retries (2026-10-17, the desk follow-ups; build review round 2 of trial-view notes
      2, N1).** A failed J12 read was once retried on every cycle. The review counted 120 calls in
      a quiet hour in place of 60, up to 960 more a project in a day. Now it backs off (J12):
      - each retry rides in a blob call the cycle already makes, or makes one of its own: at
        most one call each;
      - each opening starts the backoff again, so a window of L minutes holds at most 4 retries
        in its first 75 minutes (at 5, 15, 35 and 75), then one an hour;
      - over AC14's 10 openings and 960 window-minutes, the most one project can make is 43:
        ten windows of 75 minutes give 40 retries, and the 210 minutes left give 3 more;
      - head changes add none, since no J12 read is made during a wait. A rate limit's 60
        minutes can only lengthen the waits.
    - **From model 3.5.0's records (J13, J2; 2026-10-17):** each Chief of Staff session adds one
      small blob, its session file, read once per device and again only when its sha changes (a
      change that lands as a commit is a head change, counted in A4). Service-Desk's default
      branch holds none at 368effa; at about one session a day (E), a device's first load reads
      them at 25 to a call, about one more call a month, filled in on later cycles like the
      other optional reads. The question file of an answer with no `Milestone:` line, read
      while the tree still holds it (J2), is a blob already in the tree, at most a few at a time until tidied. The
      12 calls counted per opening still cover them, so the total is unchanged. J11's reached
      part and J2's new lines add no read: they are in blobs already read. J12's merge-commit
      message and author (the owner's answer on the asks-merge job's merges) ride J12's one query
      and add no request: they make its answer larger, not more frequent.
    - **From notifications (2026-10-18, draft 4):** the senders (Workflows 3 and 4) never call
      the desk's function and read their own repository with their own workflow token, so AC14's
      count is unchanged by them; Service-Desk's sender's hourly usage read (J21) uses the owner's
      own limit, at most 5 requests an hour. The page's J20 reads ride its blob call: one to three
      requests per open pull request per head change, each counting 1 of the call's budget of 25,
      a few a day in practice (E). AC14's test gains three open pull requests changing heads every
      10 minutes for its 16 hours (review round 2, N5): 3 × 6 × 16 = 288 head changes, not A4's
      default-branch changes and not a few a day (review round 3, N8). At worst each takes its own
      blob call, 288 calls, so that case's total is 5,500 + 288 = 5,788, still under 10,000. That
      headroom would hide a missing head cache (re-reading every minute would add 3 × 60 × 16 =
      2,880 and still pass), so AC14's case also asserts exactly one J20 read per open pull
      request per head change, 288 in all, which a missing cache fails. AC77's re-read on a tap is
      one poll call and at most one blob call, made only when the last read is over 30 seconds
      old, a few a day (E). J21's usage read rides a blob call at most once an hour per device, 3
      to 5 of its budget, at most 16 a device a day, and adds no call of its own; its requests use
      the owner's hourly GitHub limit, a few dozen a day with the sender's. The 12 calls counted
      per opening still cover the rest.
    - **For the arithmetic** (E), every opening is counted at 12 calls per project: 6 in its first
      cycle and up to 6 more for ruling reads on later cycles. At most 10 openings a day across
      devices: 5 × 12 × 10 = 600. That over-counts: ruling reads are made once per device, not per
      opening.
  - **A4:** each head change is read inside that minute's poll call (the tree), plus at most one
    blob call. At most 100 changes a day across all projects while open: 100. A change to a J11
    file adds at most that file's blob to the same call (a new dispatch-log line already changes a
    needed blob).
  - **Total:** 4,800 (5 × 60 × 16) + 600 + 100 = **5,500**, well under the 10,000 margin.
  - **With J12 failing all day** (AC14's added case, 2026-10-17): all 5 projects are v3 and every
    J12 read fails, so 5,500 + 5 × 43 = **5,715**, still under the margin. Before the backoff it
    could reach 5,500 + 4,800 = 10,300 (the review's count).
  - **With J12 flapping** (plan review round 1 of the desk follow-ups, N7; E). An answer resets
    the backoff, so reads that answer and fail in turn fall outside the all-day-failing count.
    The worst such case found is a 3-page cold read whose first two pages answer and whose third
    fails each time. It makes 3 reads every 7 minutes, about 411 a project a day, so 5 × 411 ≈
    2,057 for 5 projects, about 7,557 calls in all. That is still under the margin.
  - **Worked case outside AC14 (A1 broken):** two devices each open for 16 hours make 9,600 polls.
    That is 10,300 to 11,000 in all, depending on whether openings and changes double. It is above
    the margin, but about a tenth of Free's 100,000, so there is no Error 1027. If the check
    finds A1 broken, the owner chooses between a wider margin and a longer poll interval when several
    devices are open.
  - **GitHub, per hour:** steady polls are 4 × 5 × 60 = 1,200 requests from M2 (3 × 5 × 60 = 900
    in M1), mostly free 304s (PAT-Q004-1, library/Q-004-facts.md:18). Three cold loads in one hour
    add at most 3 × 5 × 6 × 25 = 2,250 full reads in their first cycles (E: only a project with
    many rulings uses all 6 calls). Ruling reads that spill into later cycles are once per device,
    and only Service-Desk has them today. About 3,450 is under 5,000. The function's own request
    count (AC14) doesn't change: still one poll call per project per minute.
- **Known limits:**
  - The content prefill parameter is undocumented (C). GitHub could change it; AC17 is the
    fallback, and its name is config (J7).
  - The dispatch-log times stand in for commit times (J2), checked in three cases only (C).
  - Pages may expose no per-request CPU figure (F-cf-06). EXP-001 then rests on Error 1102 counts,
    which tolerated overages can mask (F-cf-workers-08, B).
  - J10's `usage` form is now model S-020's frozen form (AC2), adopted here as L-0005; J10's
    earlier proposal was its starting point (P-001-s001-owner-questions.md, answer 3). Whether
    `autocompact_threshold`, `compactions` and `would_have_stopped` are reported at the pinned
    agent-tool version is S-020's live check; until then they are null and the desk shows them as
    "not available" (AC23's one rule).
  - CI reads push and dispatch runs only (J1's 3a and 3b). A scheduled run, or a pull-request
    run sharing the head's sha, isn't read (C, my choice). desk-deploy and Orchestrator runs are
    shown as activity, not CI.
  - MD's design answers rest on research Q-010, a graded memo of published guidance, and on one
    owner's judgment; the glance test (EXP-005) is a small sample by design (E).

## Experiments in Build (EXP-001, EXP-002 and EXP-004 on the preview after M1's merge; EXP-003 at M2's start; EXP-005 at MD's end)
- **Outcomes at M1's acceptance** (2026-10-07, decisions/questions/P-001-m1-accept.md):
  - **EXP-001 passed** every set: no Error 1102 or 1027, no call at or over 30 s, maximum wall
    time 1.9 s (1,938 ms), p95 at most 1.4 s (docs/handover/experiments/EXP-001-result.md).
    `BLOB_BATCH` stays 25, so no fallback rerun was needed. The page's 30 s call timeout (J6)
    stands. Cloudflare showed no CPU figure, so the CPU part rests on the 1102 count (Known
    limits).
  - **EXP-002:** `needs-you` is a direct-upload project, not Git-integrated. The O5 fallback, the
    custom domain and Setup C7 are not needed; AC28 deploys straight into `needs-you`.
  - **EXP-004:** every prefilled-link length tested, up to 8,000 characters, opened GitHub's
    new-file page with the content filled in on the owner's phone. `contentPrefill: true`,
    `contentParam: "value"` and `linkCap: 6000` stand. Q4: the read token can't read check runs
    on private Personal-Org-Operating-Model, which AC29 answers.
  - **Still to record:** the EXP-002 and EXP-004 result files, in `docs/handover/experiments/`,
    by the Builder in M2 from the Chief of Staff's hand-over (CS5). If EXP-004's Q1 found a
    repository that doesn't take web commits to `main`, that repository's `webCommitsToDefault`
    becomes `false` in the same pull request (AC18).
- **Ruling:** the owner's ruling of 2026-10-06 (P-001-build-path.md, question 0: "the experiments
  run during the build") replaces "measured in Define" for O3 and O5. The fallbacks are the ones the
  owner already ruled.
- **When they run:** EXP-001, EXP-002 and EXP-004 run on the preview after M1's pull request has
  merged and desk-deploy has deployed `main`. They never run on an M1 branch.
- **Registration:** each experiment is registered in `experiments/` (D-047).
- **Where results go:** `docs/handover/experiments/<id>-result.md` on `main`, by one of two
  routes.
  - **The owner commits it** through the prefilled link the EXP-001 panel offers. It is a human
    commit, which may touch any path.
  - **Or a later pull request records it** from what the owner or the Chief of Staff hands over.
    `docs/handover/**` is the Builder's surface in governance/SURFACES.md, so that pull request is
    the Builder's. A Chief of Staff commit there would fail `surface_guard`.
  - A result that changes code or config (`BLOB_BATCH`, J7's values) is applied in a fix-forward
    pull request before M1 is accepted (AC15).
- **No secrets for the Builder:** the Builder holds no secret and can't reach the Access-protected
  preview, so every live step is either the owner's or a workflow's.
- **Owner time, pre-launch** (estimates, E). The ceiling is the pre-launch 2 hours a week
  (PROJECT.md §6).

  | Item | Minutes |
  |---|---|
  | Setup C1–C5, G1–G2 (once) | 45 |
  | Reviewing and merging the Chief of Staff's PRs: CS1 (this spec), CS3 (workflows), CS4 (CI wiring), CS2 and CS8 (brief and ledger), about 5 each | 25 |
  | Answering this spec's five owner questions | 10 |
  | M1's review, merge, and trying the preview | 20 |
  | Deploy approvals: about 1 minute per merge to `src/**` and per dispatched run (M1, two fix-forwards, `exp-002`) | 4 |
  | EXP-001 | 15 |
  | Each EXP-001 fallback rerun (`BLOB_BATCH` 12, then 6), about 10 each | 0–20 |
  | EXP-004 | 15 |
  | EXP-002, only if the dashboard is needed | 0–5 |
  | CS9's gate check at M1's acceptance, by the owner on the phone | 2 |
  | **Total** | **136–161** |

  That is more than one week's 2 hours, so it is spread over two weeks:
  - **Week 1:** Setup, the Chief of Staff's PRs and the questions, about 80 minutes.
  - **Week 2:** M1's merge, the deploy approvals, the preview and the experiments, 56 to 81 minutes.

  M1's acceptance is planned for the second week accordingly. (It came on 2026-10-07. No EXP-001
  rerun was needed.)

- **Owner time, M1's acceptance to launch** (estimates, E; amendment of 2026-10-08). The same
  ceiling of 2 hours a week applies.

  | Item | Minutes |
  |---|---|
  | Answering this amendment's owner questions | 10 |
  | G3: giving the read token Actions: read (once) | 3 |
  | M2: review, merge, trying the preview, and CS9 | 22 |
  | MD: design-lab rounds, about 4 to 6 of 10 to 15 minutes each, on the phone | 40–90 |
  | MD: the phone-notification ruling, from Q-011's findings (AC45); ruled 2026-10-08, done | 10 |
  | MD: the sandbox pull request, then the pull request that makes the chosen design the default: review, merge, trying the preview | 35 |
  | MD: EXP-005's glance test, five glances of about 2 minutes, plus reading the result | 15 |
  | MD: CS9 at acceptance | 2 |
  | M3: review, merge, trying the preview, and CS9 | 22 |
  | M3: Setup C6 and C8, and the launch approval | 20 |
  | Deploy approvals, about 1 minute each (M2, MD and M3 merges, and about three fix-forwards) | 9 |
  | **Total** | **188–238** |

  That is two weeks' ceiling at the least. Spread over the build, it fits about one hour a week
  for four weeks:
  - **Week of 12 October:** questions, G3, M2.
  - **Weeks of 19 and 26 October:** the design lab, the notification ruling, the sandbox.
  - **Week of 2 November:** the merged design, the glance test, MD's acceptance, then M3 and
    launch.

  That makes V4's date of 2026-11-11 tight but possible, with no slack for a slow lab. Open
  question 1 asks the owner whether V4's date should move.

- **Owner time for notifications** (estimates, E; added 2026-10-18, draft 2; draft 4 adds N12), on
  top of the table above:

  | Item | Minutes |
  |---|---|
  | The notification questions (seven answered, one open) | 10 |
  | N1, N2: the key pair, the contact and two state keys | 7 |
  | The sender's and the receiving side's pull requests: review, merge, trying the preview | 25 |
  | Workflow 3's and Workflow 4's pull requests, and Workflow 4's review there | 15 |
  | CS9's added checks of `notify` in both projects, at MD's and M3's acceptance, about 2 each | 4 |
  | Pin-move reviews in the operating-model project, about 10 each, about two before launch | 20 |
  | N11, GitHub's own usage e-mails (optional) | 0–2 |
  | N12: the usage token, its two secrets and its test (EXP-006 case 11) | 5 |
  | N3, N4 to N8: the deploy approval, the Environment and secrets, subscribing, the switch, the test | 18 |
  | N9: the operating-model project's Environment, secrets, switch and test | 10 |
  | EXP-006, over two days | 20 |
  | A fix-forward pull request from EXP-006, if needed, and its deploy approval | 0–12 |
  | N10 at launch | 5 |
  | **Total** | **139–153** |

  About two and a half hours, ahead of MD3's remaining work. **V4's date of 2026-11-11 is at
  risk:** the earlier table already used about four weeks of the 2-hour weekly ceiling with no
  slack, and this adds more than one week's ceiling of owner time, before MD3 and M3 can finish.
  So launch by 11 November holds only if MD3 and M3 then go without a slow round. The owner rules
  whether to move it (PROJECT.md §6, the miss rule; open question 1 of 2026-10-08).

- **EXP-001 (O3), CPU and wall time** — experiments/EXP-001-o3-cpu-walltime.md.
  - **Runner: the owner's signed-in browser** (about 15 minutes of the owner's time). There is no
    test-only path in J8: an Access service token carries no `email`, so it could never pass J8.
  - **Method:** the owner opens the preview's experiment panel (`?exp=001`, built by the Builder in
    M1) on a desktop browser, starts the run, and leaves the tab in the foreground for about 75
    minutes.
    - The panel drives the real M1 function through the owner's own Access session. It runs steady
      polls, cold loads against both repositories, and heaviest-case blob calls against
      Personal-Org-Operating-Model, the largest connected repository (1,963 KB, 318 tracked files, a
      128,438-byte ledger).
    - The owner then reads the Functions CPU figures in Cloudflare's dashboard, if it shows any, and
      commits the panel's summary to `main` through the prefilled link the panel offers. The raw
      table goes in through AC17's copy fallback.
  - **Pass:**
    - zero Error 1102, zero 1027 and zero wall-time failures in every set;
    - every call completes in under 30 s, as measured by the page;
    - if Cloudflare shows a CPU figure for the Functions, its 99th percentile is under 10 ms.
  - **Fallback**, ruled in decisions/questions/P-001-o3-define.md and P-001-o3-walltime.md:
    - first, smaller batches: `BLOB_BATCH` 25 → 12 → 6, re-run at each size;
    - otherwise, the $5 Paid plan, which needs a new ceiling from the owner.
  - **Result: passed** at `BLOB_BATCH` 25 (above). No fallback was used.
- **EXP-002 (O5), how `needs-you` deploys** — experiments/EXP-002-o5-needs-you-deploy-method.md.
  - **Runner: the Chief of Staff,** through desk-deploy's read-only `exp-002` job. The Chief of
    Staff reads its log (CS5). If the log can't settle it, the owner checks the dashboard (about 5
    minutes).
  - **Pass (Direct Upload):** M3 deploys into `needs-you` through the `production` job, as AC28
    says.
  - **Fallback (Git-integrated)**, ruled in decisions/questions/P-001-o5-define.md: a new Pages
    project with a custom domain, and a permanent redirect from the old address (F-cf-07, A). A 301
    counts as meeting V4 (decisions/questions/P-001-move-to-build-values.md).
  - **Result: Direct Upload** (P-001-m1-accept.md). The fallback is not needed.
- **EXP-004, per-repository values** — experiments/EXP-004-prefilled-link-per-repo.md.
  - **Runners:** the owner's phone test (about 15 minutes), plus what the deployed preview itself
    shows. No step needs the Builder to hold a token.
    - **Q1, web commits:** what GitHub's commit dialog offers the owner during the phone test.
      The Chief of Staff may also check the branch rules with its own GitHub access.
    - **Q4, reading checks:** whether the preview's CI field reads, or says "can't read checks".
  - **What it records:** `webCommitsToDefault`, `contentPrefill`, `contentParam` and `linkCap` for
    each repository, and whether the read token can read check runs.
  - **Pass:** each value is recorded and applied in J7.
  - **Fallbacks:** AC17 if the content doesn't prefill, AC18 if web commits are blocked, and J1's
    "can't read checks" if check runs are refused.
  - **Result:** Q2 and Q3 passed, every length up to 8,000 characters. Q4: check runs were refused
    on Personal-Org-Operating-Model, so from M2 CI is read from workflow runs (AC29, Setup G3).
- **EXP-003 (O6), usage fields** — experiments/EXP-003-o6-session-usage-fields.md.
  - **Runner:** the Chief of Staff, at M2's start, with the results handed to D7.1 and J10.
  - **Fallback**, ruled in decisions/questions/P-001-o6-fallback.md, option A: show what is
    reported, and mark the rest "not available".
- **EXP-005, MD's 5-second glance test** — experiments/EXP-005-md-glance-test.md.
  - **Runner:** the owner, on the phone, on the preview once the chosen design is the default view
    (AC44 step 4). The Chief of Staff prepares the prompts and records the result (CS12). About 15
    minutes.
  - **What it checks:** that a glance of 5 seconds at the first screen tells the owner which
    projects need them, which they would open first and why, and whether any project can't be read
    (AC35, AC38, AC41).
  - **Pass:** as the registration's deciding threshold says.
  - **If it fails:** the design goes back to the lab for the failing question, and the merged
    design is fixed forward, then the test is taken again. At most two such loops; after a second
    failed retest, the owner rules (accept as is, another loop, or a different candidate).

- **EXP-006, Cloudflare Access with the desk's notifications** (added 2026-10-18, draft 4; AC74 to
  AC78, J16 to J21; Q-011's open question Q-f; Q-014's and Q-015's unknowns) —
  experiments/EXP-006-access-push.md, which governs; in short:
  - **cases:** (1) the Home Screen web app opens with no Safari bar; (2) "Turn on notifications"
    subscribes, recording which path; (3) `test: true` shows and its tap opens the desk; (4) the
    same with the Access session ended; (5) the delay from the record's GitHub time to the push's
    `201` and to its showing, for (5a) a pull request opened, (5b) a question filed through the
    asks-merge job, with no `Asked in session:` line, (5c) a run started by a workflow's own token
    reaching the sender, (5d) a deploy requested, (5e) an owner's own push, (5f) a pull request
    marked ready, (5g) in the operating-model project, a pull request opened there and one marked
    ready, (5h) a job candidate the asks-merge job leaves, if one occurs in the window, through
    an asks-merge run started by `workflow_run`; (6) the `tag` inside a Declarative push replacing
    an earlier one; (7) `test: fail` making one request, refused, nothing on the phone, and
    whether AC76's issue reaches the owner through GitHub Mobile; (8) a reused JWT accepted within
    its hour; (9) a week's Actions minutes for the account and for Workflow 4, and whether the
    operating-model project's ready workflow, its only job skipped, still starts Workflow 4; (10)
    Q-014's unknowns on a real run: the `head_repository` field and the origin gate, the REST read
    of the triggering run as a second signal, asks-merge's runs admitted by `path`, REST
    `branch=main` listing a `workflow_run` run with `head_branch` `main`, and the runs list's
    `status=success` filter; (11) Q-015's unknowns: whether a fine-grained token with Plan: read
    reads the usage (`test: usage`, Setup N12), else which classic scope does, the form of
    `repositoryName`, and the desk's minutes against GitHub's billing page with the time between
    (the lag);
  - **when:** once the notifications pull requests are deployed and Workflow 3 is on; case 5b's
    question carries no `Asked in session:` line, so AC77's hold does not touch it;
  - **threshold:** (1) to (4) pass, and (5a) to (5e) and (5g) under 5 minutes each; (5f), (5h),
    (6) to (11) decide only their own choices;
  - **fallbacks:** a sign-in page answering a fetch: Setup N10's Access exception, then the case
    again; (1) or (2) failing otherwise: fixed forward, run again once, then the owner rules; (3)
    or (4): the tap opens the desk's root, a known limit in AC74; (5) over 5 minutes: AC74 states
    the measured delay; (6): a repeat may add a notification, a known limit; (7): the issue stays,
    its limit stated; (8): the reading of Apple's hourly rule recorded, the JWT still minted at
    most hourly, the item kept unsent until Apple accepts; (9) over 600 minutes a month projected
    for Workflow 4: the cost restated to the owner as information, the ready workflow's job given
    its `true` step if a skipped job started nothing, and no further cut below the 3-hourly
    check; (10) the field absent or wrong: the program reads the triggering run through REST
    before reading any secret's value; if that lacks it too, no `workflow_run` run is admitted and
    AC74 states the 5-minute check's wait; J18 relies on its other checks and the HMAC, with
    `status=completed` if `success` is refused; (11) the fine-grained token refused: the classic
    fallback (N12 step 4), its scope recorded; neither works: AC78's line reads "can't be read",
    and the owner is told; the minutes far from GitHub's page: AC78's counting rule reviewed;
  - **runner and time:** the owner, about 20 minutes over two days and a week for (9); the Chief
    of Staff reads the logs, times and minutes, and the result goes in
    `docs/handover/experiments/EXP-006-result.md` through the Builder's route (above).

## Risks and mitigations
- **No freeze (process).**
  - This spec stays `draft`. AGENTS.md §1 says "Specs are frozen before build or measurement". The
    recorded exception is the owner's ruling, decisions/questions/P-001-build-path.md question 0:
    build from the approved brief and the draft spec, outside the pipeline. A ledger entry records
    it (Setup CS8).
  - Under the owner's lighter path, no Critic gate or Check author follows.
    The Builder writes the tests too, and the Reviewer judges each milestone against these criteria.
  - So the "builder is never the judge" rule rests on the Reviewer alone.
  - Mitigation:
    - each milestone's PR quotes the ACs it claims, with the test names;
    - the Reviewer checks every AC of the milestone, not a sample;
    - the owner merges.
  - CI's `v3_checks scope` judges Builder commits carrying `Spec: S-001` against this spec's Areas
    touched as it stands at each commit, so this spec must be on `main` before the Builder's first
    commit (Setup CS1).
- **CPU or wall time doesn't fit Free (O3).** The function parses no record content, which shrinks
  the risk. EXP-001 measures it at M1 with the owner's ruled fallback.
- **`needs-you` is Git-integrated (O5).** Closed: EXP-002 found it is direct upload, so AC28
  deploys straight into it.
- **Deploy credential.** The Cloudflare API token can probably deploy any Pages project in the
  account, `needs-you` included. That per-project scoping isn't offered is my inference (C).
  - **Who holds it:** no agent reads it. It lives only in the `preview` and `production` GitHub
    environments. Agents' admin-level access to the repository could still change those
    environments (R4-B2, CS9).
  - **What runs with it:** only desk-deploy, on `push` to `main` or `workflow_dispatch`. Every job
    waits in an environment for the owner's approval (`preview` and `production` alike). The owner
    approves only a run whose SHA equals the merge-commit SHA of a pull request the Reviewer
    passed.
    `main` is not protected and agents push under the owner's account, so this approval is the main
    control (R3-B1), not "main is reviewed". It is not airtight (R4-B2, below).
  - **The read token:** `GITHUB_READ_TOKEN` is reachable only by deployed Functions, and nothing
    deploys without that approval.
  - **What is left:**
    - A change to `desk-deploy.yml` could misuse the token. `.github/**` is the Chief of Staff's
      lane, guarded by `surface_guard`, and only the owner merges it.
    - A mistaken approval click would deploy unreviewed code.
    - wrangler's transitive dependencies are trusted by pinning only.
    - The Reviewer's reading of `src/package.json`, `src/wrangler.toml` and the lockfile is itself
      a security control.
    - Option (b), a ruleset on `main`, is possible later hardening; its availability is C.
    - Agents act under the owner's admin account, so they could remove the environment gate itself
      (R4-B2). The backstop is CS9's check at each milestone acceptance; the alternative is a
      narrower agent credential; the owner chose the backstop (closed owner question 4).
- **The owner's time before M1 is accepted.** It comes to 136–161 minutes (E, see "Experiments in
  Build"), more than one week's 2 hours. So it is planned over two weeks. If the EXP-001 reruns or
  the owner questions run long, M1's acceptance moves, and V4's date with it. (M1 was accepted on
  2026-10-07.) From M1 to launch it comes to 188–238 minutes, spread over about four weeks (the
  second owner-time table); a slow design lab moves launch, and V4's date with it (owner question
  1).
- **GitHub drops the content parameter.** AC17's copy fallback, switched per repository in J7.
- **A missed item while the desk shows "Quiet" (the owner's worst case).** These all guard it:
  - AC4 and AC5 make "Quiet" require a fresh, complete read of every project;
  - paging past 3 pages, or a truncated tree, makes a project `partial`, never quiet;
  - a misparsed card is still flagged;
  - a `waiting-owner` status without its card file is still flagged.
- **D7.1 doesn't land in time.** (Resolved: D7.1 landed as model S-020, adopted as L-0005.) Launch waits for it (owner's ruling, P-001-build-path.md question
  5). AC22's timeline works from today's records, so M2's other parts are not blocked. Only AC23's
  real figures and the launch are.
- **Records grow.** `outcomes.jsonl` and the ledger are not rotated, and dispatch logs grow about
  50 KB a month today. Each is read only when it changes, and parsing happens on the page, so growth
  costs bytes, not function CPU.
  - EXP-001's heaviest case sets the tested size.
  - The Chief of Staff re-runs EXP-001's heaviest case when any single record a call returns grows
    past twice the largest tested blob (J6's `cost.bytes` shows it).
- **Read token leaks.** It is fine-grained and read-only, limited to the connected repositories
  (L-F5), held only as a Pages secret, and never sent to the page or a model (PROJECT.md §5). D5.2
  reviews it at M1.
- **Too many agents for T1.** Build runs with at most two builder or reviewer agents at once
  (PROJECT.md §5).
- **Design work that looks finished but isn't used as a glance** (discovery map trap 11, "judged as
  pictures, used as glances"). The lab fills mockups with real records and H5's extremes, the
  sandbox runs on live data, and EXP-005 tests a real glance on the preview before MD is accepted.
- **The redesign breaks what M1 and M2 promise.** AC36 keeps every view reachable, AC35 keeps
  AC1–AC8 on the first screen, AC42 keeps the request budget, headers and 360 px, and the Reviewer
  checks M1's and M2's tests still pass on each MD pull request.
- **The sandbox is mistaken for the real view.** It is entered only by `?sandbox`, carries a marker
  on every screen, and is removed once the chosen design becomes the default (AC43, AC44).
- **Constraint creep** (discovery map trap 10): a mockup that relies on a chart library, a web font
  or extra requests can't be built. AC43 binds the lab's mockups to AC42's constraints, and the
  Chief of Staff checks each mockup against them before the owner picks.
- **Notifications widen the desk's trust boundary.** Beyond the existing card issues, a desk
  notification channel needs a server-side sender, stored state or a write credential (AC45).
  *Ruled 2026-10-08* (decisions/questions/P-001-notifications.md): Web Push sent by the
  Orchestrator's GitHub run, the subscription a GitHub secret the owner sets, no database and no
  new Cloudflare service, built in M3. Nothing is built until M3's criteria are written, and the
  Reviewer's security review still applies (plan review round 3, N3).
  - *From 2026-10-18, draft 3 (draft 4 noted):* brought forward and specified (AC73 to AC78,
    Workflows 3 and 4).
    **Stated to the owner plainly:** each sender runs unattended, on a schedule and on events, with
    the notification secrets (the VAPID private key, the subscription, the state key); no approval
    of each run is possible for notifications. So any code that reaches `main` in either project,
    including an agent's push made under the owner's account (R3-B1), runs with them on the next
    run. **The worst case** (round 2, Critic note 4): such code copies the VAPID private key and
    the subscription out, for example into Service-Desk's public run log; anyone holding them can
    then send the owner's phone notifications, with any words and, on the Declarative path, any
    address (whether iOS opens another origin's address is not documented, C), until the owner
    makes new keys and a new subscription (rollback). Both projects hold the same key pair and
    subscription, so a leak in either touches both. Pinned code running in the operating-model
    project also reads that private repository's contents with its workflow token, and could send
    them out over the network: so each pin move is a reviewed change there that reads the diff
    (CS16). It could not reach Cloudflare, the desk's deploys, or write to either repository
    beyond an issue. From draft 4 it could also copy Service-Desk's `DESK_BILLING_TOKEN` (J21):
    a fine-grained token with Plan: read shows the account's billing use and nothing more; a
    classic token, if N12 step 4 needs one, reaches what its scope gives, and with `user` that
    includes changing the owner's profile, so the narrowest that works is kept and a leak is
    handled as the keys are (rollback). The controls:
    - each sender reads only its own repository with its own workflow token; no token crosses
      projects (Workflow 4);
    - the origin gate skips runs a fork's pull request started; no `pull_request_target`; nothing
      from the triggering run is checked out, downloaded or run; no `${{ }}` in any `run:`; an
      outside author's title never reaches the lock screen (Workflow 3, AC74);
    - the state is taken only from the sender's own runs, checked and signed; keys are keyed
      hashes; issues name counts only (J18, AC76);
    - the Environment `notify` is limited to `main`, and CS9 checks it at each milestone;
    - `src/notify/**` and `src/config/projects.json` are proposed as R3, so each change there
      needs the owner's approval, landing before the sender's first pull request (CS18);
    - **the limit, stated plainly** (Critic round 3, note 5): the sender also runs the desk's
      readers in `src/lib/` (J16) and reads `src/config/`. `src/lib/**` is not R3, since it is most
      of the desk's code: a routine change there reaches Service-Desk's sender on its next run with
      the Reviewer's review only, and the operating-model project's sender only through a pin move
      whose review reads the diff (CS16). A Reviewer reading a `src/lib/` change is told, in the
      brief, that the sender runs it with the secrets;
    - the workflow files are checked on every pull request (Workflow 3's check);
    - one repository variable per project stops its sender (rollback).
  - **Passed on, a model matter:** asks-merge's skipped runs share its concurrency group, so a
    skipped run can replace a pending run that would have merged (asks-merge.yml:30-37). J16's rule
    (b) counts only runs that judged the pull request; the group is the model's to change, raised
    by the Chief of Staff in Personal-Org-Operating-Model.
- **Touching the live page before launch.** The preview is a separate Pages project. Nothing
  deploys to `needs-you` before AC28, and EXP-002 is read-only (L-0115).

## Stop rule, rollback, cost
stop rule: three Reviewer FAILs on one milestone's pull request stop that milestone, and the owner
rules (continue, re-aim or stop); V4's date and PROJECT.md §6's miss rule still apply.
rollback: before launch, delete the preview project, and nothing else changes; after launch, roll
`needs-you` back to the last Operations-Hub deployment (instant, F-cf-02), un-archive
Operations-Hub, and re-enable its `needs-you-page` workflow. MD's sandbox and design changes are rolled back on the preview by reverting their
merge and redeploying (owner-approved). Notifications (from 2026-10-18): clear `DESK_NOTIFY_ON`
in each of the two projects, which stops that project's sender; if the keys may have leaked
(Risks), make a new key pair (Setup N1), update `DESK_VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY` in both projects, have the
Builder change J7's public key and move the other project's pin (CS16), then turn notifications on
again on the phone and update `DESK_PUSH_SUBSCRIPTION` in both (N5, N6), which ends the old
subscription; if the usage token may have leaked, delete it in GitHub's token settings and make
a new one (N12); then, to remove them, disable or delete each
`desk-notify.yml`, delete each `notify` Environment with its secrets, delete the usage token and
the `GITHUB_BILLING_TOKEN` Pages secret, turn notifications off for
the desk in the iPhone's Settings or remove its Home Screen icon, and revert the desk's
notification pull requests (owner-approved deploy).
cost: hosting $0/month (Cloudflare Free; EXP-001 passed); pay-per-use AI $0/month (agents on the
owner's subscription); owner time within the pre-launch 2 hours a week (PROJECT.md §6). The
owner steps before M1's acceptance come to 136–161 minutes over two weeks (E; the table in
"Experiments in Build"). From M1's acceptance to launch, M2, MD and M3 come to 188–238 minutes
over about four weeks (E; the second table there), M3's C6 and C8 included. The design lab's pages
are Claude artifacts, private to the owner's account, on the owner's existing plan at no extra cost (E).
Notifications (from 2026-10-18, draft 4), stated as information, with no approval step (the
owner's cost instruction): Service-Desk's runs, its usage reads included, are free as a public
repository's; Personal-Org-Operating-Model's Workflow 4 uses about 500 minutes a month (E; about
690 if its ready workflow's job must run), all charged, since the account is past its 3,000
included minutes with a card on file: about $3.00 a month (about $4.15) at $0.006 a minute
(Workflow 4; research Q-015 F4.1). The desk shows the account's use and cost, and notifies each
$50 (AC78). The operating-model project's own `ci` appears to be most of the account's use,
outside this spec. The owner's time is the table "Owner time for notifications".

**Open** (amendment of 2026-10-08):
1. **Launch date.** With the design step, your part from now to launch comes to about 3 to 4
   hours, spread over about four weeks. 11 November is about four and a half weeks away. That
   leaves about three working days of slack in all (my estimate, E): one extra design-lab round,
   one Reviewer FAIL, or a failed glance test would push launch past it. Options:
   - (a) **Recommended:** keep 11 November as the aim, and decide at MD's acceptance whether to
     move it; the 14-day measuring windows of V1, V2, V3 and V5 move with it.
   - (b) Move it now to 25 November.
2. **What the CI line covers.** From M2 the desk reads each project's CI from GitHub Actions (your
   token gains Actions: read). The CI line will show the checks that ran on the latest commit,
   whether you, an agent or the Orchestrator made it. Two kinds of run are shown separately as
   activity, not as CI: deploys (which wait for your approval) and the Orchestrator's own runs.
   The Orchestrator's half-hourly timed runs are not read at all; they would pile up and stop the
   desk reading after a quiet week. Recommended: agree.

**Answered: the design lab's screens** (2026-10-14; the design-lab open questions, answerable in one
pass). *Answered 2026-10-16 in decisions/questions/P-001-design-lab-open.md: every answer as recommended except 1,
which drops the bar (see "Where the build stands", the design-lab answers). Answers 5 and 6
wait on their model changes; for 6 the owner confirmed it in
decisions/questions/P-001-design-lab-answers-followup.md ("After the model change
(Recommended)"). Kept as they were put:*

2. **One agent panel across projects.** Your ruling said one panel across projects. The lab put the
   panel inside each project and lights each Home card where an agent is working, so nothing
   crosses between projects. Is that what you meant?

   you accepted, shows "work filed". Should the model record when you accept a milestone?

   and don't stop "Quiet". Should they count?
7. **Hold cards** (the Orchestrator stuck on a failing check). I put them under Decisions. Right
   group, or a group of their own?
8. **Two settings to tune in the lab:** three upcoming lines in your tile on the Team screen, and
   30 minutes for an agent to show "Just finished".
9. **The gate document.** The desk links to a gate's case and the Critic's view on GitHub. Do you
   also want it shown inside the desk?
10. **The model changes the screens need** ("What this spec does not cover"): your Chief of Staff's
    sessions recorded (you've agreed in principle), written plain-English summaries, a recorded
    "who has the ball" and deploy approver, a milestone's "reached", the verdicts a pull request
    still needs with the Reviewer's statement, and dependencies between waiting items. Which
    should go to the model first? Until then each shows "not recorded".

1. `questions/P-001-answer-pr-on-desk.md`:

   ```
   # Ruling needed: While your answer is being recorded, should its pull request still count as waiting on you?

   Milestone: none (notifications are not yet a milestone; this question shapes how the desk counts)

   WHY: When your Chief of Staff marks a question as asked in your session, the desk will show it, once built, as "being asked", then "your answer is being recorded" once the pull request that records your answer opens, with no answer link either way. The pull request itself still shows under Merges as waiting on you while it is open, as you chose on 17 October, though the automatic job merges it within minutes; it sends no notification, as you ruled on 18 October. So the desk still shows one thing for you to do when there is nothing. A question asked without the mark, or still open after 8 hours, shows its answer link as before. Left unruled, the pull request keeps counting.
   OPTIONS:
     A. Keep counting it — As you chose on 17 October. It shows under Merges as waiting on you, with the words "Your answer to <question>: the automatic job merges it, usually within minutes", and the desk is not "Quiet" until it merges. No extra reads.
     B. Show the automatic job's pull requests as being recorded — Only pull requests the job may merge itself (ones that only file or answer a question, tidy away a question already answered, or add your Chief of Staff's notes): while open and not yet left for you, they show as "Being recorded by the automatic job", not counted, so the desk can read "Quiet". If the job leaves one for you, for example because a check failed, it counts again at once, as it would notify. The desk then reads each such pull request's checks and the job's latest runs, about 5 to 8 more GitHub reads per pull request, a few a day, within its limits. This changes your 17 October answer for those pull requests only.
   RECOMMENDATION: B — everything the desk shows as waiting on you is then something to do, as your notifications already are.
   RISK CLASS: R0   REVERSIBILITY: reversible   BLAST RADIUS: how the automatic job's open pull requests count on the desk; what notifies does not change
   DEFAULT: A   TIMEOUT: 48

   ## Context
   - The owner reported following a stale answer link on a question already answered in the session (source: docs/design/references/owner-report-2026-10-18-stale-answer-link.md)
   - The seven questions, filed in four pull requests, sat on main for about 4, 54, 6 and 3 minutes, filing by filing, between the filing and the answer being merged (source: docs/design/references/owner-report-2026-10-18-stale-answer-link.md)
   - The owner chose to keep filing every ask, and to close the gap between an answer in the session and its record landing (source: decisions/questions/P-001-file-only-required.md)
   - While open, the automatic job's pull requests show as waiting on the owner (source: decisions/questions/P-001-asks-merge-on-desk.md)
   - A pull request the automatic job will merge sends no notification (source: decisions/questions/P-001-notify-job-merges.md)
   - A question marked as asked in a session, or whose answer's pull request is open, shows with no answer link (source: specs/S-001.md, AC77)
   ```

   (DEFAULT is A, not the recommendation, because B changes an answer the owner gave on 17
   October, so it should be the owner's pick, never a timeout's.)

**Ruled, to be built in M3:** phone notifications (AC45). *Brought forward 2026-10-18: specified in
AC73 to AC77 and built first; the note below is kept as written.* On 8 October you chose push
notifications to the desk installed on your iPhone's Home Screen, sent by the Orchestrator's
GitHub run whenever it raises a card, with no database and no new Cloudflare service
(decisions/questions/P-001-notifications.md). This spec does not yet hold its criteria: they are
added to M3 before M3 starts, with the one experiment the ruling asks for. Until then you still
get GitHub's notification when a Service-Desk card waits on you (the Orchestrator's issue).

  The preview is always `https://service-desk-preview.pages.dev`.

Return: the six-part contract.
